# MAWS charter

> **Parent:** root, the project's standing purpose (this document is the root spec; `CLAUDE.md` points here). **Level name:** P0, the first release. Children dispatch per their grain hint in §12, each carrying its section as its spec; each child's artifact (execution document, ledger, findings) opens by citing this document, its child id, and the parent pin (the git blob hash of this file at dispatch).

MAWS is a codename. This document is the chartering design for the project: what it is for, what was decided and why, what ships first, and how the pieces fit. It is the root spec: the approved design up top and, from §10 on, the decomposition into children. Research behind every claim lives in `research/`.

Status: approved by the owner on 2026-09-28 after one critique round and the protocol probe; routed to decomposition into milestone specs. Decisions marked **D-n** were made with the project owner in the brainstorming rounds; decisions marked **S-n** are technical calls made by the design and can be overridden.

## 1. Purpose

**Higher purpose.** A general, state-of-the-art desktop workstation for supervising AI coding agents, built on the Claude Code engine. It replaces the Claude Code terminal UI, Claude Desktop's Code surface, and the Codex desktop app for its owner and a few colleagues. It is general at the base; the owner's own workflows (doperpowers, ptc, workflows, teams, orchestration ideas) arrive later as plugins on top of a strong base, not as hardcoded features. (D-5)

**What it is for.** Two scenes carry equal weight from the first release. (D-6)

- Scene A, supervision: several projects, many live sessions, approvals and questions pending, workflows running with many agents. Open MAWS and know within seconds where you are needed.
- Scene B, depth: one session with the diff panel open, a file tab beside the transcript, a terminal in the session's worktree, the browser showing the dev server the agent started.

**Benchmarks.** Codex desktop sets the interaction bar; it is the app generally praised for UX. Claude Desktop matters as Anthropic's own integration of the same engine we use, so it tells us how the engine is wired, what the app must own around it, and which behaviours the engine already expects from a host. (D-14)

**What it must not be.** Not "VS Code with a chat sidebar" and not "a chat app with a file tree". Not a thin client: the wire does not carry everything, and the app owns a real state layer around the engine. Not a fork of Claude Code: the engine binary stays unmodified. Nothing in either benchmark app actively annoys the owner; the terminal power features the GUIs drop (queued messages, `!` bash mode, keybindings, slash palette) are enhancements to add, not faults to fix. (D-13)

## 2. Decision log

| # | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Engine integration is a strict SDK-wire client plus an on-disk sidecar. No bundle patches. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W13): the engine is the user's installed Claude Code, resolved at every launch (binaryPath, else `~/.local/bin/claude`, else the login shell's PATH; realpath), never refused for its version and never patched; the 2.1.283 pin applies to the fixtures, the conformance and live harnesses and the probe runner, not to the product; custody and `pinnedVersion` are retired. | The raw 2.1.283 control protocol exposes 85 control request types including structured reads for status, settings, hooks, permission rules, memory, skills, sandbox, workspace diff, export, usage and context usage, rename and title generation, fork and rewind, and a runtime workflow-progress field. The remaining gaps are narrow (per-message held-peer approval, native workflow pause/skip/retry, full auto-mode classifier traces, exact statusLine ledger). Patching a Bun standalone binary is not a dependable source-only edit; Claude Code ships roughly weekly. See `research/sdk-wire-gap.md` §3. |
| D-2 | Left panel is a collapsible project tree with sessions inside, plus a pinned "Needs you" section on top listing every session blocked on the human across all projects. Superseded 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W5): the sidebar is a two-level drill-in, the project list then one project's sessions grouped Main first and then each worktree in git order, with no Needs-you or Pinned sections; cross-project attention stays on the Overview, the notifier, the dock badge and ⌘⇧J, which read the attention table directly, so nothing is lost but the sidebar's own list. Codex's flat tree is overridden because the owner has 276 projects and 3,279 sessions. Superseded again 2026-10-01 (owner's second look): the sidebar is Codex's flat tree, project rows expanding in place to five recent sessions, Show more and a Resume… picker; the drill-in and its slide are removed. The 276-project argument is answered by search, recency order, the per-project caps and the picker. | Cross-project attention is the point of Scene A; drill-down hides it. Codex's own sidebar already offers "Priority: needs input and unread first". |
| D-3 | Real engine from day one. A replay mode plays any Claude Code JSONL transcript into the UI for design iteration and tests. Static HTML mockups only for taste-heavy surfaces. | Fake data produces generic UX that the real wire then breaks. |
| D-4 | Visual identity: Codex-density chrome (dense lists and panels) with Claude-style transcript typography (generous measure and line height for reading long prose), in Claude's bright, light-first style with a dark theme as the alternate. Own token set, structured like Claude's CDS. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W2): the transcript takes Codex's measured scale instead of Claude's reading comfort: 14px prose at 1.625 line height, 12px code, 13px chrome, Codex's heading ratios, in a 768px column with Claude Desktop's 768/960/1280 width setting. | Supervising many things wants density; reading agent prose wants comfort; the owner prefers Claude's bright look over Codex's dark one (mockups were reviewed dark and judged excellent in structure). |
| D-5 | Daily driver with a general base; owner-specific workflow as later plugins. | See purpose. |
| D-6 | Both scenes equal from day one. | See purpose. |
| D-7 | Claude-native engine with one adapter boundary. Only the Claude Code adapter is built. GPT models arrive through the owner's model gateway (ClaudeUsage's loopback relay: base URL, auth marker, gateway model discovery). The Codex agent is reachable only through bash. | Keeps workflows, skills, plugins and teams first-class while leaving a second engine possible without touching the UI. |
| D-8 | Main area is a browser-like tab strip with a single pane by default and one optional side-by-side split (drag a tab to the right edge). | Two panes cover the IDE scene; N-way editor groups add layout state and hurt keyboard navigation. |
| D-9 | No extension surface in P0. The whole extension model is next-phase work: a plugin API for side-panel tabs and commands mirroring Claude Code's own plugin host shape ranks above app-specific tools; app-specific tools (batch question sheet, agent-opened panels, custom transcript block renderers, browser control, orchestration) follow. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, mods): MAWS becomes a display client of the engine's `ui_surface_v1` remote-surface protocol, in slices: R1 now (decode the engine's `ui_toast`, `ui_status`, `ui_log` pushes and init's plugin errors), R2 next (attach as the "desktop" surface: panes, the band above the prompt, the footer slot, presses and inputs, prompt access), R3 later (transcript sites). No MAWS-specific plugin API; the extension surface is the engine's own. | A strong base app comes first. Consequence: P0 renders AskUserQuestion with its existing schema; the richer question sheet and non-blocking questions wait. |
| D-10 | Right panel state belongs to the session, with any panel tab pinnable to stay across sessions. | Matches Codex's per-thread panels; the pin covers the "one terminal I always want" case. |
| D-11 | macOS arm64 only for the research preview. | Owner's platform; Claude Desktop is macOS-first. |
| D-12 | Claude Code's on-disk sessions are the store. MAWS keeps only an index. Any session started in the terminal opens in MAWS and the reverse. | Transcript, subagent transcripts, tool results, tasks and workflow journals are already durable under `~/.claude`; duplicating them would break terminal interop. A session that is live in another process (a terminal, a `--bg` job) is shown read-only as "foreign-live" (§5.1) rather than resumed into a second writer. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W7): a foreign-live pane offers "Take over here": after a confirm that names the holder, MAWS runs `claude stop` for a `--bg` job or signals the interactive holder's pid (the one it already verifies), waits for the registry to clear, then resumes the same id with its grants re-applied; a take-over mid-turn loses that turn, and the confirm says so. True attach to a running interactive process is impossible (its socket is peer messaging only). |
| D-13 | Terminal power features are P0 enhancements: queued messages, steer, `!` bash mode, `#` memory, prompt history, slash palette, keybindings. | Owner's stated wish list. |
| D-14 | Benchmark weighting: Codex for interaction, Claude Desktop for engine wiring. | Owner's note. |
| D-15 | `CC-to-SDK` and its packages are not references and are not reused. | Owner's call: below the quality bar. |
| D-16 | Sessions do not survive an app restart or crash in P0; the engine host runs in a utility process and quitting with turns in flight asks first. A detachable supervisor is P1. | Same trade-off Codex and Claude Desktop make; resume from disk covers the rest. |
| D-17 | Account-wide usage windows stay in every session's status line, backed by one shared ClaudeUsage read. Superseded 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W2 and W6): the status line is removed entirely; usage is one control, a pill in MAWS's top bar above the tabs that opens a copy of ClaudeUsage's panel; per-session context is a ring in the composer toolbar; cost lives in Session info. | Owner's stated design; the cost objection is moot with a single source. |
| D-18 | Auto permission mode is the default for new sessions (inherited from the owner's settings), and few permission prompts are expected. Needs-you is designed around questions, plan reviews, classifier escalations and errors, not a stream of approvals. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W3): auto is MAWS's own default when the user's settings name no mode; a refused auto falls back to a user-set mode, acceptEdits by default, set explicitly and announced, because most users run auto or bypass and rarely acceptEdits or manual. | Owner's note; matches how they run the terminal. |
| D-19 | Inference routes through the owner's gateway by default: every engine launch carries `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8641`, the non-secret marker `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=claudeusage-local-gateway` and `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=true`, exactly as ClaudeUsage's own toggle writes them. Accounts (one or more Claude logins, kept in ClaudeUsage's Keychain) are added, reconnected and removed in ClaudeUsage; MAWS shows them and hands off for sign-in. Direct-to-Anthropic is an explicit fallback, taken only when the relay is unreachable at launch or chosen per session, and is the only path where the engine's own login matters. | Owner's stated design: "default the app's inference to gateway, just like how we're using right now". The relay substitutes a real account token per request and chooses the account whose weekly quota resets soonest, so the engine never needs a login of its own. Duplicating the sign-in flow would be a child of its own; ClaudeUsage already owns the credential. |
| D-20 | Basic UX starts from the reference apps. Where the charter or the owner's idea notes in the vault do not say otherwise, a surface is built the way Claude Desktop or Codex desktop builds it (Claude Desktop first for the session, composer and empty states; Codex for density, supervision lists and panels), and MAWS invents only what the notes ask for. | Owner's ruling of 2026-09-28 after the mockup 05 review: "in most cases just following Claude Desktop or Codex App is the best way to begin with unless my idea notes override it". Original layouts cost review cycles and drift from taste the owner already approved by using those apps daily. |

## 3. Scope

### P0, the first release

- Shell: three regions (sidebar, main, right panel), persisted sizes, accessible resizers, native macOS window chrome with hidden inset title bar.
- Sidebar: Needs-you section, pinned sessions, project tree with sessions, "Other sessions" for sessions without a project, project "+" opens an unstarted session tab in that project (location, worktree and branch, model and effort, permission mode and directories are chosen on the composer's strip before the first message; the engine launches on first send; owner's decision 2026-09-28, §19), status glyphs, unread state, hover card, priority and recency sort, search. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W5): no Needs-you or Pinned section; a project list (name, waiting ◐N and working ●N counts, "+" on hover, search) that drills into one project's sessions grouped Main and worktrees, with "‹ Projects" back; pin is pin-to-top within the project.
- Sessions: start with cwd and additional directories chosen on the strip of an unstarted session tab, workspace trust (a docked trust card at first send for directories never trusted, attested to the engine at initialize so project settings, hooks, MCP servers and plugins load exactly as in the terminal), resume, fork (same directory, or a new worktree: launched from the new worktree with the fork flag, the engine resolves the source session across project directories and writes the fork under the worktree's own slug with a new id, no transcript copy; rollback removes the worktree and the record; amended 2026-09-28 on C10's probe, §19), rename with title provenance, archive, safe worktree cleanup. Sessions live in another process appear as foreign-live: transcript tailed read-only, holder shown, composer and approvals disabled, "attach in a terminal tab" for background jobs.
- Overview: a main-area board, the default tab, one card per live or unread session grouped by project with Needs-you first: current activity, last assistant line, the pending request inline and answerable when the card is focused, context percentage, cost since last seen, child-agent count, branch and dirty state. This is Scene A's primary surface; the sidebar section is its index.
- Quit and reload safety: the engine adapter and process table live in a utility process so renderer reloads never touch sessions; quitting while sessions are mid-turn, running background tasks or workflows, or while a terminal runs a command asks first, naming each, and offers to quit when idle (aligned 2026-09-29 with §4.4 by C12 decision D8, §19). Sessions do not outlive the app in P0.
- Main tabs: session tabs, file viewer tabs, diff tabs; single pane default, optional split; drag session rows to the tab strip.
- Transcript: streaming markdown with KaTeX, Mermaid and highlighted code; answer-first turns with a collapsible work disclosure; semantic activity groups → cells → full detail; change receipt per turn; subagent lifecycle rows; thinking disclosure; plan cards; questions; errors and reconnects; virtualised with stable scroll anchoring.
- Approvals: request docks at the composer locus and appears in Needs-you; keyboard approve/deny; scoped grants mapped to the engine's permission responses; abort and redelivery handling.
- Composer: rich editor; context strip (project, location, branch, model and effort, permission mode, add-dir); "+" menu (files and photos, slash commands, connectors, plugins); typed `@` context (files, agents, skills); slash palette; queue, steer, stop, resume semantics; queued message list; `!` bash mode; `#` memory; prompt history; drag-and-drop and paste of files and images.
- Status line under the composer: cwd, context usage, usage windows (5h, 7d), cost, permission mode; each opens a popover.
- Right panel, per session with pin: resting pinned summary (branch and diff stats, push state, subagents, background work, sources); tools: Subagents (Active/Done list, drill-in transcript, a message relayed through the parent), Review (diff of the session's changes under Codex's scopes with file navigation), Terminal in the session's cwd, Files (tree, opens read-only viewer tabs), Background work (shells, monitors, tasks, workflows with phases and agents), Session info (context, usage, model, cost).
- Slash palette widgets translating the terminal's menu commands: model, effort, fast, permissions, usage, config, plugins, mcp, sandbox, hooks, memory, skills, agents, status, compact, context, rename, resume, export, diff.
- Notifications: OS notification when a session enters Needs-you while the window is unfocused; a global "next needs-you" shortcut. Ships with the first shell milestone, not as polish.
- Replay mode: play a JSONL transcript into the UI at adjustable speed.
- Model gateway (D-19): routed by default through ClaudeUsage's relay; relay health checked at launch (`GET /api/hello` on the loopback port answers 200); when the relay is down the launch sheet offers to open ClaudeUsage or to run direct with the engine's own login; a per-session "Direct" option for debugging. Gateway models in the model picker from `list_models`. Accounts view: the relay's accounts (email, plan, tier, needs-reauthentication, which one is routing now) read from ClaudeUsage's `accounts.json`, with add, reconnect and remove handed off to ClaudeUsage's panel.
- Usage windows: the status line and its popover read the active account's 5h and 7d windows and reset times from ClaudeUsage's local data, since the engine's usage read returns no windows through the gateway; session cost and context come from the engine. Amended 2026-10-01 (W2, W6): no status line; the windows live in the top-bar usage pill's popover (W6), context in the composer's ring, cost in Session info.

### P1

- File editor tab (CodeMirror 6) and editable merge view.
- Source control panel (stage, discard, commit, push, PR from a session's worktree); the summary's pull-request status and checks arrive here (owner, 2026-09-28).
- MAWS-integrated SendUserFile: MAWS's own path for an agent to deliver a file into the right panel, in place of the engine tool that is disabled under the SDK entrypoint; the sandboxed PDF and HTML frame in the file viewer (owner, 2026-09-28).
- Bottom dock for the auxiliary panel host (terminal drawer).
- Browser panel as a separate WebContentsView with target-restricted automation and human takeover.
- Side chat (`/btw`), Tasks panel (TaskCreate lists), cross-session messaging and teams, scheduled loops.
- All-accounts usage view from ClaudeUsage's ledger (P0 shows the active account only).
- Session replay scrubber over the JSONL (time travel).
- Detachable session supervisor over a Unix socket so sessions outlive app restarts and updates; triggered when update restarts or crashes start costing real turns.
- Prewarmed spare session (`--await-claim` and `claim_session`) for instant "+" starts.
- Native rendering of the owner's to-human output style as a "what Claude told me" lane (arrives with the renderer extension API).
- Dictation.

### P2

The deferred artifact work: docs, slides, design artifacts, comments, co-browsing (see the vault note "Claude Docs study").

### Next phase, logged, not designed

Extension model (D-9): plugin API for side-panel tabs and commands; app-specific tools exposed to the agent through an in-process MCP server (batch question sheet with files, design systems, repos and "decide for me"; async questions; open a panel; show a file; browser control); custom transcript block renderers; orchestration-driven multi-agent views.

## 4. Architecture

### 4.1 Process model

- **Renderer** (sandboxed, context-isolated): React 19 with the React Compiler, Tailwind v4, Radix primitives. Talks to the main process through a small validated command/event IPC schema. Never receives the shell, git or engine bridge directly.
- **Main process** (privileged): owns windows, layout persistence, notifications, the session index, the git service, and the IPC boundary.
- **Utility processes**: the engine host (engine adapter, process table and filesystem state service, so a renderer reload or window crash never touches a session), the PTY host (node-pty), and a heavy-work worker for transcript indexing and search.
- **Engine child processes**: one `claude` process per live session. Launch line: `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-prompt-tool stdio --include-partial-messages --forward-subagent-text --replay-user-messages --include-hook-events --setting-sources user,project,local`, plus cwd, additional directories, model, effort, permission mode (auto by default, per D-18, inherited from settings), and resume or fork flags. The binary is an absolute pinned path, never resolved through PATH. The environment is constructed, not inherited: at startup the app captures the login shell's environment once (as Claude Desktop's shell-path worker does) and passes an allowlist (PATH, HOME, locale, `CLAUDE_*`, `ANTHROPIC_*` and the tool paths the engine needs), because a Dock-launched app has none of the shell's exports. The allowlist excludes the variables a parent Claude session injects into its children (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_PID`, the messaging socket and token, `CLAUDE_CODE_USER_DIALOG_TIMEOUT_MS`, and, added by C9's spike on 2026-09-28, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDE_CODE_EXECPATH`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ATTENDED`), which the second probe pass had to scrub to get a clean child and which a terminal-started `claude` otherwise reports as an inherited child-session marker. It adds `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=true` so fork-type subagents work as in the terminal, and by default (D-19) the gateway variables `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, the marker `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` and `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=true` (Claude Code turns MCP tool search off behind a base URL it does not consider first-party), after a relay health check; a direct launch is the exception, chosen per session or offered when the relay is unreachable, and changing it restarts the process. A direct launch cannot rely on unsetting them: the owner's `settings.json` `env` block routes the session regardless of the process environment, so the direct launch also passes `--settings` with empty-string `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (verified in pass 2). `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` is a per-session launch choice shown in Session info, because it silently decides which model workflow and subagent workers run on. It passes the owner's `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_*_MODEL` overrides through when present, since they are part of the routing setup; Session info shows which overrides are in effect. The host's `initialize` sets `perTaskStopAffordance: true` (otherwise Stop kills every background agent and workflow), `supportedDialogKinds`, `workspaceTrust` attestation, and dialog expiry "never" through flag settings so unanswered dialogs and elicitations wait for the human. Version-pinned; the app records the engine version per session. (S-1)

### 4.2 Engine adapter

The adapter owns the raw NDJSON transport rather than wrapping the public SDK's `query()`. Reason: the public 0.3.259 `Query` type lacks wrappers for most 2.1.283 control requests, and two writers on the same stdin is a race. The adapter implements: initialize handshake with hook registration; request ids, cancellation and timeouts; `can_use_tool`, `hook_callback`, `elicitation`, `request_user_dialog` and AskUserQuestion round trips; outgoing control requests (interrupt, set_permission_mode, set_model, effort, get_usage, get_context_usage, get_settings, list_permission_rules, get_hooks_listing, get_memory_dialog, get_skills_dialog, get_sandbox_dialog, get_workspace_diff, export_conversation, rewind_conversation, fork_conversation, rename_session, generate_session_title, stop_task, reload_plugins, side_question); stdin frames (`user`, `bash_command`, `keep_alive`); unknown-field retention and unknown-frame journaling. (S-2)

Events are normalised into a graph keyed separately by session id, message uuid, API message id, tool-use id, task id and agent id. Child-agent traffic is attributed by `parent_tool_use_id` and never flattened into the parent conversation. Raw frames are journaled per session for replay and for compatibility diagnosis. (S-3)

Verified against the real binary by the protocol probe (`spikes/protocol-probe/`, fixtures under `fixtures/`), which corrects the spec-derived picture in these places:

- No host handshake is required; the first `user` frame starts the session and `system/init` arrives per turn, carrying the command, skill, agent and model lists the palette needs (`get_skills_dialog` returned empty, so the palette reads `init`).
- AskUserQuestion reaches the host as a `can_use_tool` request flagged `requires_user_interaction`; the answer is an allow with `updatedInput.answers` keyed by question text. There is no separate question request type to implement.
- A denied tool produces an error `tool_result` and `result.permission_denials`; no `system/permission_denied` frame appears on this path. `permission_suggestions` can be absent. `updatedPermissions` rules with session destination take effect immediately.
- Child agents deliver their assistant and user records with `parent_tool_use_id`, per message, with no token-level `stream_event` for children. Subagent panels therefore update per message, and their full transcripts are the `agent-*.jsonl` files on disk. Both children in the probe ran in the background even when only one asked to.
- `--resume` restores the session but never replays history over the wire; the transcript view is rebuilt from the JSONL by the filesystem service. `fork_conversation` answered "unsupported" in a routed session; forking is the `--fork-session` launch flag.
- `bash_command` output comes back as replayed `user` records tagged as bash input, stdout, stderr and exit code, bracketed by command-lifecycle frames; the replayed-row renderer keys on those tags, and the app-owned queue does not count that lifecycle as a released user message.
- Interrupt with `cancel_queued` cancels queued messages explicitly; the running turn ends as `error_during_execution` with terminal reason `aborted_streaming`, and the next turn resumes. `bash_command` yields replayed user frames tagged as bash input, stdout, stderr and exit code.
- All thirteen structured reads succeed. `get_usage` returned session accounting but no rate-limit windows, so the status line takes its 5h and 7d windows from ClaudeUsage, with the engine's windows as the fallback for unrouted sessions.
- The first pass ran routed through the gateway with empty settings sources. The second pass (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md`, fixtures under `fixtures/pass2/`) repeated the usage, models and fork reads unrouted and ran everything else under the owner's real settings. `fork_conversation` is unsupported unrouted too. `list_models` carries `supportsAutoMode` and `supportsFastMode` per model; Haiku cannot hold auto mode and silently runs in `default`. Unrouted model turns are still unverified because the owner's login had expired; the rerun is a tracked residual.
- Memory under the owner's settings: one idle session is 270 to 420 MB for the whole process tree (stdio MCP servers are about 40 percent), about 550 MB at peak during a tool call; three idle sessions each settle near 270 MB. A parked prewarmed spare is 255 MB, `claim_session` takes under 100 ms, and a cold initialize takes 0.8 to 1.1 s with the owner's settings.
- Auto mode through the gateway: the effective mode is `auto` in the initialize response and every `system/init`; `get_status` reports the server classifier lane enabled with no refusal, dialog or warning. Allow verdicts are visible after all: each `stream_event` `message_delta.safeguard_results` entry carries `evaluated/not_flagged` keyed by tool-use id, while allows produce no control frames. Only ask rules escalated (`can_use_tool` with `decision_reason_type: "rule"`); no classifier denial was provoked, so the `automode-*` denial kinds remain unobserved. A GPT-routed main takes the local classifier lane instead (Sonnet 5 requests through the gateway, counted as usage; verified by a peer probe, recorded in the Decision Log).
- Workflows: `task_started` (`local_workflow`), an `async_launched` tool result, `task_progress.workflow_progress` snapshots with phase and per-agent entries, `task_updated`, `task_notification`, and then an engine-initiated follow-up turn that produces a `result` the host never asked for. Background completions do the same. The adapter accepts unsolicited turns and never releases a queued message into one.
- Rewind: `rewind_conversation` needs `last_seen_user_message_uuid` or answers `stale_target`; success returns `prefillText` and emits no reset frame, so the host truncates its own view and pre-fills the composer. The JSONL is never truncated: the engine appends a `last-prompt` anchor with `rewound: true` and the next turn branches from the older leaf. `rewind_files` is disabled without file checkpointing.
- Hooks: host callback hooks round-trip through `hook_callback` with no deadline and `additionalContext` accepted, but emit no `hook_started`/`hook_response` frames and are absent from `get_hooks_listing`; only settings and plugin hooks do. MCP elicitation arrives as `elicitation` with `mode: "form"` and the accepted content reaches the server. No producer of `request_user_dialog` was reachable. Dialog expiry set through `--settings` is effective.
- `perTaskStopAffordance`: without it an interrupt kills a running background workflow; with it the workflow survives. Background Bash survives either way. The interrupted foreground call is tagged `user-rejected`, not `interrupted`, so the pairing of `user-rejected` with an aborted terminal reason renders as an interruption.
- Trust: unattested project rules are ignored with a stderr notice; `set_cwd` into an untrusted directory returns `needs_trust` and succeeds on retry with trust accepted; `workspaceTrust` in `initialize` records trust and loads the rules at once; trust persists in `~/.claude.json`.
- Grants: a session grant is lost on `--resume` and `apply_flag_settings` restores it with no re-prompt. A matching ask rule outranks any allow rule, so a prompt raised by an ask rule offers no "always allow" scope. A host deny is reported as `permission-rule`.
- Resume while live: against a print-mode holder there is no engine guard, and both processes write sibling branches into one JSONL; against a `claude --bg` holder the resume is refused with an attach or fork message. MAWS detects holders itself and opens them read-only. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's report and ruling, W7): MAWS's own `-p` process registers as an interactive `sdk-ts` holder, so a terminal `claude --resume` of a session MAWS holds is not refused either, and the two write sibling branches. The owner's collision: nothing died; what was written in MAWS vanished while the terminal held the session and returned when it closed, the chain follower flipping to the terminal's branch and back. Policy: MAWS tells its own registry record from a foreign one (entrypoint and the process table's pid) and shows a contested state on a session it holds when a foreign holder joins; while the holder is live the view keeps MAWS's own branch pinned instead of flipping, and a live MAWS process yields with a notice; after the holder exits the view re-folds; the remembered foreign holder is re-applied when MAWS's process exits, and the pane tails disk after an exit instead of the dead stream.
- `bash_command` alone never reaches the model's context; the following non-querying `user` echo does, and that echo yields an empty `result` with a null terminal reason which the adapter treats as a receipt, not a turn.

Further engine semantics the design commits to:

- **Queue ownership.** MAWS owns the message queue: queued messages persist in the index and one is released into the engine when a turn ends; Steer bypasses the queue: on this engine `priority: 'next'` folds the message into the running turn at the next tool boundary and `priority: 'now'` preempts the running turn (ended as `aborted_streaming`) and runs the message as its own turn (measured 2026-09-28, `pass3/steer-next.jsonl` and `steer-now`; §19 for which one ⌥Enter sends). The engine's own in-memory queue is not used for user drafts, so the queue survives process exit. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W1): Enter while a turn runs sends the message as a `next` steer, so the engine delivers it at the next tool boundary as the terminal does; ⌥Enter places it in MAWS's queue, released when the turn ends; Esc interrupts the turn and sends the queued head now (`priority: 'now'`), or is a plain stop when nothing is queued; ⌘. stays a plain stop that pauses the queue. A queued or steered message shows at once in the transcript as a dimmed tail bubble with its caption, and a delivered steer stays at its delivery position, visible when the turn collapses. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W10): the composer offers one action: Enter during a turn is the `next` steer, Esc interrupts and sends the queued head now (a plain stop when nothing waits), ⌘. and the Stop button stop and pause; there is no hold option, no ⌥Enter and no send menu. MAWS's queue remains the holding place for steers a stop withdraws and for the rows' own actions.
- **Plan approval** has three outcomes: implement (allow, which restores the pre-plan permission mode), clear context and implement (deny, set the mode, start a fresh turn seeded with the plan), keep planning (deny with feedback). A conversation reset renders as a transcript boundary row.
- **`!` bash mode** sends the `bash_command` frame and then a non-querying `user` frame carrying the command and its output so the result lands in the model's context as it does in the terminal (verified: without the echo the model never sees the output). **`#` memory** has no wire mechanism; it is an app-side append to the memory file chosen from `get_memory_dialog`.
- **Revisable log.** Retractions (`supersedes`, retracted message uuids), refusal fallbacks, `compact_boundary` and compacting status are events the renderer applies, not ignores; the transcript is not append-only.
- **Edit and resubmit** forks by default; rewind is an explicit action. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W3): edit-and-resubmit rewinds this session to that message and resends by default (the terminal's Esc Esc semantics over `rewind_conversation` with the seen cursor); fork is only an explicit action, a Fork button in the in-place editor or `/branch`, because forking changes the session id, duplicates sessions and strands background work (subagents, shells).

### 4.3 Filesystem state service

One watcher plus append-aware JSONL tailers over the engine's own store (respecting `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`):

- `projects/<slug>/<session>.jsonl` main transcripts, titles, compaction and rewind anchors; used for resume backfill, replay and terminal-started sessions.
- `projects/<slug>/<session>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl` and `.meta.json` for child transcripts and lineage.
- `tool-results/` for spilled large results, lazily loaded.
- `tasks/`, `workflows/` snapshots and journals, `sessions/<pid>.json` for live peers, `jobs/` for detached sessions, `teams/`.
- Settings, agents, skills, plugins and commands files for the palette and the management widgets.

The service never writes into engine-owned locks, mailboxes or journals. The main JSONL is append-only even across rewind: the engine appends a `last-prompt` anchor with `rewound: true` and the next turn branches from an older leaf, so the current transcript is the parent chain from the latest leaf, not the file in order. The tailer follows that chain and re-anchors by uuid rather than byte offset, which also covers a truncating writer if one ever appears. Whole-file sidecars (`.meta.json`, `custom-title.json`, workflow snapshots) are parsed with retry because their write atomicity is unknown. Spilled results are loaded by the path the engine reports, never by a derived name, because background shell output and regular spills use different keys. The project slug is derived from the realpath of the directory, not the path as passed, so the service maps a session id to its files by discovery (`projects/*/<session>.jsonl`) or by realpath-then-slug, never from a cwd string, and reads the session's directory from the first JSONL record. Live holders are read from `sessions/<pid>.json`.

### 4.4 Session service and index

SQLite (Node's built-in `node:sqlite`, which Electron 44 exposes with FTS5; better-sqlite3 ships no prebuild for its ABI) holds only what the engine does not: project list and membership, app session records (app id ↔ engine session id, title and its provenance, worktree, pin, archive state, last-seen cursor for unread), attention items, drafts, replay fixtures. Two things the cut placed here live elsewhere (amended 2026-09-28 on C4's parent-impact): queued messages and grant snapshots live in the engine host's per-session state file under `userData`, because the engine host releases the queue at turn end and cannot reach main's database; tab and panel layout lives in main's `layout.json`. Both survive process exit, which is what the placement was for. (S-4)

Session process policy is memory-budgeted, never a fixed timer. A process stays live while it has a running turn, background tasks or workflows, a pending request, queued messages, or a held peer message (amended 2026-09-29 by the owner, §19: session grants are not a keep-alive reason, since the snapshot re-applies on resume, and connected MCP servers keep a session alive only when the owner lists that server as stateful in settings; the earlier list would never have released a process on the owner's machine, where every session connects three MCP servers). Idle processes without those are candidates for exit only under budget pressure, least recently used first; the budget defaults to 25% of physical memory (8 GB on the owner's Mac, about nineteen idle sessions at 420 MB). A released session keeps its row and card idle with a quiet released mark, no notification and no Needs-you; Session info says when and why; typing and Send resume it transparently. The budget counts the whole process tree under the engine pid (stdio MCP servers included) and is visible in Session info; measured under the owner's settings, an idle session is 270 to 420 MB and a tool call peaks near 550 MB. When a process with session grants must exit, the app snapshots the grants it issued (it sees every `updatedPermissions` it sent) and re-applies them on resume through `apply_flag_settings` (verified: the restored grant is not re-prompted). Exit never silently discards background work: the user is asked. Until the budget policy ships (C12), engine processes never exit on their own: only archive, quit, or an explicit stop ends one. (S-5) Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W7): the budget is a setting, `engine.memoryBudget.enabled`, on by default; off restores the rule above (engine processes never exit on their own) while totals stay visible in Session info and the banner, sheets and Settings rows hide.

### 4.5 Git and worktree service

Read-only status, branch, diff and log through the `git` binary, with a repository watcher for the toolbar and pinned summary. Worktrees are ordinary trusted `git worktree add` under `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/`, the same convention the engine's own `EnterWorktree` uses, so both coexist. Cleanup distinguishes archive, remove a clean checkout, discard uncommitted changes and delete a branch. (S-6)

### 4.6 Permission broker

A request router with one pending table for tool permissions, questions, plan approvals, dialogs and elicitations. Each item carries request id, session, agent, tool, input preview, engine suggestions and the scope options the engine accepts. Items move through pending, delivered, and confirmed or retired. "Delivered" is not an alarm state: a long command keeps an item delivered for minutes with nothing wrong. There is no acknowledgement frame for a `control_response`: delivery truth is a successful write to a live process; the only failure signals are a stdin write error and process exit; confirmation is the earliest frame carrying the item's tool-use id (tool result, tool progress, hook frame, denial record). If the process exits with delivered or pending items, the broker raises one Needs-you entry at exit time from its own table ("session interrupted, N approvals lost"); on resume the engine retires the dangling tool calls itself and will not ask again, so nothing is re-pended. Abort from the engine removes an item. A prompt raised by an ask rule (`decision_reason_type: "rule"`) offers no "always allow" scope, because an ask rule outranks any allow rule. An interrupt reports the aborted call as `user-rejected`; the broker pairs that with the turn's aborted terminal reason and records an interruption, not a refusal. Items are the source of the Needs-you section. (S-7)

### 4.7 Libraries

Streamdown for streaming markdown with its KaTeX, Mermaid and Shiki plugins; TanStack Virtual with end anchoring for the transcript; `@git-diff-view/react` for review; CodeMirror 6 for file tabs (P1 editing); Tiptap for the composer; terminal renderer behind an adapter, ghostty-web first with xterm.js and its WebGL addon as the fallback, decided by the spike in §7; electron-vite, electron-builder and electron-updater; Zustand stores fed by IPC events, TanStack Query for request-response data. (S-8)

## 5. Information architecture

### 5.1 Sidebar

Top to bottom: search; **Needs you · N** (every session blocked on the human, across projects, ordered by wait time, each row showing project, title, the reason: approval, question, plan review, error, interrupted; hover card previews the request with its full input and offers open, and approve or deny by explicit click only for read-only tools; keyboard approval happens only in the focused session pane or focused Overview card); **Pinned**; **Projects** as collapsible groups, each with a status roll-up and a "+"; sessions inside with status glyph (working, needs you, idle unread, idle read, done, failed, foreign-live), title, secondary line (current activity while working, last assistant line when idle), age, unread dot; **Other sessions** for sessions without a project. Project actions: new session, mark all read, archive sessions, remove project, reveal in Finder (a project-level "add directory" was retired 2026-09-28, §19: a project is one directory and a session's additional directories are the composer's chip, §5.4). Session actions: open, open in split, pin, rename, fork, archive, delete conversation, remove worktree. Sort: priority (needs input and unread first) or recency. Sessions found on disk that MAWS did not start appear under their project automatically. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W5): the sidebar is two levels. Level one is the project list: search, then each project as one row with its name, a waiting count (◐N) and a working count (●N), a "+" on hover, and "Other sessions" as a pseudo-project; the list is sorted by recency of activity. Clicking a project slides level two in from the right: a "‹ Projects" control, the project's name with its "+", then its sessions grouped Main first and then each worktree in `git worktree list` order (group label: the folder name with the branch beside it, each group with its own "+"; sibling worktrees made outside `.claude/worktrees` fold under their main project by git common dir, an index change), rows of 30px (status glyph, title, age, unread dot inline; no secondary line) with the 300ms hover card showing branch and worktree, the last assistant line, the pending request and age; a page scrolls, so there is no 20-row cap. There is no Needs-you section and no Pinned section; pin keeps a session at the top of its project; the open project is remembered across launches. Session and project actions are unchanged. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's request, New chat): a "New chat" control sits at the top of level one, above the project list, and at the top of level two (the Codex app's placement); it opens a general unstarted tab whose project is chosen in the composer; the project rows' and worktree groups' "+" keep their preselected project or worktree. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's second look, W11): the two-level drill-in is removed. Under the New chat row and the search, one list of project rows (36px: folder, name, ◐N ●N; "+" and an archive icon on hover; a 300 ms hover card with name, path, counts, last activity and worktrees) sorted by recency, each expanding in place (W8's motion, no slide; expanded state persisted, collapsed by default) to its five most recent sessions in one flat list (30px rows, worktree sessions marked by their branch), then "Show more" to eight at most; five project rows then "Show more" for the rest; a search button on the project row ("Resume a session…") opens a picker modelled on `claude --resume` (search; title over age · branch; all-projects, current-branch and all-worktrees toggles; Enter opens, ⌘Enter in a new tab). The archive icon runs "Remove from sidebar" (the project is hidden in MAWS's index, its sessions with it, restorable from "Hidden projects…" at the end of the list). An empty title never shows: a session reads "New chat" until its first prompt or generated title arrives, and the first prompt is seeded at launch.

New session from "+" (owner's decision 2026-09-28, following Codex and Claude Desktop; no modal sheet): "+" opens an unstarted session tab in the main area: one heading naming the project with the ordinary composer frame centred beneath it and nothing else in the pane. The composer's own controls are the setup while the session is unstarted (§5.4): the location chip (project directory, new worktree with a branch name, existing worktree) and add directory above the box, permission mode and model with effort in the toolbar below it. The first send creates the worktree if one was chosen, runs the trust step as a docked card for a never-trusted directory, checks the relay and offers the fallback as a docked card if it is down, launches the engine, and sends the message as the first turn. Closing an unstarted tab discards it, asking only when a draft is non-empty. Unstarted sessions do not appear in the sidebar. Amended 2026-10-01 (W2): the location chip and add directory sit in the toolbar below the box; nothing sits above it. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W3, D-18): the permission chip's initial mode is the user's `permissions.defaultMode`, else auto, never a literal "default"; when the engine refuses auto for a session (a model without auto mode, fast mode, the circuit breaker) MAWS sets the user's fallback mode explicitly, acceptEdits unless the user chose another in Settings, and shows a notice in Claude Desktop's words; the picker lists auto and bypass first.

### 5.2 Main area

Tab strip with the Overview tab first (⌘0, the default when the app opens), session tabs (status glyph, title), file tabs, diff tabs, and "+". Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W5, W6): the window's title-bar toolbar (§3's drag region with the wordmark, the sidebar toggle, the focused tab's title and branch chip, the session actions menu and the panel toggle) carries an Overview button at its left beside the sidebar toggle (⌘0 does the same) and a usage pill at its right before the session actions (W6, D-17); there is no second chrome row above the tab strip (owner's correction 2026-10-01 after W5 added one); the Overview is not a tab: the button opens it as a floating in-window panel, draggable and resizable, remembering its rect, closed by Esc or the button, with the session behind it staying live; the app opens to the last active tab. The tab strip holds session, file, diff and agent tabs and "+". Amended 2026-10-01 (New chat): the tab strip's "+", the File menu's `session.new` (⌘N, retitled New chat) and the palette entry open the same general new chat, available everywhere; when a project is focused (a project page open, or the active tab's session has one) the new chat starts with it chosen, otherwise with none. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's second look, W12): each project has its own tab set; the strip shows the current project's set (its name as a small label at the strip's left) and switches when a project is clicked in the sidebar or a session of another project is opened from anywhere; a New chat without a project lives in the no-project set until one is chosen. Clicking a session in the sidebar replaces the active tab's session (an open session is activated instead; an unstarted tab passes its close guard first; a pinned or non-session tab is left alone and a new tab opens after it); a new tab comes from ⌘-click or middle-click, the context menu's "Open in new tab", or a drag onto the strip.

The **Overview** board is Scene A's surface. Cards are grouped by project with a Needs-you group on top. A card shows the session title and branch with a dirty marker, the status glyph and current activity line, the last assistant line, the pending request inline (the same card the composer would dock, answerable with Enter and Esc when the card has focus), context percentage, cost since last seen, and child-agent count. Clicking a card opens the session tab; the sidebar row deep-links to the card. Cards for foreign-live sessions show the holder and no controls. Middle-click closes; drag to reorder; drag to the right edge opens the second pane; closing the last tab in a pane collapses the split. ⌘1..9 select tabs, ⌃Tab cycles. A session row dragged from the sidebar opens a tab. Amended 2026-10-01 (W5): the board's content, its Needs-you group and its cards are unchanged inside the floating panel; a sidebar row no longer deep-links to a card, the Overview button does.

### 5.3 Transcript

Turn structure, adapted from Codex (see `research/codex-thread-ux.md` §2):

- User message: right-aligned bubble, attachments, copy, edit-and-resubmit (which forks history, stated explicitly), collapse beyond twenty lines. Amended 2026-10-01 (W2, W3): the bubble follows Codex (§5.4) and collapses by rendered height, about nineteen lines, with "Show more"; Edit turns the bubble itself into an editor with Cancel, Send and Fork (Codex's in-place pattern): Send rewinds and resends, Fork opens a forked session, and nothing is pulled into the composer.
- Assistant turn: **answer first**. While working, a work area shows the active activity group (latest running tool or "Thinking"). On completion the work area collapses under a divider "Worked for 2m 14s · 12 tool calls · 1 denied"; clicking expands; the denied count jumps to the denial.
- Activity groups: consecutive groupable tool calls form one group with a semantic summary in fixed category order (MCP sources, loaded skills, file changes, exploration, commands, web). Standalone rows break groups: subagent lifecycle, approvals, errors, plans, questions, images, change receipts. A completed single-item group unwraps to a cell. Expanded group is a bounded scroller with cells; each cell expands to detail (command, cwd, output with exit status; edit with inline diff; read with path chips; MCP result with raw escape hatch); "Open full log" sends the cell to the right panel.
- Change receipt at the end of a turn: first three changed files with line counts, "Review changes" into the Review tool, "Show N more", Undo and Reapply bound to the engine's rewind and file checkpoints. Amended 2026-10-01 (W4): opening the Review tool from the receipt un-collapses a hidden right panel; a file row shows Codex's hover diff after 800ms (the turn's hunks, up to 420px tall) and a click opens the Review tab at that file; paths read relative to the session's directory; "Always allow in project" on a permission card names the file it writes (`settings.local.json`).
- Subagent rows: seeded glyph per agent, grouped sentences ("A, B and C started working", "finished", "finished with errors"), up to four glyphs then "N more", click opens the Subagents tool with that agent selected. Messages between agents render as rows ("Sent message to reviewer", "Message from parent").
- Thinking: separate disclosure "Thinking / Thought for 12s", bounded.
- Plan: card with the plan body and the three outcomes of §4.2: implement, clear context and implement, keep planning with feedback.
- Question (AskUserQuestion): inline form with numbered options, descriptions, recommended badge, previews rendered in an isolated frame with no privileged access, multi-select, free text; keyboard navigable; submit answers all questions. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W4): one question at a time in a 480px card with a "{n} of {N}" pager (Claude Desktop's), Next after every question, single-select included (owner's ruling 2026-10-01 after the r5 study: Claude Desktop never auto-advances, so a pick can be changed before moving on), Skip, Submit on the last step, all answers sent as the one reply the wire takes; afterwards the history row is one compact widget, one line per question reading "header or question → answer", with Skipped and Not answered kept, outside the fold like W1's persistent rows.
- Boundary rows for compaction, conversation reset and retraction, so the visible transcript matches what the model has.
- Errors and reconnects: transient rows with attempt counts; failed turns keep the partial output visible.
- Time separators after gaps; virtualised list with anchored reading position and a jump-to-latest control.

### 5.4 Composer and strip

The composer is one compact frame organised as Claude Desktop's (owner's ruling 2026-09-28, §19; Codex agrees on the split): above the box, a single left-aligned row of location chips, location (directory or worktree with its branch; its popover offers project directory, new worktree with a branch name, existing worktree, and the branch picker with search and create) and add directory; the box itself, holding only the editor and the submit control at its right, one text line tall at rest and growing to a cap; below the box, outside its border but inside the frame, a toolbar row of plain controls with "+", `@`, mic (P1), permission mode (default, acceptEdits, plan, auto, bypass) and model with effort at the right. Sizing keeps Claude Desktop's proportions (owner's note 2026-09-28, §19): the frame is about two thirds of the pane's width, capped near 720 px; the box is compact, the chip and toolbar rows are single lines of small controls, and the same proportion rule governs the session's other chat components (bubbles, cards, receipts, queue rows) relative to the transcript measure. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W2): the frame is exactly the transcript column's width (one shared class, Codex's rule), Codex's 22px radius and faint shadow, the editor 44px at rest growing to a quarter of the window height; there is no chip row above the box: the toolbar below it reads `+` · location chip · `+ dir` · permission mode … a per-session context ring · model with effort; there is no `@` button (typing `@` does the same) and no mic until dictation exists; the user bubble follows Codex (70 percent of the column, 22px radius, 10 by 16px padding, the text colour at 5 percent, prose size, actions on hover). There is no project chip (the tab names the project) and no routing, hint or explanatory line around the frame; routing and mode facts live in the status line (amended 2026-10-01, W2: there is no status line; route facts live in Session info and the model selector's Route segment, the mode on its chip). On an unstarted session the frame sits centred in the pane under one heading naming the project; once the session starts it docks to the bottom under the transcript. The permission chip shows the engine's effective mode from the initialize response and each init frame, never the optimistic selection; when a mode change is refused (auto mode is gated per model, fast mode, settings and a circuit breaker) the chip shows the refusal code in words, and the server-side classifier fallback dialog (Continue or Stop) is answered like any other request. The "+" menu carries files and photos, slash commands, connectors and plugins; the submit control's label follows state: Send, Stop, Queue, Steer, Resume. Amended 2026-10-01 (W1): while a turn runs the control reads Queue and Enter sends a `next` steer; ⌥Enter holds the message to the turn's end; Esc interrupts and sends now. Queued messages list above the composer with edit, delete, steer, "queue paused because you interrupted". `!` prefix runs a shell command through the engine's bash frame and shows output as a replayed row; `#` prefix appends to memory. ArrowUp on an empty editor recalls history. Pasted text beyond a threshold becomes an attachment. Drag-and-drop files attach; images paste inline. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W3): the model chip reads `<alias> <effort>` (opus high, sonnet xhigh, fable high, sol high), the alias from the matching catalogue row without its `claude/` prefix, and never "Default", "Default model", "Default (recommended)" or "Engine default"; the effective effort is read from `get_settings.applied.effort` when the session is ready and after each change, and an unstarted tab shows what the engine will use (the catalogue's default row resolved to its model, the user's `effortLevel`). The chip opens Codex's selector: one 254px menu anchored above the chip with two sliding views, the effort view (effort name in accent, a stepped slider, the model name as a row with a chevron, a reset) and the model list ("Select model", a "Default · Recommended" entry with a badge, a check on the current); `/model` and `/effort` open the same selector, so the inline model widget retires. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's request, New chat, the Codex app's workflow): an unstarted tab may have no project yet; its heading reads "New chat" and the location chip reads "Choose a project", whose popover lists every known project most recently active first (with search when there are many) and "Open folder…" (a native directory dialog; the chosen directory becomes a project in MAWS's own index by the existing membership rules, never a write under `~/.claude`); choosing a project patches the intent's location (trust resets as for any location change), names the tab after the project, and unlocks today's worktree choices for it; Send is disabled with "Choose a project first" until then; the engine launches on first send as before. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, the Codex app's simplicity): the location control is a project chip and a "Worktree" checkbox, nothing more. The chip's menu is Codex's: a search field, the known projects most recently active first with a check on the chosen one, "New project" (a folder dialog) and "Don't work in a project" (the session runs in the home directory and lists under Other sessions); the current branch is shown as a read-only label beside the chip; the branch picker with search and create, the named-worktree form and the existing-worktree option are removed, since users ask the agent to check out a worktree or make a branch. Checked, "Worktree" makes the first send create a new worktree with an auto-generated name and branch through C10's flow; a worktree group's "+" preselects that directory and hides the checkbox. Additional directories stay in the toolbar. Placement (owner's ruling 2026-10-01, Codex's): on an unstarted tab the chip and the checkbox sit in a slim row inside the frame above the box, chip left, Worktree right; once the session starts that row is gone and the toolbar's location chip shows the project and branch read-only. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W10): the toolbar reads `+` (files, photos, add directory, the palette, connectors, plugins) · location (project with its branch) · permission mode … model with effort · context ring, the ring outermost; there is no `+ dir` chip.

A pending permission or question replaces the composer surface with its card, naming the requesting agent, the exact operation, and the grant scope; Enter approves, Esc declines, the composer returns afterward. Slash palette: `/` opens a searchable list of engine commands, skills, custom commands and MAWS actions, each marked by origin; commands with a terminal menu open a widget over the transcript. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W3): the list stays a caret-anchored popover sized as Claude Desktop's (240 to 512px wide, at most 384px tall, app commands then engine commands with their hints); a command's widget opens as a floating dialog centred over the window (Claude Desktop's /mcp, /plugins and /sandbox dialogs), never inside the box and never in the right panel; Esc closes it and focus returns to the composer. `/rewind`, and Esc Esc on an empty composer while no turn runs, open the rewind picker: the session's user messages, with conversation, files or both, over `session.rewind` and `session.rewindFiles` (Esc while a turn runs keeps its W1 meaning, interrupt).

### 5.5 Status line

Removed 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W2): nothing sits under the composer. The six fields move: cwd to the toolbar's location chip, context to the toolbar's ring (popover with percent, the autocompact threshold and Compact), usage windows to the top-bar pill (W6), cost to Session info, mode to its chip; the Direct⇄gateway route switch and the Direct `get_usage` poll move to Session info; the near-limit banner stays, app-wide. The original definition follows for history. cwd · context used of window (with the autocompact threshold) · usage 5h and 7d with reset times · session cost · permission mode. Context and cost are per session and come from `get_context_usage` and the engine's accounting; the usage windows are account-wide and come from one ClaudeUsage read shared by every session, with the engine's own `get_usage` windows as the fallback for a direct session when ClaudeUsage is unavailable, so showing them under each composer costs nothing and matches the owner's design. Clicking opens popovers. A near-limit banner is account-wide too and appears once per window, not above every composer.

### 5.6 Right panel

Tab strip with the session's tools, "+" to add a tool (Subagents, Review, Terminal, Files, Background work, Session info; Sent files belongs to the next phase, P1, as a MAWS-integrated SendUserFile, owner's ruling 2026-09-28), pin toggle per tab, maximize, close. Resting state with no tab open: the pinned summary, a compact card with collapsible sections: branch and diff stats with "Review", push-state row (`N ahead of <upstream>`, `Not pushed`, with "Open on GitHub" from the remote URL; pull-request status and checks are P1 with source control, owner's ruling 2026-09-28), Subagents (avatars, active/done counts), Background work (N running), Sources (files and tools used). Every row opens its tool.

- Subagents: Active · N and Done · N lists; row shows glyph, name, objective or status, elapsed; drill-in shows the child transcript with the same renderer, model and effort, a relay composer, and "Back". The relay (owner's ruling 2026-09-28): the engine offers no host path to a running child, so the drill-in composer sends one steer-priority message asking the parent to forward it, labelled `Relay via <session>`, shown as "Asked <session> to relay…" until the `SendMessage` row appears in both transcripts, and enabled for a background child of a live parent (its `session.steer` with `priority: 'next'` folds into the parent's running turn at the next tool boundary, or runs at once when idle), disabled for a foreground child with the reason (corrected 2026-09-28, §19). Nested children render under their parent. A topology view is a later addition, not the default.
- Review: file list and diff of the session's changes (unified or split) under Codex's scopes (owner's ruling 2026-09-28, D-20; the engine records no session-start commit and none is invented): Uncommitted (working tree against `HEAD`, the default on a dirty tree), This branch (three-dot against the main worktree's branch, the default on a clean tree), Last turn (the change receipt's files), Staged when the index differs; file navigation, path chips open file tabs; P1 adds staging and commits.
- Terminal: PTY in the session's cwd; warns when the session moved to another worktree and offers a new terminal instead of pretending it moved.
- Files: tree rooted at the session's directories; click opens a viewer tab in the main area. The P0 viewer (owner's ruling 2026-09-28) is read-only: code in CodeMirror 6 with syntax highlighting and a line jump (the P1 editor's base), images inline, markdown rendered with a Source toggle; PDF, HTML and media are "open externally" rows; the sandboxed frame for PDF and HTML is P1 (owner, 2026-09-28).
- Sent files: the collection of files the agent delivered through SendUserFile, rendered by type, with a back arrow from item to list. Deferred to the next phase, P1, as a MAWS-integrated SendUserFile (owner's ruling 2026-09-28, no P0 stand-in): the engine's `SendUserFile` is disabled under MAWS's entrypoint and, when enabled, uploads the bytes to claude.ai, so MAWS's own delivery path is P1 work.
- Background work: shells, monitors, task lists and workflows; a workflow shows title, description, phases with agent tables (model, tokens, time) and per-agent transcripts, stop control; pause, skip and retry are not available over the wire and are not shown.
- Session info: engine version, model, context, usage, cost, hooks and MCP status, permission rules.

### 5.7 Attention and notifications

With auto mode as the default (D-18), most items are questions, plan reviews, the classifier's escalations and errors; ordinary approvals are the exception, not the stream. An item enters Needs-you when a permission, question, plan approval, dialog or elicitation is pending, when a turn or background task ends in error, when a process exits unexpectedly or retires answers, or when a peer message is held (informational, and it expires, so the row says so). A session is "unread" when its turn ended while its tab was not visible. The sidebar glyph, the tab glyph, the Overview card, the Needs-you row and the OS notification all derive from the same attention table. ⌘⇧J jumps to the oldest Needs-you item. Under a split, Enter and Esc bind to the focused pane only. Stream deltas are batched per animation frame before crossing IPC.

### 5.8 Keyboard

⌘N new session, ⌘K command palette, ⌘B sidebar, ⌘⇧B right panel, ⌘J bottom dock (P1), ⌘1..9 tabs, ⌃Tab cycle, ⌘W close tab, ⌘\ split, ⌘⇧J next Needs-you, Enter/Esc on a docked request, ⌘Enter send when the editor is multi-line, ⇧Tab cycles permission mode as in the terminal. All bindings editable.

## 6. Visual identity

Light-first, in Claude's bright style, with a dark theme as the alternate; the mockups under `mockups/` were built dark and approved for structure, so their light rendering is the reference for tokens. Chrome follows Codex density: 13px UI text, 4px spacing base, 6 to 12px radii, muted metadata at 60 percent foreground promoted on hover, thin separators, no heavy cards. Transcript follows Claude's reading comfort: 15 to 16px prose, 1.6 line height, 68 to 72 character measure, headings with clear rhythm, code at 13px monospace. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W2, D-4): the transcript takes Codex's measured values: 14px prose at 1.625 line height (22.75px pitch), 12px code, 13px chrome, headings at 1.5/1.25/1.125/1 of prose, a 768px column with 16px inline padding set in px rather than ch (Claude Desktop's 960 and 1280 as a setting), wide blocks allowed to 896px; the measure no longer follows the font. Colors are semantic tokens (surface, surface-raised, foreground, foreground-muted, accent, success, warning, danger, diff-added, diff-removed) so the whole look can be re-skinned. Fonts are tokens too; the mockups use system fonts and the owner chooses.

## 7. Delegated unknowns

Empirical questions the design cannot settle; each has its resolving move and runs first.

1. **Protocol fixture probe**: first pass done. Seven scenarios recorded under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/` with a fixture test file, plus a real subagent transcript and its meta sidecar copied from the probe session so the child-JSONL mapping has a fixture; findings folded into §4.2. Second pass (child C2) done: `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md`, 17 scenarios and 29 fixtures under `fixtures/pass2/`, findings folded into §4.1 to §4.6 and X1/X6. The unrouted model turns (engine usage windows, the direct classifier lane) did not run because the owner's login had expired; under D-19 the direct path is a fallback, not a P0 acceptance path, so this residual is closed as not required. `node run-pass2.mjs auto-mode` remains available if the direct lane ever becomes load-bearing. Still unobserved: the `automode-blocked` and `automode-unavailable` denial kinds, any `request_user_dialog` producer, and rewind with file checkpointing on (C3 verifies).

Replay has two sources that test different code: the JSONL exercises backfill only (it holds no stream events, control requests or progress), while the raw-frame journal and the probe fixtures replay approvals, streaming, queueing and child forwarding. Both are P0.
2. **Terminal spike** (C9 M1; research already points at xterm.js, see the Decision Log): ghostty-web with node-pty under real Claude Code and Codex TUI workloads (alternate screen, synchronized output, CJK and emoji, IME, selection, hyperlinks, scrollback, resize, sustained output). Fallback is xterm.js with WebGL.
3. **Transcript spike**: a long streaming transcript with nested child activity, markdown, code and diagrams, with TanStack Virtual end anchoring; verify that expanding old work never drags the reader back to the stream.
4. **Gateway behaviour**: model discovery and per-session gateway toggling with ClaudeUsage's relay. Known from its source: the relay answers `GET /api/hello` locally (a health check that never reaches Anthropic), forwards every `/v1/` request with the account token substituted, serves `/v1/models` with the gateway catalogue, keeps accounts in `~/Library/Application Support/ClaudeUsage/accounts.json`, usage history per account under `history/<uuid>.jsonl`, and session-to-account assignments in `pins.json` (format read at C11 time). Confirmed so far: `get_usage` reports no rate-limit windows when routed, which is why the windows come from ClaudeUsage; routed `get_status` reports the auto-mode server lane enabled and its verdicts arrive in `safeguard_results`; unrouting needs the flag-settings env override, not just an unset variable.
5. **Fonts and final tokens**: chosen from the mockups.

## 8. Milestones

Superseded by the cut in §12. The original M0 to M5 sequence was a build order; the children below are the units of ownership, and the waves in §14 are the build order.

## 9. Research index

- `research/codex-thread-ux.md`: transcript, activity grouping state machine, approvals, composer, queue semantics.
- `research/codex-shell-ux.md`: shell geometry, sidebar organisation, pinned summary, subagents panel, composer strip, worktree lifecycle, tokens.
- `research/claude-desktop-ux-and-engine.md`: Claude Code session UI on claude.ai, the 307-method LocalSessions contract, how Desktop spawns and brokers the engine.
- `research/sdk-wire-gap.md`: the complete 2.1.283 wire, the 67-row gap matrix, the four app-side mechanisms.
- `research/landscape.md`: other agent workstations, terminal embedding, rendering libraries, Electron practice, Playwright over a WebContentsView.

---

# Roadmap

The sections below extend the approved design into the composite spec for P0: the cut into children, the contracts that cross them, the ordering, and the record that tracks the unit to its retrospective. Decisions D-1 to D-17 and the verified engine facts in §4.2 are binding everywhere; the grades on the rest are given per child.

## 10. Parent-level acceptance

P0 closes when, on the owner's Mac with the pinned engine, every item below is observed in one sitting:

1. MAWS opens to the Overview showing the owner's existing terminal-started sessions under their projects, with sessions live in a terminal shown as foreign-live.
2. From a project's "+", a session starts in a new worktree with trust attested; project settings, hooks, MCP servers and plugins load as they do in the terminal.
3. From an Overview card, the owner approves a command and answers an AskUserQuestion for a session in another project while an OS notification fires for a third session that needs them; ⌘⇧J lands on it.
4. In one deep session: a subagent's transcript opens in the right panel and receives a message relayed through its parent; the Review tool shows the session's diff; a terminal opens in the session's worktree; a file opens in a viewer tab; a workflow shows its phases and agents in Background work (Sent files deferred to P1, 2026-09-28).
5. That session resumes afterward from the terminal with `claude --resume`, and a terminal-started session resumes in MAWS.
6. Nine concurrent live sessions run under the memory budget of §4.4: with the budget lowered below their total, the least recently used idle session holding nothing that would be lost is released without a notification or a Needs-you item and resumes on Send with its session grants; sessions holding a turn, work in flight, background work, a request, queued messages, a held message or a stateful server are never released; quitting while sessions are mid-turn, running background work or holding a busy terminal asks first and can wait for idle. (Restated 2026-09-29 at C12's dispatch under decision D1, §19.) Measured 2026-09-29 under a scratch config home (C12 M3, run 7, `docs/doperpowers/evidence/c12/budget-run.json`): nine trees, sum 2,854 MiB (the independent `ps` read agrees within 5% per tree and in sum, asserted), budget lowered to 2,553 MiB, releases 1 (the least recently used eligible session, 0.19 s after the budget was set; with the budget then below the protected sessions, four more releases and one banner, and nothing holding work stopped), resume 0.63 s from Send to ready with the session grant re-applied (no approval asked). A scratch tree is 310 to 360 MB; the owner's real figures come from the sitting.
7. A five-hundred-turn transcript stays anchored while old work is expanded, and streaming never drags the reader down.
8. The slash palette opens the model, effort, permissions, usage, context, config, plugins, MCP, sandbox, hooks, memory and skills widgets backed by the engine's reads; `!` and `#` work.
9. Queue, steer, stop and resume behave as labelled; a queued message survives a process exit; a denied command is visible while its group is collapsed; a process that dies with approvals pending raises "N approvals lost" (restated 2026-09-30 at C12's landing: on 2.1.283 both ⌘. and quit cancel a pending approval through `control_cancel_request`, so only a crash loses one).
10. The light theme is the default and the dark theme switches cleanly.

## 11. Design authority

Binding, because only the joint view could settle them: the purpose and scenes (§1), D-1 to D-17, the process model and launch contract (§4.1, extended by X6), the engine adapter semantics verified by the probe (§4.2), queue ownership, plan outcomes, `!` and `#` semantics and the revisable-log rule (§4.2 "further engine semantics"), the filesystem service rules (§4.3), the memory policy shape and interim rule (§4.4), the broker states (§4.6), the attention model (§5.7), and every cross-child contract in §13. Advisory, a child may overturn with evidence recorded in the Decision Log: library picks (§4.7), the per-surface layouts and copy in §5, the keyboard defaults (§5.8), token values (§6), and the sequencing suggestions inside each child section.

## 12. Children

Level name for every child: "child", dispatched in the waves of §14. Ledgers: a plan child's record is its execution document under `docs/doperpowers/plans/` plus the subagent-driven-execution ledger; a brief child opens `docs/doperpowers/ledgers/<date>-<child-id>.md`; a spike writes its findings under `spikes/`.

### C1: Foundations — plan

- **Purpose:** the repository everything else builds in: electron-vite scaffold with React 19, the React Compiler and Tailwind v4; the process model skeleton (main, engine-host utility process, PTY host utility process, preload); the validated IPC schema (X2) and the shared types for the event graph and session record (X1) with runtime validators; the theme tokens (X7) light-first with a dark alternate; the test harness (unit tests, an Electron end-to-end smoke test, fixture loading); packaging skeleton for a local unsigned build.
- **Acceptance:** `pnpm dev` opens a window with the three regions and the light theme; the engine host and PTY host start as utility processes and answer a ping over IPC; the PTY host spawns a shell and echoes; X1 and X2 schemas validate the probe fixtures' frames and reject malformed ones in unit tests; the end-to-end smoke test passes in CI-style headless mode; a local build produces an app bundle.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: none. blocks: C3, C4, C5, C6, C9.
- **Contracts:** owns X1, X2, X7; delivers the schema code that X3, X4, X5, X6 extend.
- **Design inheritance:** §4.1 [binding], §4.7 [advisory], §6 [advisory values, binding token names per X7], `mockups/tokens.css` and `tokens-light.css` as the starting token set.
- **Residue decisions (C1, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c1-foundations.md` carries the milestones and the concrete X1 and X2 definitions. Grades: binding where the design binds the outcome, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Package manager*: pnpm 11.23, pinned in `packageManager`. Two pnpm facts shape the scaffold because they fail silently: pnpm 11 blocks dependency install scripts unless listed under `allowBuilds` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (Electron's binary download and node-pty's prebuild selection are install scripts; `electron`, `node-pty`, `esbuild`, `@tailwindcss/oxide` are listed), and electron-builder needs a hoisted `node_modules` (`nodeLinker: hoisted` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `node-linker=hoisted` in `.npmrc`, both, because electron-builder 26.15 reads pnpm's config output to detect the layout and pnpm 11 stopped printing that key). Alternative: npm, which needs neither, rejected for consistency with the sibling projects. Advisory.
  - *Electron*: 44.4.5, pinned exactly (Chromium 152, Node 24.21.0; macOS 13+). Exact because the packaged app, the N-API prebuild path and the Playwright launcher are all verified against one runtime; upgrading reruns the last milestone's gate. Alternative: Electron 43 for longer remaining support, rejected because nothing needs it and 44's sandboxed-renderer start-up gains are what the next year builds on. Advisory.
