  - *Toolchain around it*: electron-vite 5.0 peers on Vite 5 to 7, so Vite 7.3 and `@vitejs/plugin-react` 5.2 (6.x requires Vite 8); React 19.3 with `babel-plugin-react-compiler` 1.0 through plugin-react's babel option, enabled globally since there is no legacy code; Tailwind 4.3; TypeScript 5.9.3, not the 7.0 that npm's `latest` points at, because typescript-eslint 8.70 caps below 6.1 and the Go compiler is a swap this scaffold should not also absorb; ESLint 10 with typescript-eslint 8.70 and `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7 (React Compiler rules); zod 4.6; zustand 5; TanStack Query 5.104; vitest 5 with happy-dom; `@playwright/test` 1.63; electron-builder 26.15; node-pty 1.1.0 exactly (the version whose tarball ships the darwin-arm64 prebuild); `@types/node` 24. Exact pins for `electron`, `node-pty`, `electron-builder`; carets elsewhere; lockfile committed. Advisory.
  - *Module format*: CommonJS for main, preload and the utility-process entries; ESM for the renderer. Electron runs sandboxed preloads as plain scripts and electron-vite documents that an ESM preload must be unsandboxed; sandbox is binding (§4.1), so the preload is CJS, and main and hosts stay CJS to keep one module system in the bundle set. Alternative: ESM main with a CJS preload by output-format override, rejected as complexity without payoff in P0. Advisory.
  - *Directory layout*: `src/main`, `src/preload`, `src/renderer`, `src/engine-host`, `src/pty-host`, `src/shared`, with `shared/` split into `ipc` (X2), `graph` (X1 events, session record, attention item), `wire` (engine frames and transcript records) and `host` (the utility-process runtime), because those have different owners downstream and a reviewer reads one folder per contract; alias `@shared/*` from every target; unit tests beside their source; `test/support/` for the fixture loader; `e2e/` for Playwright. Alternative: a `packages/` monorepo, rejected because there is one app and one lockfile. Advisory.
  - *IPC registry composition* (2026-09-28 review): the X2 registries are composed from one module per namespace, `src/shared/ipc/schema/{session,engine,fs,git,pty,layout,attention,settings}.ts` (plus `common.ts` for the shared pieces), merged by object spread in `src/shared/ipc/schema.ts`, which stays the only import path and X2's canonical file. Reason: five wave-2 children (C3, C4, C5, C6, C9) extend X2 on parallel branches; with per-namespace files each adds a file's worth of entries in its own namespace and the merge has no shared hunk, where one registry file would conflict on every landing. A test pins that each module's keys carry its namespace and that the merged key count equals the sum, so a duplicate key cannot be silently overwritten by the spread. The host protocol has no per-host parts (one envelope; hosts register the X2 entries they own), so nothing there splits. Alternative: one file with namespace sections, rejected for the merge conflicts. Advisory. Debt wave 4 (2026-09-29): each namespace also has a zod-free name list, `src/shared/ipc/names/<namespace>.ts`, merged in `src/shared/ipc/names.ts` (which owns `NAMESPACES` and the channel helpers); the preload's allowlist is those lists, so it bundles no schema (313 kB to 8.5 kB); `schema.ts` refuses to compile unless they name exactly the registries' keys; a child adding an entry to `schema/<ns>.ts` adds its name to `names/<ns>.ts`.
  - *Validation library*: zod 4 (`import * as z from 'zod'`), the sibling projects' choice. `z.looseObject` is the wire's unknown-field retention rule, `z.strictObject` is what the app's own event schemas want, `.brand<>()` gives the eight identifier types at zero runtime cost, and discriminated unions compose. Rule: validators in `src/shared/` never transform, default or coerce, so an accepted frame deep-equals its parse (the round-trip test). Wire frames are validated by dispatch on `type` (and `system` `subtype`) to one schema per known discriminator, with the loose schema applied only to discriminators no table knows, because zod 4's discriminated union cannot carry a string-typed catch-all member and a plain union lets a malformed known frame fall through to the catch-all (2026-09-28 review). Alternative: Valibot or TypeBox, rejected for team familiarity. Advisory.
  - *State wiring*: Zustand stores fed by X2 events; TanStack Query for request/response data. C1 ships one store (settings, subscribed to `settings.changed`) and one hook (`useCommand` over `invoke`) as the pattern, nothing more. Advisory (§4.7).
  - *Test runners*: vitest 5 as two projects (node environment for shared, main and hosts; happy-dom for the renderer); Playwright's `_electron` for the end-to-end smoke. Playwright labels Electron support experimental, and it is still the only maintained driver that launches an Electron main entry and evaluates in both the main process and the page; it cannot evaluate inside utility processes, so the hosts are proven through the bridge (pings and the PTY echo), the only path the product uses anyway. Alternative: WebdriverIO's Electron service, rejected as a second driver stack for no gain. Advisory.
  - *Utility processes, build and start*: each host entry is bundled by electron-vite through the `?modulePath` import suffix (the documented route; the import evaluates to the emitted file's path in dev and inside the packaged asar alike) and forked by a supervisor in main with `utilityProcess.fork`, one `MessageChannelMain` port per host, a shared host-side runtime, and a request/response/event envelope validated at both ends with the X2 schemas. The supervisor starts once at app ready and its start is idempotent (a second call forks nothing), so hosts are app-scoped and survive renderer reloads and window re-creation, the §4.1 reload-survival boundary; host state is readable as a snapshot command as well as emitted as events, because a reloaded page has no history of transitions (2026-09-28 review). Crash policy in C1: restart with backoff, three restarts per minute then stay crashed, pending requests fail as host-unavailable; C3 decides engine-host semantics beyond that (D-16). Fallback if the suffix misbehaves: explicit rollup inputs for the host entries. Binding on the process model (§4.1), advisory on mechanism.
  - *node-pty native module*: use the N-API prebuild shipped in node-pty 1.1.0 (`prebuilds/darwin-arm64/pty.node` and `spawn-helper`); no rebuild for Electron, because N-API is ABI-stable across Node 22 (vitest) and Electron 44 (the PTY host). Consequences: `npmRebuild: false` in electron-builder, node-pty external to the bundle and listed in `asarUnpack` so the binary and the helper keep their executable bit. Documented fallback: `electron-rebuild -f -w node-pty` as a script, with a dated line here if it ever becomes the default. Alternative: a prebuilt fork, rejected because upstream ships what our one platform needs. Advisory.
  - *Theme switching*: a persisted `settings.theme` of light, dark or system, default light (D-4 and §10 item 10 make light the product's default rather than a follower of the OS). Main owns the setting, sets `nativeTheme.themeSource` so native chrome follows, resolves system against `nativeTheme.shouldUseDarkColors`, and emits `settings.changed` with the setting and the resolved theme; the renderer sets `data-theme` on `<html>`, `index.html` ships `data-theme="light"` for the first paint, and the window's `backgroundColor` is the resolved theme's `--bg`. Tokens: `:root, [data-theme="light"]` and `[data-theme="dark"]`. Tailwind's default palette and size scales are removed (`--color-*: initial` and kin) and every X7 token is mapped through `@theme inline`, so `bg-bg` exists and `bg-red-500` compiles to nothing; a guard test fails on colour literals and palette classes in renderer sources. Alternative: `prefers-color-scheme` alone, rejected because it cannot express an explicit choice and leaves native chrome out of step. Binding on light-first and tokens-only; advisory on mechanism.
  - *Window security defaults*: `sandbox`, `contextIsolation`, `nodeIntegration: false`, window-open and navigation denied, a production CSP set from main (`default-src 'self'` with inline styles allowed for Tailwind, no remote scripts; in development the same policy with a per-launch nonce shared between the header and the dev server's HTML, so the inline fast-refresh preamble runs without `'unsafe-inline'` for scripts), the IPC router checking that the sender is the app window's top frame at the app's origin before dispatch; `titleBarStyle: 'hiddenInset'` per §3. Binding (§4.1; `research/landscape.md` Part D).
  - *Packaging skeleton*: electron-builder with the `dir` target for arm64, unsigned (`identity: null`, `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false` so a Developer ID in the keychain is not picked up by accident), `asarUnpack` for node-pty, `npmRebuild: false`; output `dist/mac-arm64/MAWS.app`. Signing, notarization, DMG and the updater are C12. Advisory.
  - *Run-everything script*: `pnpm verify` chains typecheck, lint, unit tests, `electron-vite build`, the Playwright smoke against the build, the unsigned app build, and the same smoke against the packaged binary, as one npm script with no runner dependency. No CI workflow file in C1 (no remote yet; signing is C12). Advisory.
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** in-flight since 2026-09-28 (execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c1-foundations.md`; dispatch pin 32b75d61, authored against the pin recorded in that document).

### C2: Protocol probe, second pass — spike

- **Purpose:** turn the remaining engine unknowns into recorded fixtures and findings before the engine host is specified, so C3 and C10 are built on observed behaviour.
- **Acceptance:** fixtures and a findings document covering: every scenario run with `--permission-mode auto` as the default, recording what the classifier emits on allow, escalation and denial (the wire-gap report expects only escalations and denials to be observable), both routed through the gateway and unrouted, with `auto_mode_server_fallback` and `refusal_fallback_prompt` declared in `supportedDialogKinds`, recording, per scenario, which lane decided (a server-side verdict on the main response, or a local classifier request, read from the status read's Auto mode server row and the debug log's `classifier_request_started` line), the local lane's model when used, and whether the server-fallback dialog appeared; unrouted runs (gateway variables unset) of the usage, models and fork scenarios; the same under the owner's real settings sources with MCP discovery on, including the process-tree memory measurement per session; workflow progress frames from a real Workflow run; rewind; hook callbacks with `--include-hook-events`; MCP elicitation and `request_user_dialog` with dialog expiry set to never; the effect of `perTaskStopAffordance` on Stop with a background task running; `workspaceTrust` attestation and `set_cwd` needs-trust; re-applying session grants through flag settings after a restart; resuming a session that is live in a terminal (guard or not); the non-querying `user` frame after `bash_command`; `--await-claim` and `claim_session`. Each item states what was observed and what the design should do.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: none. blocks: C3, C10.
- **Contracts:** delivers X8 fixtures.
- **Design inheritance:** §4.2 [binding as verified], §7.1 [binding list], `spikes/protocol-probe/harness.mjs` [advisory, extend it].
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** landed 2026-09-28 (parent pin 32b75d61, D-18 pin 4ff2f2df; commit f776f7e). Findings in `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md`; 56 fixture tests pass. The unrouted model turns did not run (expired login) and are closed as not required under D-19; acceptance otherwise met, except the two denial kinds no scenario could provoke and the local classifier lane, which C2 did not exercise (a Claude main through the gateway always took the server lane); peer session maws-14 verified the local lane on a GPT-routed main the same day (see the Decision Log).

### C3: Engine host — plan

- **Purpose:** the adapter that owns one engine process per session over the raw wire: transport and framing, request ids, cancellation and timeouts, the initialize handshake with hook registration and trust attestation, the control-request surface, the permission broker with its states, the app-owned queue, the process table with the interim never-exit rule, session launch from X6, raw-frame journaling, and normalisation into X1 events.
- **Acceptance:** against the pinned binary: a session starts, streams text and thinking, runs a tool through a permission round trip answered from the host, answers an AskUserQuestion, dispatches subagents whose text arrives attributed by parent, queues a second message and releases it at turn end, steers with priority, interrupts and resumes, exits and is resumed with the queue intact and its session grants re-applied without a re-prompt; an engine-initiated turn after a workflow or background completion is accepted without releasing a queued message into it, and when that turn ends with the session idle the queued head releases as after any turn (architect, 2026-09-28, §19); rewind sends `last_seen`, truncates the host view and pre-fills the composer; a direct launch is verified direct by `get_status`, and the default launch is verified routed; the broker raises "interrupted, N approvals lost" at exit; every structured read is exposed as a typed call; the journal replays into the same X1 graph in a unit test; conformance tests run against the C2 fixtures from both passes. C3 also verifies rewind with file checkpointing enabled, which C2 could not.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C1 (C2 landed). blocks: C7, C10, C11.
- **Contracts:** owns X6; produces X1; consumes X8.
- **Design inheritance:** §4.1, §4.2, §4.4 process policy, §4.6 [all binding]; `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md` [binding as verified, per item]; §4.7 stack [advisory].
- **Residue decisions (C3, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c3-engine-host.md` carries the interfaces, milestones and gates. Grades: binding where a sibling codes against the outcome, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Transport framing and backpressure*: stdout is decoded with a UTF-8 `StringDecoder`, split on `\n`, blank lines dropped, a leading BOM stripped; an unterminated line over 64 Mi characters is a transport error that kills the process (the engine's own budget is 256 Mi; ours protects the host); a line that fails to parse is journaled and surfaced as `error.raised { scope: 'transport' }`, never fatal. Stdin has one serialised writer; a write resolves when the OS accepted the bytes (write callback, `drain` honoured), which is the §4.6 delivery truth for a `control_response`. No `keep_alive` is sent (the engine drops it on input); U+2028/2029 are not escaped (the engine's reader splits on `\n` only). Alternative: Node `readline`, rejected because it hides the budget and the decoder boundary. Advisory.
  - *Request ids and timeouts*: host-minted ids are `${launchId}-${n}` (eight base32 characters per spawn, then a counter), distinguishable at a glance from the engine's UUIDs. Four timeout classes: `read` 8 s (`get_*`, `list_*`, `mcp_status`, `export_conversation`, `get_status`, `rewind_files` dry run), `mutate` 8 s (`set_*`, `apply_flag_settings`, `rename_session`, `stop_task`, `cancel_async_message`, `set_cwd`, `rewind_conversation`, `rewind_files`, `end_session`), `interrupt` 8 s, `long` 120 s (`initialize`, `claim_session`, `generate_session_title`, `side_question`, `reload_plugins`, `mcp_reconnect`, `mcp_toggle`); inbound human-facing requests have none (dialog expiry is never). The bounded classes are 8 s because X2's router fails a host command at 10 s and C1's `CommandSpec` has no per-command deadline: every X2 command the host answers synchronously runs under one 8 s budget (pass-2 evidence: reads and mutations answer in milliseconds), and the `long` subtypes a command can reach are *operations* (`session.generateTitle`, `session.reloadPlugins`, `session.sideQuestion`, `session.mcpReconnect`, `session.mcpToggle`): an `OperationReceipt { opId }` at the flushed write and completion through the event `session.operation`, emitted once more with `late: true` when a timed-out request answers inside the late table. A timeout rejects the caller, writes a `control_cancel_request` (harmless for uncancellable subtypes), and parks the id for 60 s so a late answer is journaled and reconciled (a warning notice; the state corrected by the next frame carrying it), not treated as unexpected; a timeout never proves the mutation did not apply. Alternatives: one 30 s timeout, rejected because `initialize` under the owner's settings and MCP prewait legitimately exceeds it; lengthening C1's router timeout or adding a per-command deadline to `CommandSpec`, rejected because the router is C1's and ten seconds is the right bound for anything a click awaits. Advisory. Amended 2026-09-30 (the C3 corrective after C12's landing, `docs/doperpowers/ledgers/2026-09-30-c3-handshake-stall.md`, merged a2b88bde): a starting engine that writes no stdout frame and no stderr line within 30 s of its spawn fails its launch as `init_failed` with the code `no_output` (the ladder without `end_session`, the row resumable, the reason in the toast), since a healthy start writes within 0.3 s; an engine that has written anything keeps `initialize`'s 120 s `long` class, because 2.1.283 runs SessionStart hooks (600 s default each) before it reads `initialize`. Main's resume path relaunches once after `no_output`, visibly (the setup detail, a `launch:retry` notice, the reconnect rows reading attempt 2 of 2) and never chained; the retry's watch ignores the launch it replaces. Main's `LaunchWatch` counts only events with a journal `seq`: C4's follower synthesises a `process.exited` with `seq: null` for a transcript whose last turn never ended, and that event had killed 15 of 29 such resumes 2 ms after their spawn. Debt wave 5A (2026-09-30, 5d52598d): each SessionStart hook frame the engine streams before answering `initialize` restarts its 120 s deadline (`ControlClient.extendDeadline`), and a hook that has reported starting but not finished holds it at 600 s plus 120 s, so a slow project or plugin hook no longer fails a legitimate resume; the 30 s first-output bound is unchanged.
  - *X2 surface*: the engine host owns `engine.binaryInfo`, `engine.relayHealth`, `engine.sessions`, `engine.shutdown`; `session.launch` (returns `launching` at spawn, readiness by event, or `relay_unreachable` / `refused` with `already_live`, `binary_missing`, `binary_version`, `replay_disabled` or `settings_conflict`), `session.status`, `session.events` (reload snapshot with `afterSeq`), `session.send`, `session.steer`, `session.queue.{list,edit,remove,promote,setPaused}`, `session.interrupt`, `session.answer` (one discriminated `RequestAnswer` for permission, question, plan, dialog, elicitation), `session.setPermissionMode`, `session.setModel`, `session.setEffort`, `session.setThinking`, `session.applyFlagSettings`, `session.stopTask`, `session.rewind`, `session.rewindFiles` (C6's `engine.rewindFiles` shape, dry run included, under `session.*`), `session.bash`, `session.memoryAppend`, `session.setCwd`, `session.renameSession`, `session.generateTitle`, `session.reloadPlugins`, `session.mcpReconnect`, `session.mcpToggle`, `session.sideQuestion` (with `session.generateTitle` and `session.reloadPlugins` the five operations, answering `OperationReceipt { opId }`), one `session.get*`/`list*` command per structured read, `session.stop`; events `session.eventBatch` (C6's name; batched 16 ms / 500; C4 emits the same for backfill and replay), `session.status`, `session.queue`, `session.operation` (operation completion, once more with `late: true` for a late success), `session.brokerItems` (internal, to main); command `session.pendingRequest`. The `attention.*` entries (`attention.list`, `attention.changed`, `attention.request`) are owner `main`, defined here and answered by C3's attention service in main, which merges the broker's items with the derived kinds and persists them through the `AttentionStore` port that C4's index implements (ruling 1 of the wave-2 ledger). Every per-session payload names the session `appSessionId`. Payloads in the execution document's Interfaces. Binding (C6, C7, C10, C11 code against the names; C5 against `attention.*`).
  - *Bash echo convention*: the `!` echo frame is sent with `origin: { kind: 'human' }` (the engine's closed origin vocabulary; verified in pass 2) and is never replayed by the engine, so the host emits its `user.message` event itself from the `out` row with `origin: 'bash_echo'` and `isSynthetic: true`; renderers hide it and treat the following empty `result` as the receipt. Alternative: C6's proposal to stamp `origin.kind: 'bash_echo'` on the wire, rejected because an unrecognised kind fails closed at the engine's human-trust gates. Binding.
  - *Spill path*: `tool.result.spillPath` is the absolute path the engine reports, from Bash's `tool_use_result.persistedOutputPath` or the `Output too large (…). Full output saved to: <path>` prefix of any other tool's result text; null otherwise; background shell output files ride `task.notification.outputFile`. Binding (C4 reads the file, C6 shows the affordance).
  - *Reconnect and exit payloads*: `process.exited` carries `lostAttentionCount` from the broker; `session.reconnect` fires only for a user-initiated resume in P0 (`trigger: 'user'`, `attempt: 1`, `maxAttempts: 1`), `attempting` at accept, `reconnected` at the initialize response, `failed` with the error text; the other triggers are reserved for C12. Both derive from `meta` rows the host journals (`exit`, `reconnect`), so their `seq` is the row's and a journal rebuild reproduces them. Binding on the payloads, advisory on the P0 restriction.
  - *Journal*: `<userData>/engine-host/journal/<appSessionId>/<launchId>.jsonl`, one file per process launch, X8 rows with `seq` from 1, `meta` rows for spawn (args with the `--settings` JSON, env key names only), relay health, initialize summary, gateway verification, queue releases and exit; rotation at 128 MiB to `<launchId>.<n>.jsonl` with `seq` continuing; pruning at host start to the 20 most recent launches per session and 30 days; archive deletes the session's directory; the probe's redaction (secret-looking env values, `sk-ant-*`, e-mail addresses). Alternative: one file per session across launches, rejected because a resume is a new process with its own id space and exit row. Advisory.
  - *Queue and grant storage port*: `SessionStateStore` in `src/shared/engine/session-state-store.ts` with async `load/save` for the queue (entries plus paused flag), the grant snapshot (rule strings) and the last launch spec, and `forget`; P0 ships `FileSessionStateStore` at `<userData>/engine-host/state/<appSessionId>.json` (temp file then rename) and uses it in production, which is where §4.4 (as amended 2026-09-28) places queued messages and grants; the port is async so a cross-process adapter is possible without touching the engine host. Queue entries carry the submission states `queued`, `submitting` (persisted before the `user` frame is written, `messageUuid` minted then) and `released` (the engine's `command_lifecycle` named the uuid), plus `uncertain` for a submission a crash interrupted, which recovery re-queues at the head with the queue paused and a warning notice so the human decides between re-sending and removing, never a silent drop or duplicate; a send at an idle session is a submission too, so two quick sends serialise through the queue and never both reach the engine. Alternative: the engine host writing SQLite directly, rejected because two writers on the index from two processes is C4's decision to make. Advisory (binding on the port's shape and the entry states, which C7 renders).
  - *Process table*: `EngineSessionStatus` per live process (Interfaces): process state, engine state, activity, current turn with origin, model, effort, permission mode as requested and effective with refusal, pre-plan mode, gateway (`mode`, `baseUrl`, `verified`, `autoModeServer`, `localLaneObserved`), subagent model and model overrides, engine version, file checkpointing, background tasks, grants, MCP servers with status from the latest `system/init` (a "connected MCP server" is one whose status is `connected`), held peer messages from `peer_message_hold` with a five-minute expiry, pending request count, queue length and paused flag, `keepAlive` reasons (§4.4's list, computed, not acted on under the interim rule), memory (own and tree RSS sampled every 10 s with one `ps`), journal path, host hooks, last event seq. `toSessionRecordPatch` maps it onto the X1 session record for C4's index. Binding on the field names (C11's Session info (hosted by C8a) and C12's budget read them), advisory on sampling.
  - *Conformance tests in plain Node*: the adapter's modules import no Electron; a `ReplayTransport` plays a fixture's `in` rows and advances on the adapter's matching `out` writes (recorded request ids rewritten to the minted ones), answers the host's own `initialize` and `get_status` from a checked-in handshake prelude (`test/fixtures/engine/replay-handshake.jsonl`, rows copied from `pass2/auto-routed.jsonl`) when the fixture did not record them, and ends the replay as a `crash` with a `replay_mismatch` meta row on any other unrecorded write, so a test cannot pass by advancing past a frame the probe never saw; a scripted answerer replays the probe's recorded responses; the suite replays every fixture of every pass into the X1 graph, validates each event, forbids `frame.unknown` for fixture frames, and asserts C2's findings as graph facts; a journal written by a replayed run re-normalises to a deep-equal event list, ids and `seq` included. Runs under vitest's node project in `pnpm test`. Binding (the charter's compatibility gate for engine upgrades).
  - *Live tests*: a vitest project `live` (`pnpm test:live`, `MAWS_LIVE=1`) outside `pnpm test` and `pnpm verify`; gated by the binary at the pinned path answering `--version 2.1.283` and by `GET /api/hello` → 200, otherwise skipped with a message naming the relay; every process launched with the real X6 contract (gateway, auto mode, the owner's setting sources, Sonnet, `--max-budget-usd 0.50`), one scratch git repository per case, serial execution, journals printed, the cost sum reported. Advisory.
  - *"Verified routed by `get_status`"*: after the `initialize` response and before the first user frame, `get_status` is read; the `Session` section's `Anthropic base URL` row must equal `http://127.0.0.1:8641` for `verified: 'routed'`; a direct launch is `verified: 'direct'` when that row is absent; anything else is `mismatch` and posts a warning notice; re-read after each human turn. The `Environment` section's `Auto mode server` row is the lane. Binding (the acceptance and C11's Session info depend on the mechanics).
  - *Relay health check*: `GET http://127.0.0.1:8641/api/hello` over `http.request` with a 1500 ms timeout, success iff HTTP 200, cached 5 s, run before every gateway launch and on demand (`engine.relayHealth`); an unreachable relay answers `session.launch` with `relay_unreachable` before any spawn, and the launch sheet decides. Binding on the endpoint and outcome, advisory on timeout and cache.
  - *File checkpointing in print mode*: the engine disables checkpointing in `-p` mode unless `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_SDK_FILE_CHECKPOINTING` is set (SPEC 15.12.1; the `fileCheckpointingEnabled` setting alone does not apply headless), so the X6 environment sets it to `1` and `rewind_files` becomes usable; M6 verifies a real file restore and records the fixture `pass3/rewind-files.jsonl`. The two `rewind_files` answers differ (`cli.pretty.js` 2.1.283 `vS`, around lines 731401–731416): a dry run returns `filesChanged` as the list of paths, `insertions` and `deletions`; a restore returns `skippedLinks` as a count and no statistics; `RewindFilesOutcome` converts each explicitly (`dryRun`, `changedFiles`, `insertions`, `deletions`, `skippedLinks`) and a restore's absent counts never read as checkpointing disabled; acceptance asserts the restore's `canRewind` plus the file's contents on disk. Binding (X6; C6 reads the outcome).
  - *Auto-mode lanes in the session record*: X1's `SessionRecord.gateway` gains `baseUrl` and `autoModeServer` (`enabled`/`disabled`/`unknown`), filled from `get_status`; `EngineSessionStatus.gateway.localLaneObserved` becomes true on a `safeguard_results[].status.type === 'unavailable'` (the gateway's `provider_not_anthropic` answer) or the engine's `system/informational` warning about the classifier, which also surfaces as `notice.posted` for C11's session notice. Binding (X1 additive change; C4 fills `null`/`unknown` for disk sessions).
  - *Plan outcomes on the wire*: implement = bare allow (the engine strips the injected `plan`/`planFilePath` and restores the pre-plan mode itself); keep planning = deny with the feedback as `message`; clear context and implement = deny, then after the result `set_permission_mode` to the recorded pre-plan mode, a `user` frame `/clear` (a `local` command marked non-interactive-capable; M6 verifies the `conversation_reset`), then a `user` frame `Implement this plan:` with the plan body. Fallback if `/clear` is refused headless: a fresh launch seeded with the plan, recorded as a dated line. Binding on the three outcomes (§4.2), advisory on the `/clear` mechanism.
  - *Steer*: a `user` frame written immediately with `priority: 'next'` (folds into the running turn at the next tool boundary; owner's ruling 2026-09-28), never entering the app queue; the caret menu offers "Interrupt and send", `priority: 'now'`, which aborts the running turn and runs the message as its own turn, labelled so; ordinary sends during a turn enter the app queue with no `priority`. Binding (§4.2 Steer priority).
  - *Exit policy*: every way out runs one termination ladder and the process is `exiting` until the OS reports the exit: `session.stop` and `engine.shutdown` write `end_session`, wait 3 s (`graceMs` capped at 3000), close stdin and `SIGTERM`, wait 3 s, `SIGKILL`, wait 1 s, answering within 7 s (inside the bridge deadline); a failed `initialize` enters the same ladder without the `end_session` rung and keeps the process table's reservation until the real exit, so a retry is refused `already_live` while the first engine still runs and can never become a second writer on one session; an exit nobody asked for is `crash` (non-zero or signal) or `unknown` (zero), pauses the queue and raises the broker's approvals-lost item; the engine host never relaunches on its own (D-16), and a user-initiated resume emits `session.reconnect { trigger: 'user' }`. Advisory.
  - *Session id pinning*: new sessions launch with `--session-id <uuid>` minted by the host so the journal, the state file and the session record know the engine id before the first frame; never combined with `--resume`. Advisory.
  - *Entrypoint and host hooks*: `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=sdk-ts` (the probe's identity; all fixtures were recorded under it); no host hooks are registered in production launches in P0, the registry exists and is exercised by tests. Advisory.
  - *Reload survival*: normalised events live in the engine host (a 5,000-event ring per session, the journal beyond it) and `session.events { afterSeq }` re-delivers them, so a renderer reload rebuilds its view without touching the process (§4.1). Every event derives from a journal row (the host journals `meta` rows for exit, reconnect and transport or process errors before normalising them, and the rewind boundary derives from the correlated `rewind_conversation` request and response rows), so a rebuild from the journal reproduces the live graph, ids and `seq` included, and no rewound-away history can resurface after the ring has rolled over. Binding.
  - *Id-less requests and the deny answer*: a broker item whose request carries no tool-use id (MCP `elicitation`, `request_user_dialog`; `pass2/dialogs` seq 41) cannot be confirmed by a frame, so §4.6's confirmation rule is read as applying to items that have an id and an id-less item retires (`turn_ended`) at the `result` that closes the turn in which it was delivered, the earliest frame proving the engine moved past it; the approvals-lost count stays honest for both kinds. A permission denial carries no `scope`: `RequestAnswer` requires `scope` for `allow` only, so C5's `{ kind: 'permission', decision: 'deny' }` is the schema's own shape. Binding (C5 and C7 code against the answer shape).
  - *Launch settings merge*: `--settings` is built from `extraSettings` with the host's protected keys applied last (`dialogExpiry`, the direct-launch `env` override, `effortLevel` when the spec sets effort), `env` merged one level deep so a caller's other keys survive; an override of a protected key is refused as `settings_conflict`, never silently replaced. Binding (X6).
  - *Block indices*: the engine emits one `assistant` frame per content block, before that block's `content_block_stop`, so `assistant.block.complete.blockIndex` is the stream's `content_block_start` index paired per `apiMessageId` in order, and a message with no stream events (child agents) gets indices in content order per message id; C6 keys live blocks as `<apiMessageId>:<blockIndex>` on that guarantee (verified over all 119 `assistant` frames of both passes). Binding (C6).
  - *Human prompt bubble*: the `user.message` for a human prompt and the `turn.started` that opens its turn derive from the host's own outgoing querying `user` frame (the host mints `uuid`, which C2 verified becomes the JSONL record uuid), never from the engine replaying it: most fixtures were recorded without `--replay-user-messages` and echo nothing, and where the echo exists (`pass2/bash-context-echoed` seq 16/21, same uuid, `isReplay: true`) it is deduplicated by uuid; `system/init` marks acceptance on the status's turn (`acceptedAt`) and opens a turn only for engine-initiated ones; the `!` echo stays the bash-echo receipt. Binding (C6's replay stub mirrors it and its snapshot suite proves equivalence against this normaliser).
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** in-flight since 2026-09-28 (execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c3-engine-host.md`, authored against pin 87f8c788, reconciled at 2af720f; under review).

### C4: Filesystem state, index and replay — plan

- **Purpose:** the durable side of sessions: tailers over the engine's JSONL store that follow the leaf's parent chain across rewind branches, sidecar readers with retry, discovery of sessions the app did not start and of their live holders, the SQLite index of app records, the two replay sources (JSONL backfill and raw-frame journal), and the read-only git service (status, branch, diff, log, watcher).
- **Acceptance:** with the owner's real `~/.claude`: every project and session appears with its title, cwd from the first record and holder state; a session live in a terminal is marked foreign-live within two seconds of its process starting; a rewind in the terminal shrinks the transcript in MAWS without a restart; a subagent transcript from the fixtures maps to the X1 graph; replaying a fixture journal and backfilling the same session's JSONL produce one consistent graph; the index survives restart with titles, pins and layout; git status for a dirty worktree updates on file change.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C1. blocks: C8, C10.
- **Contracts:** owns the replay engine in X8; produces X1; participates in X6 (holder detection informs launch).
- **Design inheritance:** §4.3, §4.4 index scope [binding]; `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md` items 3, 4 and 10 (workflow disk layout, rewind branching, holder behaviour) [binding as verified]; §4.7 [advisory].
- **Residue decisions (C4, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c4-filesystem-index-replay.md` carries the milestones, the store shapes, the X2 additions, the cross-child reconciliation and the record. Grades: binding where the design binds the outcome, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Process placement*: the filesystem state service (watcher, discovery, tailers, sidecar and registry readers, spill reads, backfill, replay clock and sources) runs in the engine-host utility process; the SQLite index and the git service run in main; they talk only over C1's host protocol: the host emits validated X2 `fs.*` events and C3's `session.eventBatch`, which main consumes through a new `HostSupervisor.onEvent(name, listener)` and re-emits to the renderer, and main sends `fs.*` commands as `req`, including `fs.bind`, which tells the host the `appSessionId ↔ sessionId` pairs the index allocated so every renderer-facing payload carries `appSessionId`. The heavy-work worker §4.1 names is not forked in C4: discovery reads 64 KiB heads and tails (about one second for the owner's 3,690 transcripts), tailing is incremental and the index holds records, not text; the worker arrives with its first CPU-bound consumer (transcript search or the P1 scrubber). Alternative rejected: the index in the engine host beside the service, which would put app records behind a process C3 may restart. Binding on placement (§4.1); advisory on deferring the worker.
  - *SQLite binding*: `node:sqlite` (`DatabaseSync`), built into Electron 44.4.5's Node 24.21.0 and into the Node 22 vitest runs on, replaces better-sqlite3 (S-4). Evidence: better-sqlite3 13.0.3 publishes no prebuilt binaries and 12.11.1's newest Electron prebuild is ABI 146 (Electron 41), so Electron 44 (ABI 149) would need a node-gyp rebuild plus a second build for tests; `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 electron@44.4.5 -e` shows `node:sqlite` present with SQLite 3.53.4 and FTS5; `node:sqlite` is Stability 1.2 (release candidate) from Node 24.15, the API is synchronous like better-sqlite3's, and the app uses only `exec`, `prepare`, `run`, `get`, `all` and transactions behind a one-file `Db` wrapper. Consequences: no `allowBuilds`, `asarUnpack` or `npmRebuild` change; `NODE_OPTIONS=--disable-warning=ExperimentalWarning` on the test script for Node 22. Documented fallback: better-sqlite3 with `electron-rebuild -f -w better-sqlite3`, C1's node-pty treatment, with a dated line here if it ever becomes the default. Advisory.
  - *Watcher mechanism*: Node's `fs.watch` with `recursive: true` (FSEvents on macOS, one stream per root) over `projects/`, `jobs/`, `tasks/` and a flat watch over `sessions/`; events classified by relative path, coalesced per key with a 50 ms trailing debounce; a null filename or watcher error triggers a readdir-diff rescan of that root and re-opens the watch after 1 s; a 30 s reconcile timer stats followed files and a 2 s poll checks holder pids, as the floor under coalesced or dropped FSEvents. chokidar rejected: its add/change/unlink classification and write-finish heuristics are not needed by stat-and-offset readers, D-11 removes the cross-platform reason for it, and chokidar 5 is a second module-format question in a CommonJS host. Binding on "one watcher plus tailers" (§4.3); advisory on the API.
  - *Tail strategy and partial lines*: per followed file a read cursor (the byte position up to which the file has been read) and a partial-line buffer holding the bytes after the last `\n`; on change, `fstat` then read `[cursor, size)` exactly once, so the ranges read are contiguous and disjoint, no byte is read twice and a fragment is never prepended to its own re-read; `size < cursor` or an inode change means the file was rewritten (GC, tombstone removal, hydration, relocation) and triggers a full reload through the same line dispatch (a trailing `rewound` anchor keeps its cut) with uuid re-anchoring; lines are split at `\n`, leading NULs stripped, unparseable lines skipped and counted (the engine's own reader rule), a torn fragment sealed by the writer's `\n` prefix parses as garbage exactly once; the follower keeps a uuid skeleton (parent, type, offset, length) and never the payloads, re-reading records by offset when a branch flip brings them onto the chain; a `rewound: true` anchor cuts the chain at its `leafUuid` immediately, emits `boundary.rewind` and leaves the follower announced until the continuation, whose parent is the last context attachment the engine injected after that leaf rather than the leaf itself (verified on the pass-2 transcript: anchor line 27, continuation line 30, three records cut) and which rejoins without a second boundary; a branch without an anchor, or one an announced rewind cannot explain, emits `boundary.retraction { reason: 'transcript_branch' }` and a warning notice (the second-writer case of pass-2 item 10). Disk-derived events carry `seq: null` and deterministic ids (`<uuid>`, `<uuid>#<n>`, `anchor:<offset>`, `session:<id>:started`); consumers dedup by id, never by `seq`. Binding as the execution of §4.3's re-anchor rule.
  - *Sidecar write-atomicity retry*: whole-file JSON sidecars (`custom-title.json`, `agent-*.meta.json`, `wf_*.json`, `tasks/*/*.json`, `sessions/*.json`, `jobs/*/state.json`) are read and parsed at 0, 20, 50, 100, 250 and 500 ms after a change until they parse and pass an acceptance predicate (`sessionId` present for registry records, which the engine patches by read-modify-write); after six failures the previous value is kept and the next change restarts the schedule. Reason: the SPEC documents bare `writeFile` for the registry and no atomic rename for the others. Binding (§4.3 "parsed with retry").
  - *Holder detection*: a holder is a `sessions/<pid>.json` with a `sessionId` whose pid is alive (`kill(pid, 0)`, EPERM counts) and whose `procStart`, when present, equals the trimmed output of `ps -o lstart=` run under `LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC`, the engine's own probe environment, so the token is a C-locale UTC timestamp on every host (verified against all ten real registry records on the owner's `America/Los_Angeles` Mac; the local-time form matches none); `interactive` maps to X1 `terminal`, `bg` to `background_job` with `jobId`, `daemon` and `daemon-worker` to `background_job` with no `jobId`; spares and parked records are not holders; `jobs/<short>/state.json` contributes name and state but never liveness; `-p` processes were believed to write no record; corrected 2026-09-29 by C10's M6 on the pinned 2.1.283: every `-p` launch writes a registry record, MAWS's own included, so occupancy counts a MAWS-run session once (its own record is not a foreign holder) and MAWS's own sessions are `self` by C3's hand, not the registry's. Binding as verified (pass-2 item 10, SPEC 35.27.1).
  - *Index schema and location*: `<userData>/index.sqlite` (C1's `MAWS_USER_DATA` honoured), WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`; migrations as an ordered array with `PRAGMA user_version`, v1 the five tables `projects`, `sessions` (the X1 record plus C3's `gateway` extension and the discovery facts behind it; titles as engine facts `custom_title`, `ai_title`, `first_prompt` overwritten by every summary beside a MAWS-only `title_override` that a later terminal rename supersedes, so terminal renames and regenerated AI titles always reach the record), `attention_items`, `drafts`, `replay_fixtures`; a newer-than-known version opens read-only, an unopenable file is set aside as `index.sqlite.broken-<epoch>`. Sessions removed from disk by the engine's retention sweep stay in the index as archived records. Queued messages and grants are persisted by C3's per-session state file and tab and panel layout by C5's `layout.json`, so neither is a table here (§4.4 as amended 2026-09-28). Binding on the scope (§4.4 as amended); advisory on the columns.
  - *Project membership*: a session joins the project whose directory equals the realpath of its effective directory (`relocatedCwd` else first `cwd`) or contains it under `.claude/worktrees/`; discovery auto-creates projects (`origin: 'discovered'`) for directories that exist and have a session with messages; a removed project is hidden, not deleted; sessions of vanished directories go to "Other sessions"; C5's `ProjectRecord` is the row projected. Advisory (C5 owns the visible grouping).
  - *X2 surface*: `fs.*` (owner engine-host): `state`, `scan`, `bind`, `unfollow`, `subagents`, `agentEvents`, `workflows`, `tasks`, `readSpill` (the last two names are C6's); events `sessionChanged`, `sessionRemoved`, `holderChanged`, `followState`, `subagentsChanged`, `workflowChanged`, `tasksChanged`, `scanProgress`. `session.*` (owner main): the record side `list`, `get`, `update`, `projects.{list,add,update,remove}`, `drafts.{get,set}`, `replay.{start,fixtures}`; `replay.{launch,control}` (owner engine-host; `launch` carries the ids, source and speed from main's `start` and fails before any event, main rolling the record back); events `changed`, `removed`, `projects.changed`, `replay.progress`. C4 emits C3's `session.eventBatch` for backfill, tail and replay, answers C3's `session.events` for non-live app sessions through the `FsService.eventsSnapshot` seam, and applies C3's `session.status` to the record. C5's read model is fed through the `SessionSource` port (`observeProject`, `observeRecord`, `observeRemoved`, `setDigest`; `onSetVisible` back), and C5's `session.setVisible`, `session.digests`, `session.digest` stay C5's. No `attention.*` or `layout.*` entries; `Index.attention` implements C3's `AttentionStore` port. `git.{status,branches,diff,log,watch,unwatch}`; event `git.changed` carrying the fresh status. `fs.readSpill` refuses paths outside `projects/`, `tasks/`, `jobs/` under the config home. Binding once landed (X2); the `session.projects.*` names and the record side are settled with C5 in the wave-2 ledger.
  - *Backfill of mid-turn prompts and `/compact` (W1, 2026-10-01, landed c22771b7)*: a human `queued_command` attachment in a main transcript (the only record a delivered `next` steer or a terminal-queued prompt leaves; the live steer writes no user record of its own, `w1-steer-next-disk.jsonl`) maps to the prompt's `user.message` under `source_uuid` and a `command.lifecycle started`, so a reloaded session folds the steer where the live stream did; `isCompactSummary` is read as meta (never a bubble); a command's `<local-command-stdout>` stays in the command's turn; a compaction's post-token count is kept.
  - *Project membership (W5, 2026-10-01, landed d6ddf6d6)*: a linked git worktree outside `<main>/.claude/worktrees/` folds under its main checkout by git common dir, on content evidence (a `gitdir` file naming the main's `.git/worktrees/<name>`), never on the name alone; a start-up pass off main's start path moves records filed under the old rule (measured on a copy of the owner's index: 599 sessions moved, 297 visible projects became 99, idempotent, about 1.8 s in the background for 200 worktrees); the remaining separate-git-dir edge is tracked debt.
  - *Git invocation and watcher*: `/usr/bin/git` (or `MAWS_GIT`) via `execFile` with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `LC_ALL=C`, 10 s timeout; `status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z`, `for-each-ref`, `diff` with `--numstat`, `log --format … -z`; one recursive `fs.watch` per repository root (plus the git dir of a linked worktree), `.git/` events counted only for `HEAD`, `ORIG_HEAD`, `MERGE_HEAD`, `index`, `packed-refs`, `refs/**`, `objects/**` and `*.lock` ignored, 250 ms trailing debounce, one status in flight per root, `git.changed` only when the status differs. Binding on read-only (§4.5); advisory on mechanism.
  - *Replay clock and speed*: absolute-deadline scheduling from a re-basable origin (`deadline = base + Σ min(Δt, maxGap·speed)/speed`), speeds 0.25× to 50× and `'instant'` (batches of 500), recorded gaps longer than 2 s shortened to 2 s at speed 1, pause/resume/speed change re-base at the current row, each run under a fresh `rp_<uuid>` session id and a fresh app id with the fixture's ids rewritten so concurrent replays never collide, started by main's `session.replay.start` through the host-side `session.replay.launch`, which loads and validates the source and binds the ids before the first event, a failure leaving no record and no event; the journal runner hands both directions to C3's `Normalizer` through a `RowNormalizer` interface (a stand-in until C3 lands), the transcript runner paces backfill events by record timestamp. C3's `ReplayTransport` is a separate lockstep test seam. Binding on the two-source rule (X8); advisory on the clock.
  - *Acceptance against the real store*: a negative control runs first against an immutable APFS clone of `projects/`, `sessions/`, `jobs/` and `tasks/` (`cp -Rc`, then `chflags -R uchg`: every Node write path fails with `EPERM`, reads and `fs.watch` work, no root needed) under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, failing on any `EPERM`/`EACCES`/`EROFS` in the app's output; the real-store run then uses the owner's `~/.claude` with a throwaway `MAWS_USER_DATA` for its first pass and attributes every changed path under the four roots to a live engine session (the engine writes during acceptance and `projects/` is git-ignored there, so counts and `git status` prove nothing); a guard test fails on any write API imported under `src/engine-host/fs/**`; `sandbox-exec` around the app was rejected because Chromium's sandbox will not initialise inside an outer profile; unit tests run in plain Node under vitest against a temporary `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` tree built by `test/support/store-fixture.ts` from copied fixtures (`test/fixtures/transcripts/{rewind-real,subagent-parent,torn-tail}.jsonl`, the probe sidecars and workflow-disk directory), never against the owner's files. Binding on read-only (§4.3, D-12); advisory on the proof mechanics.
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** in-flight since 2026-09-28 (execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c4-filesystem-index-replay.md`, authored against pin 87f8c788, reconciled at 2af720f; under review).

### C5: Shell — plan

- **Purpose:** the window the owner lives in: three regions with persisted sizes and accessible resizers, the sidebar (search, Needs-you, pinned, project tree with status glyphs, other sessions, hover cards, priority and recency sort, actions), the Overview board, the tab strip with the single pane and optional split, layout persistence, the keyboard map, OS notifications, the next-needs-you shortcut, quit coordination, theme switching.
- **Acceptance:** driven by fixtures and then by C3 and C4: the sidebar and Overview show nine sessions across three projects with correct glyphs and Needs-you ordering; a hover card previews a read-only tool request and approves by click only; a notification fires for a session entering Needs-you while unfocused and ⌘⇧J opens it; tabs reorder, split and collapse as specified; layout survives restart; foreign-live sessions show holder and no controls; quitting with a running turn asks; every mockup 01 state renders.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C1. blocks: C8, C10.
- **Contracts:** consumes X1, X2, X4, X7; owns the Overview card layout.
- **Design inheritance:** §5.1, §5.2, §5.7, §5.8 [layouts advisory, attention model and split-focus rule binding]; mockups 01 and 04 vignette 1 [advisory].
- **Residue decisions (C5, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c5-shell.md` carries the milestones and the concrete X2 read-side, `attention.*` and `layout.*` definitions. Grades: binding where the design binds the outcome or where siblings code against the name, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Stores and events*: Zustand stores `sessions` (C4's records and projects plus C5's digests and C3's engine statuses; one file, merged at landing), `attention` (items per session, notification delivery), `layout` (the four layout keys plus transient drag and focus), `notices` (banners and toasts), `usage` (windows, filled by C11), and C1's `settings` extended with `keymap` and `notifications`; all fed by X2 events through one subscription module that subscribes before invoking the snapshot commands and keeps the higher revision (C1's rule) where the payload carries one; `attention.list` and `attention.changed` carry none by agreement, so the attention store buffers the changes that arrive while its snapshot is outstanding and replays each per-session replacement (empty ones included) over the snapshot, rather than adding a revision to a settled payload. C5's X2 additions are small because C3 and C4 already own the rest: `session.digests` and `session.setVisible` (owner main), event `session.digest`; events `attention.notified`, `attention.navigate`; `layout.get`, `layout.set`, `layout.list`, `layout.openExternal` (owner main), event `layout.action`, and the value shapes of the layout keys. Consumed as siblings defined them: C3's `session.answer`, `session.status`, `session.eventBatch`; C4's `session.list`, `session.get`, `session.update`, `session.projects.*`, `session.changed`, `session.removed`, `session.projects.changed`; the main-owned `attention.list`, `attention.changed`, `attention.request` that C3's attention service answers. Alternative rejected: a parallel C5 record store, which C4's index makes redundant. Binding on the names and payloads (siblings code against them), advisory on the store split.
  - *Session digest*: the live facts a row, card and status line show that X1's record does not carry (phase, activity, last assistant line, context, cost total and since last seen, child-agent counts, pending count) are a `SessionDigest` folded in main's read model from C3's event batches and statuses by a pure reducer over the parent conversation only (an event with `agentId` or `parentToolUseId` set is child traffic and feeds only the pending and child-agent counts, so forwarded child messages and C4's sidecar backfill never set a working parent idle; echo receipts are not turns; events are deduplicated by id), emitted as `session.digest` and snapshotted by `session.digests`; the stub scripts the same shape. Reason: the sidebar must describe sessions nobody has opened, so the fold cannot live in a mounted transcript; main sees every batch anyway (re-emit path) and the notifier and quit guard need the same facts. Alternative rejected: reading C6's transcript store for every live session, which would run the row derivation for sessions with no visible transcript. Binding on the shape and the `phase` vocabulary (`starting`, `working`, `waiting`, `idle`, `exited`, `failed`, `foreign`), advisory on the reducer's activity copy.
  - *Read model and stub source*: main holds a `SessionReadModel` that folds the digest of every live session from the event batches and C3's statuses on the re-emit path, observes C4's record events and the attention events the same way, keeps the visible set, and answers `session.digests`; the notifier, dock badge and quit guard read it, so main has a view without a renderer. Sources write into it through `SessionSource`: C5's stub source (`MAWS_SESSION_SOURCE=stub:<scenario>`, dev and E2E only), which also fills an in-memory stub store answering C4's `session.list/get/update/projects.list`. The stub is selected by launch mode, decided once at app ready (`select-source.ts`: `scenario` when `MAWS_SESSION_SOURCE` is `stub:<path>` and the app is unpackaged or `MAWS_E2E=1`, else `index`): in scenario mode the read model refuses to start any other source, so C4's `IndexSessionSource` never issues `fs.scan` and discovery never runs, the stub store shadows the index's four record-side handlers through `interceptCommand`, and `dev:stub` uses its own `MAWS_USER_DATA`; the stub store is permanent, the same smoke runs against it on the rebased branch with C4's index present, and C4's block in `src/main/index.ts` is untouched (the `layout.json` store is C5's product store, not a shim). Scenario files under `test/fixtures/shell/` are fixture-derived (ids, directories, request payloads copied from the probe journals, synthetic parts marked) and validated with the X1 schemas; `nine-sessions.json` is the acceptance scenario, `empty.json` and `many-sessions.json` the state scenarios. The stub intercepts, for its own items only, `session.answer`, `attention.list` and `attention.request` through a router seam (`interceptCommand`) that a production launch never installs. Alternative rejected: a renderer-only fake store, which would leave the IPC path and the main-side consumers untested until C3 and C4. Binding on the source interface and the scenario format, advisory on the shims and the interception mechanism.
  - *No router*: there is no URL router. One window, one tab model already held in the layout store; deep links (sidebar row → Overview card, notification → session tab) are store actions carried by `attention.navigate` and `layout.action` events. Alternative rejected: react-router with tab state in the URL, which adds a second source of truth for state that must persist across restarts anyway. Advisory.
  - *Unread*: C4's index owns `record.unread` under its rule (`lastRecordAt` advances for a session whose tab is not visible; the engine appends the JSONL for MAWS-owned and foreign sessions alike); C5 supplies the missing input, visibility, through `session.setVisible` on every change of active tab, window focus and document visibility, forwarded by the read model to the source's `onSetVisible`, and the index updates `lastSeen` for a visible session in the same step as the advance, so the dot never flickers; "Mark all read" is `session.update { unread: false, lastSeen }` per session. This is §5.7's definition made mechanical. Alternative rejected: the renderer marking seen after each digest change, which races the turn end. Binding (§5.7).
  - *Needs-you ordering and grouping*: one row per session, showing its oldest pending or delivered item; actionable kinds (permission, question, plan, dialog, elicitation, auto-mode unavailable, error, interrupted) form tier 1 ordered by wait time, then held peer messages (informational, expiring) as tier 2; the count badge shows further items; ⌘⇧J goes to the first row that is not the current session, wrapping. The Overview's Needs-you group uses the same order; project groups follow the sidebar's project order and exclude sessions already in Needs you. Reason: §5.1 orders by wait time and §5.7 sends ⌘⇧J to the oldest item, and an expiring informational row must not outrank a question. Binding on one-row-per-session and wait-time order (§5.1, §5.7), advisory on the tier split.
  - *Glyphs*: the status vocabulary of §5.1 as a precedence table (foreign-live, question, plan, error, approval, working, failed, done, idle unread, idle) with tokens for tone and an `aria-label` per row (execution document, Derivations). Colour is never the only signal: the glyph character differs per state. Advisory on the characters, binding on "one derivation for sidebar, tab, card" (§5.7).
  - *Sidebar scale*: no virtualisation; a project renders at most 20 sessions in the chosen sort plus "Show N more", collapsed projects render nothing, archived sessions are hidden; `many-sessions.json` (360 sessions) is the scale check. Tree expansion state (`collapsedProjects`, `collapsedSections`) and the sort mode persist in the `regions` layout key; Needs you is never collapsible. Alternative rejected: TanStack Virtual in the tree, which complicates roving focus and drag for a list that is bounded by disclosure anyway. Advisory. Amended 2026-10-01 (W5): a drilled-in project page renders all of its sessions in a scrolling list (no 20-row cap, no "Show N more"); `collapsedProjects` and `collapsedSections` give way to the open project id in the `regions` layout key; `many-sessions.json` stays the scale check.
  - *Hover card*: Radix `HoverCard` on the Needs-you row (opens on hover after 300 ms and on focus) rendering C7's X4 slot with `placement: 'hover'` and the originating request fetched through `attention.request`; C5's fallback previews the full input and offers Approve and Deny only for `permission` items whose tool is in the shared `READ_ONLY_TOOLS` list, answered through `session.answer` with `scope: 'once'` or a plain deny; Enter on the focused row never answers. Mockup 01 shows a `git push` approved from the hover card; the charter's §5.1 rule (read-only tools only) wins and the mockup is followed for structure only. Binding (§5.1 safety rule and X4 placement), advisory on the read-only list. Amended 2026-10-01 (W5): the Needs-you row is gone with its section; the same HoverCard (300ms, focus too) moves to every session row, showing branch and worktree, the last assistant line, the pending request with the read-only approve rule unchanged, and age.
  - *Tab strip*: custom `role="tablist"` with roving focus; Overview permanent and first; deterministic tab ids so a session has one tab across panes; reorder by native HTML drag and drop (`application/x-maws-tab`), sidebar rows drag with `application/x-maws-session`; middle-click and ⌘W close; ⌘⇧T reopens from a ten-entry history; pinned tabs sort first and survive "close others"; overflow scrolls horizontally with the active tab kept in view. Tab kinds: overview, session, agent (a child transcript in the main area, C6's child variant), file, diff, diagnostics. Alternative rejected: dnd-kit, which does not do cross-component native drags and adds a dependency for three drags. Advisory (§5.2 layouts), binding on "Overview first and permanent" (§5.2) and the single-tab-per-session rule (it is what makes ⌘1..9 and Needs-you navigation unambiguous). Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W5): the Overview is no longer a tab, so "Overview first and permanent" is retired; `closeTab('overview')`, the forced index 0 and the hydrate re-insert go; the single-tab-per-session rule stands.
  - *Split*: at most two panes (D-8); drag a tab to the right edge (a drop zone over the rightmost 15 % while dragging) or ⌘\ to create the second pane with that tab; ⌘\ with a split moves the active tab across; closing or moving the last tab out of a pane collapses the split; `splitRatio` 0.25–0.75 persisted; the focused pane is set by pointer or focus inside it and is the target of every pane-scoped action. Binding on the pane count and collapse rule (D-8, §5.2) and on pane focus as the scope of Enter and Esc (§5.7), advisory on the gesture.
  - *Resizers*: one `Resizer` component, `role="separator"` with `aria-valuenow/min/max`, pointer drag with capture, arrow keys 8 px, Shift 32 px, Home and End to the bounds, Enter or double-click to the token default; sidebar 200–480 px, panel 280–640 px, a drag 40 px past the minimum collapses the region; the same component in horizontal mode is the split divider. No layout library: the regions are a CSS grid on the X7 width tokens and a flex-based panel library would fight it for two handles. Alternative rejected: react-resizable-panels. Advisory.
  - *Window state and layout persistence*: window bounds, maximized and fullscreen persist under the main-only layout key `window.main`, restored only when intersecting a current display, else 1280×820 centred; minimum 960×600; `trafficLightPosition { x: 16, y: 15 }` for the 44 px toolbar. The renderer writes each changed layout key through `layout.set` 250 ms after the last change and on `beforeunload`, and validates every key it reads against C5's shapes with a default on failure. Advisory.
  - *Right-panel region*: C5 owns the region, its width, collapse and maximize (main hidden, panel takes the width), the header strip, and the persistence of panel tabs as `PanelTabRef { id, toolId, pinned }` split into the layout keys `panel.pinned` and `panel.<appSessionId>` (D-10: a pinned tab is shown for every session, first); the region renders the `panelHost` slot with `PanelHostProps` (`panel/host-slot.ts`); C8 fills it and reads C9's `panel/tool.ts` for tools. Flagged as the X5 overlap: C8 adopts or adapts at those two files. Binding on the pin split (D-10), advisory on the props.
  - *Overview card*: title row (glyph, title, project when in the Needs-you group, branch chip with a dirty marker once C4's git service reports it), an activity line (the activity while working, else the state in words) and a separate last-answer line whenever the digest has one, so a working session shows both what it is doing and what it last said (§5.2 lists the two), the X4 slot with `placement: 'card'` when a request is pending, footer `ctx N%` · `$N since seen` · `N agents`; foreign-live cards show the holder and no controls; click opens the session tab, ⌘-click in the split, arrow keys move focus, Enter opens (or is the card's answer key once C7's card is present, bound to the focused card by DOM ownership), and a sidebar Needs-you row focuses its card. Cards exist for sessions that are live (a live process in any phase, read idle included: §5.2's board is live or unread), unread, or in Needs you; exited read sessions have none. Advisory (C5 owns the card layout per §12).
  - *Status line container*: rendered by the shell under the composer slot with the six fields of §5.5 in order, from the record (cwd, effective permission mode with the refusal code in words), the digest (context, cost) and the `usage` store (5h and 7d windows), with `—` placeholders where data is absent; each field is a button whose popover C7 supplies; the near-limit banner is not here but in the banner host. Binding on the fields and their sources (§5.5), advisory on layout.
  - *Banner placement*: a banner host between the tab strips and the panes, spanning the main region, holds account-wide and app-wide banners (C11's near-limit banner once per window); transient notices are toasts at the main region's bottom right. Binding (§5.5 "once per window"), advisory on position.
  - *Keyboard map*: one action table (`src/shared/keymap/actions.ts`) with the §5.8 defaults in Electron accelerator syntax and a sink per action: `menu` actions are registered by the macOS application menu (main) and dispatched as `layout.action`; `renderer` actions are matched by the renderer's dispatcher, which also enforces the focus rules (a pane-scoped handler fires only when the element it registered with actually contains DOM focus and the key's target, the innermost such handler winning, so a remembered pane index or the last-registered card can never answer for another; Enter and Esc never from a button, any text-entry element whatever its content, an element under `data-key-sink`, an open menu or dialog, or during IME composition). The split is forced by Electron: `registerAccelerator: false` is Windows and Linux only, so a chord shown in the macOS menu is always registered there. Overrides live in `settings.keymap` (null unbinds); the menu rebuilds on change; the table also feeds the palette and tooltips; C11/C12 add the editing screen. Alternative rejected: a global shortcut registry (`globalShortcut`), which fires even when MAWS is not the active app. Binding on the table as the single source and the focus rules (§5.7, §5.8 "all bindings editable"), advisory on the defaults.
  - *Command palette*: ⌘K opens one palette (cmdk in a Radix Dialog) over sessions, projects and the action table; the sidebar search field filters the tree in place with the same index. The mockup labels the sidebar search ⌘K; the charter's §5.8 gives ⌘K to the command palette, and one palette that searches sessions satisfies both. The slash palette is C7's and separate. Advisory.
  - *Notifications*: main's notifier posts one macOS notification per attention item that enters `pending` with an actionable kind while the window is unfocused (or absent) and `settings.notifications.enabled`; click focuses the window and navigates; the dock badge is the tier-1 Needs-you count at all times. macOS delivers notifications only from a code-signed binary (verified against the Electron 44 documentation: unsigned binaries emit `failed`), so dev runs (signed Electron) notify natively and the unsigned C1 build falls back to a dock bounce plus an in-app toast until C12 signs; the fallback is chosen on the first `failed` and reported through `attention.notified { delivery }`, which is also what the smoke observes. Permission flow: Electron has no request API in main; macOS asks on first delivery, and a user denial shows as `failed` too, handled by the same fallback; the setting and the observed delivery state are shown by C11's settings screen. Binding on "notification on entering Needs-you while unfocused" (§3, §5.7), advisory on the fallback and the badge.
  - *Quit coordination*: on `before-quit`, sessions mid-turn are counted from C3's `session.status` kept in the read model (a process in `starting`, `initializing` or `ready` with a turn open, or still starting), never from the folded digest, which a stale batch or child backfill can skew; a status exists only for a process C3's host owns, so holder `self` is implied. When non-zero a native dialog asks `N sessions mid-turn` with Quit and Cancel; Quit calls C3's `engine.shutdown { graceMs: 5000 }` then quits. Under `MAWS_E2E=1` the answer comes from `MAWS_E2E_QUIT_ANSWER` and is logged. Binding (D-16), advisory on copy.
  - *Empty and loading states*: skeleton rows until both snapshots have loaded; an empty workspace reads "No projects yet" with the two creation actions (disabled until C10); an empty Needs you keeps its heading with "Nothing needs you right now"; an empty Overview reads "Nothing live or unread" with a new-session action; a foreign-live session pane shows the holder notice in place of the composer. Advisory.
  - *Accessibility*: Radix primitives (`radix-ui` unified package) for menus, context menus, hover card, tooltips, dialog and toasts; a custom `role="tree"` sidebar and `role="tablist"` strip with roving tabindex; resizers as ARIA separators; every glyph labelled; focus visible through the `--accent` outline; `prefers-reduced-motion` disables the working pulse; hover actions also reveal on focus-within. Advisory on primitives, binding on keyboard operability of resizers and tabs (§3 "accessible resizers").
  - *Slots*: the shell renders later children's components through a registry (`registerSlot`/`useSlot`) with a tokens-only fallback per slot (`transcript`, `composer`, `requestCard`, `panelHost`, `fileTab`, `diffTab`, `newSession`) and an action-handler registry that renders unregistered actions disabled (rename, fork, archive, add project, new session until C10). Reason: five children land into this window on separate branches; a fixed prop contract per slot is the merge seam. Binding on the slot names and props (C6, C7, C8, C10 code against them), advisory on the fallbacks.
  - *Testing*: vitest happy-dom for stores, derivations, the keymap, the layout transition table and component behaviour (Testing Library with user-event); vitest node for the read model, stub source, notifier (Electron mocked), quit guard, layout and settings migrations, window state and menu; one Playwright spec over the built app with the stub scenario on a manual clock (`MAWS_SCENARIO_CLOCK=manual`, advanced from the test through `electronApp.evaluate`, so no timed transition races the test), driving menu-sink actions by menu item id (Playwright's keyboard never reaches a macOS menu accelerator) and renderer-sink chords by keyboard, observing notifications through `attention.notified` after the blur, checking both themes by computed token colours, persistence by relaunch and the quit guard by its log line, whose count is the one the driven scenario state implies; the spec runs on the rebased branch with C3 and C4 present. Advisory.
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** in-flight since 2026-09-28 (execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c5-shell.md`, authored against pin 87f8c788, reconciled at 2af720f; under review).

### C6: Transcript — plan

- **Purpose:** the presentation model (X3) and its renderer: streaming markdown with math, diagrams and code; user and assistant turns with answer-first work disclosure; activity groups, cells and detail; change receipts; subagent lifecycle and message rows; thinking; plan cards with the three outcomes; answered questions as history; errors and reconnects; boundary rows; time separators; virtualization with anchored reading position. Its first gate is the anchoring spike.
- **Acceptance:** G1 (anchoring spike, required, first): a synthetic five-hundred-turn transcript streams while old work is expanded without moving the reader, at sixty frames per second on the owner's machine. G2: every element of mockup 02 renders from fixture frames; the group state machine matches the classification in X3; a denied action is visible while its group is collapsed and the divider's count jumps to it; a receipt's Undo calls the rewind interface; replayed bash rows render from their tags.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C1. blocks: C7, C8.
- **Contracts:** owns X3; consumes X1, X7; exposes the renderer for C8's drill-in.
- **Design inheritance:** §5.3 [binding for the element list and the group semantics, advisory for visual details]; `research/codex-thread-ux.md` §2 [advisory]; mockup 02 [advisory].
- **Residue decisions (C6, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c6-transcript.md` carries the milestones and the concrete X3 definitions. Grades: binding where the design binds the outcome, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Derivation architecture*: an incremental reducer (`applyEvents`) that keeps one `TurnState` per turn and re-derives rows per turn on change, with rows memoised per turn version, the first delta of a new block bumping the version once (a live row appears) and every later delta written to a live buffer that only the live row subscribes to; the flat row list for the virtualiser is the concatenation of per-turn row arrays. Reason: a five-hundred-turn transcript receives up to sixty delta batches a second, and only the open turn may pay for them; retraction, supersession and rewind need "unapply", which a per-turn rebuild from retained events gives for free. Alternative: memoised selectors over the whole event array, rejected because every selector is O(events) per invalidation and the memo keys would have to reinvent turn boundaries. Binding on stable ids and per-turn re-derivation (C7 and C8 key on X3 ids); advisory on the store mechanism (Zustand, per C1's *State wiring*).
  - *Row identity*: ids are deterministic functions of X1 identifiers (`user:<messageUuid>`, `text:<apiMessageId>:<blockIndex>`, `cell:<toolUseId>`, `group:<firstCellId>`, `divider:<turnId>`, `subagent:<taskId>`, `boundary:<eventId>`, …; the full table is in the execution document), never positions; text and thinking rows key on the block identity `<apiMessageId>:<blockIndex>`, which a live delta already carries, so a row's key survives its block's completion. Reason: TanStack's end anchoring and prepend stability require stable keys, and a re-derived turn must produce the same ids so expansion state and scroll anchors survive. Alternative: index keys, rejected (every prepend and every collapse renumbers). Binding (X3).
  - *Tool classification*: the table in the execution document maps tool names to the six categories of X3 plus a catch-all `other` that is phrased only when it is the sole category, with three presentations (grouped, grouped-but-hidden for bookkeeping tools such as `TodoWrite` and `Task*`, standalone), overrides in order (denial, human decision, image result), and a group-start rule (first groupable cell after the turn start, a standalone row, a commentary text block, a subagent row or a boundary; thinking cells never start a group). `Agent`, `Task` and `Workflow` calls are not cells: their lifecycle events are the subagent row. Hooks from settings and plugins are command cells inside a turn and are not rendered outside one. Reason: the function must be total over MCP, plugin and future tools, and a category for the unknown keeps the six-bucket order intact. Alternative: dropping unknown tools from the summary, rejected because it hides work. Binding (X3).
  - *Denials, interruptions and the safeguard verdict*: every `DENIAL_KINDS` member has a disposition: `denied` (`user-rejected` outside an aborted turn, `permission-rule`), `auto_denied` (`automode-blocked`, `automode-parsing-error`) or `stopped` (`user-rejected` inside a turn whose terminal reason is an abort, the interruption of pass-2 item 7; `interrupted`; `cancelled`; `automode-unavailable`, which is X1's Needs-you kind and never a denied cell). A `denied` or `auto_denied` cell is standalone whatever its group; the divider counts `denied` and `auto-denied` separately, each count linking to the first such cell, expanding the work area if collapsed and moving focus; a `stopped` cell renders "stopped" and is counted in neither. The safeguard verdict appears as one line in cell detail and as one segment of the turn summary popover, never on the cell line. Reason: §19, §13 X1 (`automode-blocked` informational, `automode-unavailable` Needs-you) and pass-2 item 7. Binding.
  - *Copy*: the divider states (`Working`, `Working for {t}`, `Worked for {t} · {n} tool calls`, `Continued for {t} …` for engine-initiated turns, `You stopped after {t} …`, `Failed after {t} …`, ` · {d} denied`, ` · {a} auto-denied`), the group phrases per category, the cell lines, the subagent sentences, the boundary and error lines are fixed in the execution document's copy table. Reason: copy that lives in one table can be reviewed and translated; the count semantics are what §5.3 binds. Alternative: Codex's strings verbatim, adapted where the engine differs (no "review" concept, denial kinds). Binding for what the counts mean; advisory for the words.
  - *Streamdown configuration*: Streamdown 2.6.0 with `@streamdown/math` 1.0.2 and `@streamdown/mermaid` 1.0.2 (code highlighting is our own plugin, residue *Code highlighting*), `mode` `streaming` for the live block and `static` for completed ones, raw HTML removed by our own remark stage before `remark-rehype` and `rehypePlugins` passed without Streamdown's default `rehype-raw` (behind that default its `skipHtml` removes nothing, and its exported `defaultUrlTransform` is the identity; both verified in a scratch install on 2026-09-28), rehype-sanitize with Streamdown's schema plus our two chip schemes, rehype-harden as the coarse gate and our own `urlTransform` as the exact allowlist (links: `https:`, `http:`, `mailto:`, `maws-path:` for path chips, `maws-image:` for image chips; images: `data:image/` only; every other URL, `javascript:` and `file:` included, loses its attribute), gated in both modes with raw-HTML, `javascript:` and `file:` inputs, `linkSafety` off with our own `a` and `img` components routing clicks to C5's open-external and open-file commands and rendering non-data images as chips, copy controls only (download and fullscreen need main-process dialogs), no line numbers, code blocks bounded at 480 px and tables at 320 px with internal scroll.
