Streamdown's dual theme rides on Tailwind's `dark:` variant and on shadcn token names, both absent after C1's palette reset, so `app.css` gains `@custom-variant dark` keyed on `[data-theme="dark"]`, the `@source` lines for the four packages, and a token bridge (`--color-background: var(--bg)`, `--color-muted: var(--bg-raised)`, `--color-muted-foreground: var(--fg-muted)`, `--color-primary: var(--accent)`, `--radius-xl`, `--text-sm`, `--text-xs`) so every colour Streamdown paints is an X7 token. Reason: the package was verified current (modified 2026-08-24) and designed for unterminated markdown; with our gates in front of its sanitiser and C1's CSP behind, model output can load or execute nothing. Alternative: react-markdown with our own streaming repair, rejected as work Streamdown already does. Advisory (§4.7) except the sanitisation posture (no raw HTML, nothing loaded by path), which follows §4.1 and is binding.
  - *Code highlighting*: Shiki through our own implementation of Streamdown's `CodeHighlighterPlugin` interface (`view/markdown/code-plugin.ts`, `shiki` as a direct dependency in the range `@streamdown/code` declares) on the main thread with its JavaScript regex engine, grammars lazy per language, tokens cached by full content with a collision regression test, themes `github-light`/`github-dark` (the container chrome is ours, tokens only). `@streamdown/code` 1.1.1, the latest published, is not used: its cache key is the language, themes, length and first and last hundred characters, so two equal-length blocks differing in the middle render one another's tokens (reproduced 2026-09-28), and no wrapper can reach that cache. Promote-to-worker criterion: if the spike or the acceptance run records a long task over 50 ms attributable to highlighting a block under 500 lines, move highlighting to a worker over `@shikijs/stream` and record it here. Reason: the plugin is forty lines against an exported interface, the cache and lazy grammars keep the cost at a few milliseconds per rendered block (virtualised), and a worker adds a message round trip and a second bundle for a cost nobody has measured. Advisory.
  - *Mermaid*: the `@streamdown/mermaid` module is imported dynamically the first time a completed `mermaid` fence appears, so mermaid's bundle (about 2.5 MB) never loads for sessions without diagrams; fences render as code until then and while streaming. Config: `securityLevel: 'strict'`, `suppressErrorRendering: true`, theme `base` with `themeVariables` read from the computed X7 tokens and rebuilt on theme change; no iframe (strict mode plus C1's CSP is the isolation). Reason: the plugin imports mermaid statically, so laziness has to happen at the plugin boundary. Advisory.
  - *Math*: `@streamdown/math` with `$$` delimiters only (single-dollar off, as currency text is common in transcripts), KaTeX's stylesheet imported once by the transcript root, errors in `var(--danger)`. Advisory.
  - *Images and attachments*: the renderer shows only what arrives as data: base64 image blocks in user messages and tool results render inline from `data:` URLs; markdown images and file references become chips naming the source; files the agent sends belong to C8's Sent files. Reason: the sandbox and CSP (§4.1) forbid loading by path, and a chip is honest about it. Binding on "nothing loaded by path"; advisory on the chip.
  - *Spilled results*: a result with `spillPath` shows the wire preview and a "Load full result" action that calls C4's `fs.readSpill`, streaming into the bounded detail with the same 64 KB DOM cap; beyond 2 MB the detail shows the head and offers "Open full log" (C8's callback). Advisory on the numbers; the command is an overlap with C4.
  - *Virtualisation and anchoring*: TanStack Virtual 3.14.13 with `anchorTo: 'end'`, `followOnAppend: true`, `scrollEndThreshold: 80`, stable `getItemKey`, `measureElement` on every row, a per-kind estimate table, `overscan: 6`, normal DOM order and `overflow-anchor: none`. Follow mode is `isAtEnd()`; any upward scroll past the threshold breaks it; "Jump to latest" restores it. Collapsed work-area rows leave the row list rather than hide. A finished turn auto-collapses its work area only when the reader is following; otherwise it stays open until the reader closes it, so completion never yanks content from under a reader. Reason: verified in the published `virtual-core` 3.17.11 typings (`anchorTo`, `followOnAppend`, `scrollEndThreshold`, `scrollToEnd`, `isAtEnd`), and the behaviours are what §5.3 and §7.3 demand. Alternative: react-virtuoso, rejected because TanStack's chat mode now documents exactly this and keeps the heterogeneous row rendering ours. Binding for the behaviours (§5.3, §7.3, G1); advisory for the library and its parameters.
  - *Bounded surfaces and truncation*: group scroller 224 px, cell output 144 px, thinking 140 px, plan body 144 px collapsed, user bubbles collapse past 20 lines, code blocks 480 px; any tool output over 64 KB is shown as its first 32 KB and last 16 KB with an "N KB omitted" marker (copy still copies the whole string in memory). Reason: DOM size and measurement stability; the mockup's values. Advisory.
  - *Copy and export*: per-row copy (user text, assistant markdown source, code block source, command, output, whole cell, thinking) through `navigator.clipboard`, which the sandboxed renderer may use on a user gesture; "Copy turn as markdown" on the divider menu via `serializeRows`; no file export in C6 (C11's `/export` widget uses the engine's `export_conversation`). Advisory.
  - *Subagent rows*: one row per `subagent.started`, merged into a grouped sentence when several start from the same assistant message, up to four glyphs then "N more", the glyph seeded by hashing the task id into a fixed set of eight marks, the name from the task description; the row's detail line updates from `subagent.progress` and from the child's own events (last tool, elapsed) without rendering any child row in the parent; workflows are subagent rows of kind `workflow` with the phase and agent counts from `workflow.progress`; click calls C8's `openSubagent`. The drill-in is this same `Transcript` with `variant: 'child'` over the child's event slice. Binding on never flattening child traffic (X1); advisory on the sentence rules and glyphs.
  - *Engine-initiated turns*: a turn is engine-initiated only on positive evidence, `turn.started.origin` or `turn.ended.origin` non-null and not `human` (C3's `engine`, the wire's `task-notification`); a null origin, which C4's backfill emits for every disk turn, is human, and a missing user message is not evidence either. An engine turn has no user row; the `task.notification` or `subagent.finished` row that triggered it is its first row and its divider reads "Continued for …". Reason: the charter's turn origin exists so the reader can tell whose turn it was, and reading "not human" as engine would erase every user bubble of disk history. Binding on the evidence rule; advisory on copy.
  - *Boundaries*: compaction and reset are rows between turns; retraction removes the retracted messages' rows and leaves a row where they were; rewind removes the hidden branch from the row order, keeps it in memory, and its boundary row can reveal it muted and marked not in context. Reason: §4.2's revisable log; the app-synthesised rewind boundary of §19. Binding on the outcomes; advisory on reveal.
  - *Replay stub and harness*: `journalToEvents` under `src/renderer/src/transcript/dev/` implements C1's derivation rules and C3's outgoing-user mapping for the frames the transcript renders (a querying `out` frame is the turn start and the prompt's `user.message`; a `shouldQuery: false` echo is a `bash_echo` message that opens nothing; a `system/init` with no turn open is an engine turn) and feeds the snapshot suite and the harness; it is deleted when C3's normaliser lands and the suite switches to C3's output. The harness is a second renderer entry (`harness.html`) built in serve mode or under `MAWS_HARNESS=1`, loaded by main under the same variable and shared with C9's terminal harness through a hash router (`#transcript` is C6's page), with fixtures bundled through `import.meta.glob` and a `window.__maws_harness` API for Playwright. Synthetic journals in X8 format fill the gaps no probe fixture covers and are validated by C1's wire validators. Reason: D-3 (real data from day one) with no engine yet. Advisory.
  - *Performance budget*: full derivation of a fifty-thousand-event, five-hundred-turn transcript under 300 ms; a delta batch under 2 ms in the store; during sixty-hertz streaming with that transcript loaded, requestAnimationFrame intervals p95 ≤ 17 ms and none over 50 ms after the first half second; DOM rows ≤ visible plus twice the overscan; no long task over 50 ms from highlighting. Reason: §10 item 7 and G1. Binding (the thresholds are the gate).
  - *Testing*: the presentation model is snapshotted for every probe fixture of both passes and every synthetic journal (`test/snapshots/transcript/<pass>/<fixture>.rows.json`), every fixture gate states counts read from the fixture and mockup-exact compositions are synthetic journals, the classification table and the group state machine have table tests, row components have happy-dom tests for the behaviours acceptance names, and scroll behaviour, follow mode, the denial jump and G1 run in Playwright over the harness. Binding on "snapshot every fixture" and the G1 metrics; advisory on layout.
  - *X3 location and public surface*: X3 lives under `src/renderer/src/transcript/model/rows.ts` (types), `classify.ts`, `reduce.ts`, `derive.ts`, `copy.ts`; the public surface is `src/renderer/src/transcript/index.ts` (`Transcript`, `TranscriptSource`, `TranscriptActions`, `RewindInterface`, `useTranscriptStore`, the pure functions and the types). Renderer-local because rows never cross a process. Binding (canonical files for X3).
  - *X2 overlaps*: C6 consumes `session.eventBatch` (event) and `session.events` (snapshot command) from C3 under one hydration protocol (subscribe and buffer, apply the snapshot, drain the buffer in order with deduplication by event `id`, never by `seq`, then deliver live), with C4 emitting and delegating for disk-backed sessions, `fs.readSpill` and `fs.agentEvents` from C4, `session.rewindFiles` (with dry run) from C3, `layout.openExternal` from C5 and C5's layout store action `openTab` (not IPC), and the `user.message.origin === 'bash_echo'` X1 value convention from C3; shapes as settled in the wave-2 interface ledger, each behind one adapter line in C6's `TranscriptSource` and `TranscriptActions`. Binding.
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** in-flight since 2026-09-28 (execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c6-transcript.md`, authored against pin 87f8c788, reconciled at 2af720f; under review).

### C7: Composer and requests — plan

- **Purpose:** how the owner talks to a session: the rich editor with attachments and paste handling, the context strip, the "+" menu, typed `@` context, the slash palette shell (commands from init, skills, custom commands, app actions, marked by origin), queue list with edit, delete and steer, the submit control's five states, `!` and `#` input modes, the request card (X4) in all its placements, the status line and its popovers with the ClaudeUsage read and the engine fallback, the near-limit banner once per window.
- **Acceptance:** with C3 live: sending, queueing, steering, stopping and resuming behave as labelled; a permission replaces the composer and Enter/Esc answer it in the focused pane only; scope options map to the engine's grant kinds and take effect; a question form submits all answers; `!` runs and its output reaches the model's context (or the documented terminal-only fallback); `#` appends to the chosen memory file; ArrowUp recalls history; the status line shows cwd, context, windows, cost and mode; mockup 04 vignettes 2 to 5 render.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C1, C3, C6. blocks: C11.
- **Contracts:** owns X4; consumes X1, X2, X3, X6.
- **Design inheritance:** §5.4, §5.5 [binding for semantics, advisory for layout]; §4.2 queue and `!`/`#` [binding]; mockup 04 [advisory].
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** document closed 2026-09-28 at deccae0 after two adversarial rounds and final edits; blocked-by C3, C6; the executor dispatches on opus when both have merged, after a refresh pass against landed code.
- **Residue decisions (C7, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c7-composer-requests.md` carries the milestones, the X4 card, the composer frame and the concrete X2 additions. Grades: binding where the design binds the outcome, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Editor and document model*: Tiptap 3.31.3 (exact, the whole family on one version) with a plain-text schema: `Document` carrying an `attachments` attribute, `Paragraph`, `Text`, `HardBreak`, `Placeholder`, `UndoRedo`, `Mention` with two triggers (`@` for context, `/` for the palette, the terminal's trigger rules), a `Pasted` atom for long pastes, `FileHandler` for paste and drop, and one keymap extension; no marks, headings or lists (the model reads markdown from the text). The doc is Tiptap JSON and is what C4's `Draft.doc` stores; images and PDFs ride in `doc.attrs.attachments` (up to 4 MiB persisted), so a draft restores whole. Alternative: a textarea with a chip overlay, rejected because atoms, undo and IME are what an editor library exists for. Advisory (§4.7).
  - *Serialisation to the wire*: pure `serializeDraft(doc, { sessionId, cwd })`: text is paragraphs joined by newlines; a file mention is `@<path>` (quoted when it holds whitespace), an agent mention `@agent-<name>` (the forms the engine's `at_mentioned_files` and `agent_mentions` producers resolve, SPEC 11 §11.8, so the engine attaches the file or the agent hint itself and nothing is read app-side); a pasted atom is the terminal's exact framing `\n\n<pasted_content id="hhhh">\n…\n</pasted_content id="hhhh">\n` with the id on both tags (SHA-256 of the session id, four hex; random for an unstarted session's first send) and the terminal's neutralisation of typed tags (`<` → `<\` before `/?pasted_content`, so the closing tag keeps its slash; SPEC 42 §42.15, `cli.pretty.js:898640`), applied always even though the terminal gates its own wrapping behind `tengu_virtual_pancake` (default off): the JSONL is the store (D-12), so the segment must be recoverable from the text itself, and the model reads the content either way; the engine's own parser (`vmt`) is the grammar C6 extracts with; with no attachments `content` is the string, otherwise `[images…, documents…, { type: 'text' }]` as Messages API blocks (SPEC 45 §45.15.1 admits them on the stdin `user` frame); `!` and `#` at offset 0 select the bash and memory modes. Caps: images downscaled to 1568 px and refused over 5 MiB, PDFs over 32 MiB, a text paste becomes an atom over 1024 characters or 10 lines. C6 extracts pasted segments in its presentation model before its raw-HTML deletion, into `text` attachments carrying the body, with copy untagged and edit rebuilding the atoms (the Ask of C6 in this document's Interfaces). Landing order (architect's ruling): C6 merges first, so C6 creates the shared grammar module `src/shared/composer/pasted-content.ts` (`PASTED_BLOCK`, `pastedPlaceholder`, `countLines`) and the golden fixture `test/fixtures/composer/pasted-40-lines.json` from the spec in the Ask, and C7 consumes both and adds the send-side wrapper and neutraliser beside them in `src/shared/composer/pasted-framing.ts`, whose golden test reproduces the fixture's `content` byte for byte; neither child edits the other's file. Binding on the forms the engine resolves, on the framing and on the ownership (C6 codes against it); advisory on the caps.
  - *Catalogue protocol*: the engine host's C7 block keeps a per-session, per-launch `catalog` (`launchId`, `revision`, `commands`, `agents`, `skills`, `terminalOnly`) fed from the initialize response, the raw `system/init` frame and `commands_changed`, answers `session.catalog` from it and publishes the whole catalogue as `session.catalogChanged` at every revision bump; the renderer store subscribes first, buffers, snapshots, drains and then applies live events by (`launchId`, `revision`), never from `session.init` or `session.commands`. Alternative rejected: deriving revisions in the renderer from X1 events, which carry no revision and not all the fields. Binding on the X2 shapes (C11 reads the same catalogue); advisory on the buffering details.
  - *Slash palette sources and ranking*: three sources unioned, each with an origin badge: the engine's own catalogue through `session.catalog` (the `initialize` response's `commands` with descriptions and argument hints, `agents`, the latest `system/init`'s `skills` and `terminal_slash_commands`, refreshed by `commands_changed`), because `system/init` carries names only and the engine has already merged builtin, plugin, project and user commands as the terminal sees them; the declared widget catalogue `WIDGET_COMMANDS` as origin Widget for the ten names the engine's catalogue does not list (`permissions`, `plugins`, `sandbox`, `hooks`, `memory`, `skills`, `status`, `resume`, `export`, `diff`), so C11's registrations have a palette entry; and MAWS actions from C5's action table through `runAction`. Badges: Engine (`builtin`, no `:`), Skill (in `skills`), Plugin (a `:` in the name), Custom (the rest), Widget, MAWS. One dispatch policy: a registered widget opens for its name whatever the origin; else a catalogue command not in `terminalOnly` inserts `/<name> ` as text and runs headless (`compact` included, before C11); else the item is listed, disabled, with the "arrives with C11" hint. Ranking is deterministic and ours (exact, prefix, substring, subsequence on the name, then description word prefix; origin order and a recent-use tier), never cmdk's. Alternative: reading command and skill files from disk through C4, rejected because the engine's merged view is the truth the terminal shows. Binding on `session.catalog`, `WIDGET_COMMANDS` and the widget registry (C11 codes against them); advisory on ranking and badges.
  - *Typed `@` context*: files from a new main command `git.lsFiles` (`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` cached 10 s, a bounded walk outside repositories, a subsequence scorer), agents from the catalogue, and skills listed under `@` but inserting `/<skill> ` at the start of the document because the engine has no skill mention. Advisory.
  - *Attachments, paste and drop, "+" menu*: paste and drop reach the renderer as `File` objects; a dropped file's path comes from `webUtils.getPathForFile` exposed as `MawsBridge.pathForFile` (one additive line in `src/shared/ipc/schema.ts` and the preload), picked files through a new main command `layout.pickFiles` (native dialog; bytes returned only for images and PDFs under the caps); non-image files become `@path` mentions, pathless files become pasted atoms. Binding on the sandbox rule (nothing loaded by path in the renderer); advisory on the mechanism.
  - *Submit control and queue list*: a pure `submitState` table over `EngineSessionStatus`, the queue, the editor and the C10 setup intent (present, starting, message held) decides Send, Queue, Steer (⌥Enter or the caret menu), Stop (an empty editor during a turn, and ⌘. always) and Resume (a paused queue); on an unstarted session the control is Send and its dispatch is C10's hook `start`, which launches the engine and sends the first turn; the composer sets a per-session `starting` flag synchronously before it awaits `start` and clears it when the call settles, and while `starting`, or while the hook's `intent.message` is non-null (C10 holds the first message), a second submission is refused, every chip is disabled and the editor is read-only, so a slow start cannot be double-submitted or edited under; a `{ ok: false }` answer leaves the text in place and re-enables everything; Enter sends in a single-line doc, ⌘Enter always, Enter inserts a newline once the doc is multi-line (§5.8). Queue rows map C3's states: `queued` with Edit, Delete, Steer (`session.queue.promote`); `uncertain` at the head with a warning, Resume and Delete; `submitting` as `Sending…` without actions; `released` unlisted (it is the running turn); the paused header reads `Queue paused because you interrupted`. Binding on the mapping of C3's states; advisory on chords and copy. Amended 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W10): the control is one button, Send when the box has text (idle `session.send`, during a turn the `next` steer with the tooltip "Sends at the next step"), Stop when the box is empty during a turn, Resume on a paused queue; no caret menu, no secondary Stop, no hold; Esc interrupts and sends the queued head now.
  - *Request card (X4)*: one component with a variant switch and a placement switch; per kind: permission (bounded tool preview, requester named from the item's `agentId` and the MCP server from `mcpServer`, scope group, `Deny… with a reason`, `Deny and stop`), question (radios or checkboxes, `Other…` free text, previews in a sandboxed `srcdoc` frame, Submit only when complete; Skip removed 2026-09-29 because C3's broker refuses an answer whose kind does not match the request, and whether the engine's question tool accepts a plain deny on the wire is a C3 tracker row that would restore it), plan (C6's `Markdown`, Implement, Keep planning with feedback, Clear context and implement with one confirm), dialog (exactly the two kinds C3's `initialize` declares, each validated against the pinned binary's schema and answered with its string result: `refusal_fallback_prompt` → `retry_fallback` (Enter), `edit_prompt` (button: the initiating human prompt is captured from the transcript store before the answer and prefilled in edit mode after delivery, with its pasted bodies and images; `retractedMessageUuids` names the turn's assistant messages and tool results, never the prompt), `cancelled` (Esc); `auto_mode_server_fallback` → `continue` (Enter), `interrupt` (Esc), never `cancelled`; an undeclared kind or an invalid payload stays pending and visible with Open session and is never completed or cancelled by C7, SPEC 45 §45.22.13), elicitation (a form from `requested_schema`, Accept, Decline, Cancel; `url` mode opens the link). Answers are `toAnswer(state)` and validate against C3's `RequestAnswerSchema`. Scope options: `once` always, `session` unless downgraded, `local_settings` only from an engine `localSettings` suggestion and never when downgraded; downgraded is `decisionReasonType === 'rule'`, `matchedAskRule` present or `suppressAlwaysAllow` (C3's broker downgrades exactly these). Quick approval: `defaultToNo` disables Enter and digits (the Allow button stays); `requiresUserInteraction` removes every approve path in every placement (no Allow, no scope group, no Enter, no digit; hover shows Open session only), because the flag means the tool's own card or a local-only consent text is the surface and MAWS has neither, so a flagged request is denied or opened in a terminal, never generically allowed. Placements: docked (through the shared `DockedCardFrame`: hides the editor without unmounting it, takes focus when the pane had it), card (Enter and Esc bound to the Overview article that owns focus, resolved by walking to the nearest `tabindex` ancestor; no C5 change), hover (unflagged read-only tools, click only, C5's rule), inline (the full card; a pointer row when the same request is docked in that pane). Delivery: answering, delivered (collapsed line until the item retires), not_pending, write_failed with Retry. Binding (X4, §5.1, SPEC 45 §45.19.1 and §45.22.13, C3's answer contract); advisory on copy and bounds.
  - *X1 additive extension*: `permission.requested` gains optional `decisionReason`, `suppressAlwaysAllow`, `defaultToNo`, `matchedAskRule`, `requiresUserInteraction` and `mcpServer` as a strict `{ name, source }` object (the wire's shape, SPEC 45:4696; never a string), filled by C3's normaliser from the `can_use_tool` request with an explicit projection, which also sets `agentId` from the request's `agent_id`; the ledger's C7 block records it. Reason: the card must not offer scopes the broker will downgrade, must honour `default_to_no` and `requires_user_interaction`, and names the MCP server and its source. Binding (X1 additive, ruling 6).
  - *Status line and usage*: main reads ClaudeUsage's files (`accounts.json`; `pins.json` for the drain target and the session-to-account pins; `history/<uuid>.jsonl`'s newest row per `limitKey`, where `session` is the 5-hour window, `weekly_all` the 7-day window and `weekly_scoped#<Model>` a per-model week; no relay endpoint exists for usage), watches them with `fs.watch` and a 60 s floor, and answers `settings.usage { engineSessionIds }` and emits `settings.usageChanged`; the renderer's `usage` store (C5's, extended) selects a session's windows by its pin, else the drain target; a direct session polls `session.getUsage` instead (D-19). Popovers: cwd, context (`get_context_usage`, Compact now when `/compact` is available headless), 5h and 7d (the account, three window rows, other accounts, C11's hand-off seam), cost (`get_session_cost` and per-model usage), mode. The near-limit banner posts once per (account, window, reset time) at 90 % through C5's banner host and stays dismissed through the layout key `usage.banners`. Binding on the sources (D-17, D-19, §5.5); advisory on the threshold and layout.
  - *History storage*: ArrowUp walks this session's own prompts from the transcript store, then a merged global list answered by main (`session.history.list`): MAWS's `<userData>/prompt-history.jsonl` in the terminal's record shape, `pastedContents` included with text bodies inline, plus, read-only, the terminal's `~/.claude/history.jsonl` filtered by project with `contentHash` bodies resolved from the terminal's `paste-cache/<hash>.txt` when present (the engine's own filename: a 16-lowercase-hex SHA-256 prefix validated before any path is built, `cli.pretty.js:543732–543781`), so prompts typed in a terminal recall in MAWS with their pastes; entries deduplicate on display and body hash, never on display alone; a placeholder with no stored body (an image, an absent cache file) recalls as an unavailable atom that is never sent as its placeholder text. MAWS never appends to the terminal's file or cache (D-12), a P1 candidate once the format proves stable. Advisory.
  - *Edit-and-resubmit*: C6's `editAndResubmit` prefills the composer in an edit mode, capturing the seen cursor (the transcript's newest user uuid at that moment) with the edit; Send calls the `ResubmitProvider` C10 registers (fork, rewind in the fork, send) and, until C10, offers the explicit alternative of rewinding this session to that message and resending, driven by a state machine (`confirming` → `rewinding` → `rewound_awaiting_send` → `sent`, with `rewind_uncertain` and `rewind_unresolved` beside it): the confirm reconfirms when a later turn began after the edit started and only then advances the cursor; a send that fails after a successful rewind retries the send alone, never the rewind (a repeat would answer `target_not_found`); the engine's `rewound: false` is the one definite refusal and returns to edit mode with its reason; a process exit returns to edit mode with the draft kept; and an IPC rejection of `session.rewind` (`timeout` or `internal`, neither of which proves the mutation unapplied, c3:204) enters `rewind_uncertain`, where the composer issues nothing and waits for C3's `boundary.rewind` for the target (the authoritative record, also for a late response inside C3's 60 s late table) before treating the rewind as done; after that window it offers the human three explicit choices, rewind again, send without rewinding, or keep editing, so the composer never repeats a rewind on its own and never sends against an unknown conversation state. Forking stays the default and rewind the explicit action (§4.2). Binding on the seam's shape (C10 codes against it), on the cursor rule (C3's guard depends on it) and on the one-rewind-per-decision gate; advisory on copy and the window length.
  - *Composer frame* (§5.4 as amended by the owner's ruling and sizing correction, 2026-09-28): one compact frame organised as Claude Desktop's for every session, about two thirds of the pane's width capped near 720 px with light padding: above the box a single left-aligned chip row with location (directory or worktree with its branch, over `git.watch`; its popover offers the project directory, a new worktree with a branch name, an existing worktree and the branch picker with search and create, read-only under a live engine because branch writes are C10's) and `+ dir` (through `session.applyFlagSettings { permissions: { additionalDirectories } }` because `/add-dir` is gated to remote entrypoints headless, SPEC 28 §22, with "Restart with directory" as the recorded fallback); the box holding only the editor and the submit control at its right, one line tall at rest and growing to a cap; and below the box, outside its border but inside the frame, a toolbar of plain controls: "+", `@`, mic (P1 placeholder), the mode chip showing only the engine's effective mode with the five refusal codes in words and ⇧Tab cycling `default → acceptEdits → plan → auto`, and model with effort from `session.listModels` through `session.setModel` and `session.setEffort` (C11's widget replaces the menu when registered). No project chip (the tab names the project), no routing, hint or explanatory line around the frame (the status line carries those facts). C5 places the frame (centred under one heading naming the project on an unstarted session, docked under the transcript once it starts); C7 renders one component for both. Binding on the anatomy and the chip semantics (the owner's ruling; C5 places it); advisory on the sizes and copy.
  - *Keyboard map entries*: `composer.steer` (⌥Enter, composer scope, delivered only inside the editor's sink), `composer.stop` (⌘., app scope, resolving the focused pane through the layout store because C5's dispatcher refuses pane-scoped dispatch from a contenteditable and from under any `data-key-sink`, which is where ⌘. is pressed), `composer.focus` (⌘L, app scope), renderer sink, no menu placement; the two app-scoped actions are registered exactly once by `ComposerAppKeys` (mounted once from `App.tsx`) and resolve the focused pane's session centrally, never once per Composer, because C5's app scope ignores `within` and selects a single enabled handler, so per-pane registrations in a split would race on registration order; `data-key-sink="composer"` marks the editor subtree only, so the docked card, chip row, toolbar, queue list and submit control stay ordinary pane content and `request.accept`/`request.decline` keep C5's exclusions untouched; everything else the composer answers is editor-local. Advisory on the chords; binding on the marker's extent and on the single registration (C5's dispatcher rules depend on both). Amended 2026-10-01 (W10): `composer.steer` (⌥Enter) is removed.
  - *Unstarted sessions and the docked host* (owner ruling 2026-09-28): the composer serves an unstarted session as it serves a live one, the same frame centred by C5 under C10's heading; its chips take a `ChipBinding` and are agnostic of the store behind it, `intentBindings` over C10's setup-intent hook while unstarted and `liveBindings` over C3's status afterwards; Send on an unstarted session calls the hook's `start` with the serialised first message and C10 launches, under the starting and message-held gates of the *Submit control* bullet; while the hook is non-null the draft rides in the intent through `patch({ draft })` and is restored from `intent.draft`, never through C4's `drafts` table (C10's Ask of C7), and `prefillComposer` writes through the same path; `!`, `#` and the queue are disabled until the engine exists. The docked placement is a host: `DockedCardFrame` carries the chrome, the focus rules and the Enter/Esc registration, and `dockCard` lets C10 dock its trust and relay-down cards ahead of any request under exactly a permission card's rules. Binding on the frame, the `dockCard` seam and the hook's expected shape (C10 codes against them; the Ask of C10 in Interfaces); advisory on copy.
  - *Dev-only resume launcher*: the Diagnostics tab gains a `Resume live` control (unpackaged only) that launches `session.launch` with `resume` for an existing record, passing its `Direct`, `Permission mode` and `extraSettings` fields through literally, the only product-shaped way to put a live process behind a record before C10; C10 removes it. Advisory.
  - *Testing*: vitest happy-dom for the pure serialisation over Tiptap JSON (headless `Editor`, or `Node.fromJSON` if happy-dom refuses) with golden pasted-framing cases, paste classification, history navigation and recall with bodies, `submitState`, queue rows, scope options, the quick-approval policy, `toAnswer` against `RequestAnswerSchema`, the two declared dialog kinds and the undeclared state over SPEC-derived synthetic requests (`test/fixtures/requests/*.json`, since no fixture producer exists), the refusal dialog's `Edit prompt` restoring the initiating prompt from a store whose retraction list holds assistant uuids only, card placements and delivery states, the card's focus owner inside a focusable article, the actual Composer DOM under C5's actual dispatcher with two Composers mounted against focus order and one Stop handler, the docked frame hosting a foreign card, the resubmit state machine (a queued turn during confirmation, a send failure after a rewind, a rewind whose command rejected and is reconciled against `boundary.rewind`, a process exit mid-rewind), the unstarted send's starting and message-held gates over a deferred `start`, ranking and the palette union, the catalogue store's reconciliation, the banner rule; vitest node for the usage reader over a redacted fixture directory, prompt history with a fixture paste-cache, `git.lsFiles`, the catalogue block's answers and publications over replayed handshakes; Playwright over the `#composer` harness and C3's replay launch (`MAWS_ENGINE_REPLAY=1`) for keys, popups, paste, drop, the request round trips against `permission-roundtrip`, `question-roundtrip`, `pass2/dialogs`, `pass2/grant-reapply-start`, `pass2/bash-context-echoed` and `pass1/queue-and-interrupt` (the card-page cases at M2, the real-composer case at M4, the whole suite again at M6), and the status line over the fixture directory; the live acceptance with C3 through the relay, auto mode for the auto cases and `default` mode with inspected rules for the grant case. Advisory.

### C8: Right panel tools — decomposing at dispatch

- **Purpose:** the auxiliary panel: the host with per-session tabs, pin, maximize and the "+" menu (X5); the pinned summary as the resting state; and the tools Subagents (lists, drill-in with the C6 renderer over child transcripts, message a child), Review (session diff with file navigation), Files (tree and viewer tabs), Background work (shells, monitors, task lists, workflows with phases and agents, stop), Session info.
- **Acceptance:** coarse until its own cut: every state of mockup 03 renders from live sessions; pinned tabs persist across sessions; each pinned-summary row opens its tool; a child receives a message relayed from the drill-in through its parent; Review opens a file tab at the changed line. Sent files is deferred to P1 as a MAWS-integrated SendUserFile (owner, 2026-09-28).
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C4, C5, C6. blocks: C10 (attach affordance), C12.
- **Contracts:** owns X5; consumes X1, X3.
- **Design inheritance:** §5.6 [binding for the tool list and the per-session-with-pin rule, advisory for layout]; `research/codex-shell-ux.md` §3 [advisory]; mockup 03 [advisory].
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** decomposing run complete 2026-09-28: grounding 7cf8957, composite spec `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c8-right-panel.md` at 94b1bd8 (adversarial review closed 2026-09-28: seven findings, all fixed; K1 to K4 as the spec states, K2's source C6's under C8's ask), cut approved by the owner into C8a (panel host, tool registry, pinned summary; owns X5; after C5 and C6), C8b (Subagents, Background work, Log; after C8a, C3, C6, C7) and C8c (Review, Files, viewer, `files.*` service; after C8a, C4, C6); all three children landed (C8a b360711, C8c e02dffb8, C8b 89edebd0); recomposition closed 2026-09-29 on every agent-verifiable point (closing pass at 950ee9ab, merged aada347c: Session info registered and observed; item 9 restated to Attach activating its session and verified; G3 run live under a scratch home and passed, US$0.10; acceptance text amended; lineage clean, no corrective child); the owner's one sitting remains, C12's closing act.

### C9: Terminal — plan

- **Purpose:** a real terminal in the session's worktree: the PTY host service (lifecycle, resize, backpressure), the renderer adapter with ghostty-web first and xterm.js with WebGL as the fallback, decided by its spike gate; the Terminal tool registered into X5; the worktree identity warning.
- **Acceptance:** G1 (spike, required, first): ghostty-web under the acceptance list in §7.2 (both TUIs, alternate screen, synchronized output, CJK and emoji, IME, selection and copy, hyperlinks, scrollback, resize and reflow, sustained output, sleep and resume); a failed item switches the adapter to xterm.js and records why. G2: a terminal opens in the session's cwd, survives switching sessions when pinned, warns when the session moved worktree and offers a new terminal, closes with confirmation when a command is running.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C1. blocks: C10.
- **Contracts:** implements X5; owns the PTY IPC subset of X2.
- **Design inheritance:** §5.6 Terminal [binding for behaviour], §7.2 [binding acceptance list], `research/landscape.md` Part B [advisory].
- **Residue decisions (C9, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c9-terminal.md` carries the spike, the milestones and the concrete `pty.*` definitions. Grades: binding where the design binds the outcome or where siblings depend on the shape, advisory where a later child may overturn with a dated line here.
  - *Renderer*: xterm.js `@xterm/xterm` 6.0.0 with `@xterm/addon-webgl` 0.19.0 is the expected primary, with xterm.js's own DOM renderer as the fallback and ghostty-web discarded; the spike (M1) observes and records this rather than assuming it. Evidence at authoring: `ghostty-web`'s last stable release is 0.4.0 (2025-12-09) with prereleases ending 2026-06-28 and no push since 2026-07-02; on 0.4.0 Korean IME is broken (coder/ghostty-web #119, open; the owner types Korean), `getHyperlinkUri()` always returns null (#137), a WebAssembly heap-corruption fix (commit bec9e16) is unreleased, synchronized output is never honoured by its renderer, the Kitty keyboard encoder is not wired, the WASM ships as a `data:` URI that a strict CSP blocks (#188), and its `write` callback fires on the next animation frame with no backpressure design; those are four charter list items failed on paper (IME, hyperlinks, synchronized output, sustained output) before any measurement. xterm.js 6.0.0 (2025-12-22) supports mode 2026, OSC 8 through `linkHandler`, grapheme clustering through `@xterm/addon-unicode-graphemes` 0.4.0, IME, and `write(data, callback)` flow control. Ghostty's core is measurably faster (libghostty-vt versus xterm.js, 2 to 7× on throughput per xtermjs/xterm.js #5686), which is why the adapter keeps a `RendererKind` seam and the probe page stays: a maintained libghostty package (ghostty-org's signed `ghostty-vt.wasm` has no renderer or npm package yet) re-runs the same spike without touching the tool. Kitty keyboard protocol is deferred: it exists only in xterm.js 6.1 betas, which also drop single-character IME commits under it (#6112); both TUIs fall back to legacy keys. Alternative rejected: shipping ghostty-web `0.4.0-next.20.g1858a59` (the registry's `next` tag, what Coder's Mux pins) for its speed; it carries the same IME and design gaps and no release process. Advisory (§4.7), with the spike's switch rule binding (G1).
  - *WebGL policy*: a terminal holds a WebGL context only while visible (mounted and its tab selected); hiding disposes the WebGL addon, showing reloads it, so contexts in flight equal visible terminals (one, two under a split) against Chromium's cap of 16 per renderer process; a budget of 8 concurrent WebGL terminals is enforced in the registry, the rest draw with the DOM renderer. On `webglcontextlost` the addon waits three seconds for a restore, then fires `onContextLoss`; the adapter disposes it, continues on the DOM renderer, and retries WebGL on the next show. The same path covers sleep and resume and is exercised deterministically through the `WEBGL_lose_context` extension in the end-to-end test. Alternative rejected: raising `--max-active-webgl-contexts`, which moves the cliff instead of removing it. Advisory.
  - *Scrollback and memory*: 10,000 lines per terminal, a constant `SCROLLBACK_LINES` in `src/renderer/src/terminal/constants.ts`, no settings surface in P0 (C11 may lift it into settings). Bound: xterm.js stores 12 bytes per cell, so a 10,000-line buffer costs 12 × cols × 10,000 bytes at most, 6 MB at the panel's 50 columns and 24 MB at 200; the host additionally keeps, per pty, a 512 KiB ring buffer of raw output and the latest checkpoint the renderer sent (*Reattach checkpoint*), a string the size of the serialised scrollback. Alternative rejected: xterm.js's 1,000 default, too short for `pnpm test` output. Advisory.
  - *PTY stream and flow control*: output crosses X2 as binary (`Uint8Array`) chunks, one `pty.data` event per node-pty read (observed ≤ 64 KiB), each stamped with its stream `offset`; keystrokes stay strings (`pty.write`). Backpressure is credit by offset: the renderer acknowledges the offset it has parsed (`pty.ack`), coalesced to one ack per 32 KiB or 16 ms; the host pauses the pty when emitted-minus-acknowledged bytes reach 256 KiB and resumes at 64 KiB (within xterm.js's flow-control guide, which caps the high mark at 500 KB and drops data past a 50 MB buffer if nobody pauses the producer). A hidden terminal acknowledges at most 1 MiB/s so a runaway `cat` in a background tab blocks its producer instead of starving the UI thread. `pty.attach` returns the latest checkpoint, the ring's bytes from that checkpoint's offset (or the whole ring when none reaches it) and the offset at which live events continue; the renderer drops any event below that offset. The ring is twice the high watermark so that while the pty is paused it always reaches back to the last acknowledged offset, and a checkpoint is never taken below that offset; that invariant is what makes reconnection after a renderer reload exact (*Reattach checkpoint*). Binary because both candidate renderers take bytes, the renderer's decoder then sees exactly what the program wrote, and the host can split on byte counts without caring about character boundaries. Alternative rejected: strings with host-side decoding, which is what C1 shipped and works, but forces two decoders and per-chunk string copies on the hot path. Binding on the X2 shape (siblings validate these entries), advisory on the numbers.
  - *Terminal environment*: the shell is the user's login shell (`$SHELL`, else `/bin/zsh`) started with `-l`, so PATH and exports come from the owner's own dotfiles exactly as in Terminal.app; the seed environment is the base (X6's captured login-shell environment, `captureShellEnvironment()` from C3's `src/shared/host/shell-env.ts`, run once at PTY-host start by `baseEnvironment()` in `src/pty-host/env.ts`, with the host's `process.env` as the fallback when the capture fails) minus the parent-session exclusions of §4.1, plus `TERM=xterm-256color`, `COLORTERM=truecolor`, `TERM_PROGRAM=MAWS`, `TERM_PROGRAM_VERSION=<app version>`, `CLAUDE_CODE_FORCE_SYNC_OUTPUT=1` (Claude Code 2.1.283 enables synchronized output only if XTVERSION is answered, which xterm.js 6.0.0 does not, or if this variable or a `TERM_PROGRAM` allowlist says so), and `LC_CTYPE=UTF-8` when no `LANG`, `LC_ALL` or `LC_CTYPE` is present (a Dock-launched app has none; this makes the tty UTF-8 without choosing a language). No gateway variables, no `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT`: a terminal is the owner's shell, not an engine launch. Alternative rejected: reusing X6's engine allowlist verbatim, which would route a hand-started `claude` through app-chosen settings instead of the owner's shell. Binding on the exclusions and the no-injection rule (D-12 interop), advisory on the additions.
  - *Unicode width*: the terminal must allocate cells the way the program computed them. xterm.js runs with `@xterm/addon-unicode-graphemes` active so ZWJ emoji sequences and Hangul are one cluster of two cells, matching Ink's `string-width` and ratatui's `unicode-width`; the spike verifies with a cursor-position report (`CSI 6 n`) after printing fixture strings and compares the reported column with the expected width. Advisory on mechanism; the CJK-and-emoji item is binding (G1).
  - *Search*: not in P0. Nothing in §5.6 asks for it, neither TUI needs it, and Codex's own terminal ships Clipboard, Fit and WebLinks only; `@xterm/addon-search` drops in later behind an adapter method. Advisory.
  - *Splits*: no split inside a Terminal tab. The panel is 380 px wide; a second terminal is a second Terminal tab (`multiInstance: true`, titled "Terminal 2"), which also gives "multiple ptys per session" without layout state. A terminal in the main area or a bottom dock is P1 (§3). Advisory.
  - *Persistence*: a terminal's renderer instance and container `<div>` live in the terminal registry, not in React state; the tool's render mounts the existing container and detaches it on unmount, so switching tabs, closing the panel or switching sessions under a pinned tab never re-creates the emulator or loses scrollback, and a running process is untouched. A pinned Terminal tab keeps its shells: it does not move to the new session's cwd; when the shell's spawn cwd differs from the shown session's directory (or its worktree path), the tool shows the worktree warning with "New terminal here". After a renderer reload the registry re-adopts live ptys from `pty.list` by `(appSessionId, label)` and restores each from its checkpoint and the ring buffer (*Reattach checkpoint*); after an app restart there are none (hosts die with the app, D-16). Binding on "pinned keeps its shells and warns" (§5.6, D-10), advisory on mechanism.
  - *Reattach checkpoint*: a raw byte tail cannot restore a terminal: the modes a program set before the tail (bracketed paste, the alternate screen, application cursor keys, mouse tracking) are gone, and the tail's first byte can split a UTF-8 or escape sequence (reproduced on xterm.js 6.0.0). A reattach therefore has two sources: a checkpoint, the emulator's own serialised state (`@xterm/addon-serialize` 0.14.0: screen, scrollback, cursor, attributes, the alternate screen and the persistent modes; not cursor visibility, the title or mode 2026) stamped with the stream offset it reflects, sent as `pty.checkpoint` and kept in the host per pty; and the ring's bytes after that offset. The addon keeps no parser or decoder continuation and restores SGR mouse encoding (`?1006`) as the default (verified in a real round trip), so checkpoints are boundary-safe: taken only where the parser is in its ground state with no escape or partial UTF-8 sequence pending (a pure byte-level stream tracker over every written chunk; a checkpoint due mid-sequence waits for the next boundary, and `pagehide` sends none then), with the mouse tracking and encoding modes `?1000`, `?1002`, `?1003`, `?1006`, focus reporting `?1004` and any other the round-trip test finds missing captured by the tracker and re-applied explicitly after `serialize()`'s output. The renderer checkpoints every live terminal on `pagehide` and a dirty one 500 ms after its last parsed byte, never on a byte count (serialising 10,000 lines must not run per chunk in a flood). Contract: exact when the ring reaches back to a boundary-safe checkpoint (guaranteed after a `pagehide` checkpoint by the ring-size invariant of *PTY stream and flow control*): the state, then the replay, nothing reset or redrawn; lossy whenever exactness cannot be established: the emulator is reset, the tail written from the byte after its first line feed, the tab's bar reads "Reconnected; earlier output was not kept", and a full-screen program is nudged to repaint. `TerminalInfo.reconnect` records which path ran. Alternatives rejected: a byte-count cadence, which serialises exactly when the UI thread is busiest; a host-side emulator, a second parser to keep in step. Binding on the two-path contract, boundary safety with the explicitly re-applied modes, and the notice, advisory on mechanism.
  - *Keyboard*: with a terminal focused, every key without ⌘ goes to the pty (Esc, ⇧Tab, Tab, ⌃C, ⌃D, ⌃L, ⌃R, arrows, ⌥-chords with `macOptionIsMeta: true`), except ⌃Tab and ⌃⇧Tab, which stay the app's tab cycle; every ⌘ chord goes to the app, except ⌘C (copy selection; nothing when there is none), ⌘V (paste), ⌘A (select all), which the terminal handles. ⌘K remains the command palette (§5.8): clearing a terminal is ⌃L or the tab's context menu. This is the focused-pane rule of §5.7 applied to a terminal: the app's Enter/Esc/⇧Tab meanings belong to the composer and request card, which are not focused. Implemented as one key filter installed through the adapter, plus a contract with C5's window-capture dispatcher, which runs before xterm's own `keydown` listener on its helper textarea: the terminal container carries `data-key-sink="terminal"`, C5's dispatcher refuses pane-scoped dispatch (`request.accept` on Enter, `request.decline` on Escape, the ⌃Tab pair) inside a `[data-key-sink]` element or on any text-entry element whatever its content, and delivers composer-scoped actions (⇧Tab, ⌘Enter) only inside `data-key-sink="composer"`; menu-sink ⌘ chords (⌘K, ⌘W, ⌘1..9, ⌘⇧J, ⌘B, ⌘⇧B, ⌘N, ⌘\) reach main through the application menu before the page sees them, and the terminal forwards ⌃Tab and ⌃⇧Tab itself through C5's `runAction`. `macOptionClickForcesSelection: true` so ⌥-drag selects while a TUI tracks the mouse. Kitty keyboard protocol off (see *Renderer*). Binding on the rule and on the marker contract with C5 (wave-2 ledger), advisory on the chord list.
  - *Copy, paste and links*: ⌘C copies the selection as plain text; ⌘V pastes, bracketed when the program asked for it; the tab's context menu offers Copy, Paste, Select all, Clear. OSC 8 links and detected URLs open on ⌘-click through `pty.openLink` (owner main; its handler calls C5's `openExternalUrl` in `src/main/open-external.ts`, the one scheme-checked `shell.openExternal`: `http`, `https`, `mailto`; anything else is logged and answered `opened: false`). `file:` links and bare paths that should open a viewer tab are P1 (they need C8's Files tool). OSC 52 clipboard writes from programs are not honoured. Advisory.
  - *Fonts and palette*: 21 new X7 token names, added to `mockups/tokens.css`, `tokens-light.css` and `src/renderer/src/styles/tokens.css`: `--term-bg` (light: `var(--bg-raised)`), `--term-fg` (`var(--fg)`), `--term-cursor`, `--term-cursor-fg`, `--term-selection`, and `--term-ansi-<name>` and `--term-ansi-bright-<name>` for `black red green yellow blue magenta cyan white`. Values are advisory (light: a VS Code-light-derived readable set on a white ground; dark: the same family on `#1a1a1a`). The adapter reads them with `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)` and rebuilds the theme on every `settings.changed`, so the terminal recolours with the app and no component holds a colour literal. Font: `--font-mono` and `--text-code`, line height 1.2. Names accepted into X7 (wave-2 ledger, ruling 6) and binding, values advisory.
  - *Lifecycle and close*: a terminal is `starting`, `running`, `exited` (with code and signal) or `lost` (the host crashed or restarted: main's supervisor emits `pty.exit` with null code and null signal for every pty it knew, C1 M4). `exited` and `lost` keep the dead screen visible and show a bar: "Process exited (code N)" or "Terminal lost, the terminal service restarted", with "New terminal" (enabled once `pty.hostState` is `ready`, same cwd) and "Close". Closing a `running` terminal asks `pty.foreground`; when a program other than the shell is in the foreground the tool shows an inline bar "A command is running: `<name>`. Close anyway?" with "Close" and "Keep" (no dialog dependency), otherwise closes at once; closing kills the pty with SIGHUP. The tab title follows the program's OSC 0/2 title, falling back to the foreground process name. Binding on the behaviours G2 names, advisory on copy.
  - *Terminal tool and X5*: the tool is `src/renderer/src/tools/terminal/index.ts` exporting `terminalTool: PanelTool<TerminalTabState>` and `openTerminal({ appSessionId, cwd, command? })` for C10's attach affordance (the command is written to the shell followed by a newline; the tty buffers it until the shell reads). The X5 interface it assumes is written in `src/renderer/src/panel/tool.ts` (Interfaces) and holds four things C8's host must honour or C9 adapts at that file: state is created once per tab and kept across visibility changes, panel close and session switches under a pin; the render context carries the shown session's record (for the worktree comparison); `beforeClose` can veto a close; `multiInstance` allows a second Terminal tab. Until C8 lands, the harness's `#panel` page is the host. Advisory (X5 is C8's).
  - *Testing and harness*: vitest node project for `src/pty-host/**` against real node-pty (flood, pause and resume, ring buffer, attach offsets, checkpoint and replay window, environment, initial command, foreground detection) and for `src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.test.ts`; vitest happy-dom project for `src/renderer/src/terminal/**` with a `FakeRenderer` implementing `TerminalRenderer` (ack coalescing, offset dedupe, pending buffering across attach, exact and lossy reattach, checkpoint cadence, visibility throttle, WebGL budget, key filter, theme assembly from an injected token reader) and, for the checkpoint round trip through the real emulator without a DOM, `@xterm/headless` with the serialize addon; Playwright `e2e/terminal.spec.ts` launching the built app with `MAWS_HARNESS=1` against the real renderer, the real host and real shells; the `#probe` page doubles as the performance harness with its measurements written as JSON by `spikes/terminal-probe/run.mjs`. Advisory.
  - *Dependencies*: `@xterm/xterm` 6.0.0, `@xterm/addon-webgl` 0.19.0, `@xterm/addon-fit` 0.11.0, `@xterm/addon-web-links` 0.12.0, `@xterm/addon-unicode-graphemes` 0.4.0, `@xterm/addon-serialize` 0.14.0, all exact (the 6.1 line is beta-only through 2026-08-30 and the addons' internals track the core's minor); `@xterm/headless` 6.0.0 exact as a devDependency (the checkpoint round-trip test); `ghostty-web` 0.4.0-next.20.g1858a59 exact as a devDependency while it is a candidate, removed on discard (the registry's `next` dist-tag; the bare `0.4.0-next.20` does not exist, and M1 opens with an install gate that proves both candidates resolve). Advisory.
- **Required:** required.
