### C10: Sessions, worktrees and trust — plan

- **Purpose:** the session lifecycle end to end: the unstarted session tab and its launch on first send (§5.1 as amended 2026-09-28: `session.create` allocates the app session id and a setup intent; the composer strip's chips, owned by C7, read and write that intent while the session is unstarted; `session.start` at first send creates the worktree, runs trust and relay checks as docked cards, launches, and sends the first turn), the trust step and attestation (trusting is optional at start: a quiet "Start without trusting" secondary launches untrusted and surfaces the engine's own notice in the session; owner's decision 2026-09-28, §19), worktree create, reuse and cleanup under the engine's convention, fork in place and fork to a new worktree (no transcript copy: the engine writes the fork under the worktree's own slug, §19 2026-09-28 C10) with rollback, rename with provenance, archive, delete conversation, foreign-live behaviour with "attach in a terminal tab" for background jobs, and the grant snapshot hook for later re-application.
- **Acceptance:** each path in §3 "Sessions" works against the real engine and the owner's repositories; a never-trusted directory prompts once and then loads project settings exactly as the terminal does (compared by the init frame; for the home directory, whose trust the engine never persists, the launch attests per session and the card names what cannot load, C10 parent-impact 2026-09-28); a fork to a new worktree produces a session that resumes from the terminal; cleanup distinguishes archive, remove clean checkout, discard changes, delete branch; a `--bg` job attaches in a terminal tab.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C3, C4, C5, C9. blocks: C12.
- **Contracts:** extends X6 with trust and worktree fields; consumes X2, X5.
- **Design inheritance:** §3 Sessions, §4.5, §5.1 new-session sheet, D-12 [binding]; `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md` items 8, 9, 10 and 12 (trust attestation paths, grant re-application, holder behaviour, spare claiming) [binding as verified]; `research/claude-desktop-ux-and-engine.md` B7, B8 [advisory]; `mockups/05-sessions.html` (new-session sheet, trust step, relay-down launch, fork and rename, cleanup ladder, foreign-live, archive and delete) [advisory, taste reference].
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** document closed 2026-09-28 at 42a5722 after two adversarial rounds, a bounded third-round check and final edits; blocked-by C3, C4, C5, C9; the executor dispatches on opus when all four have merged, after a refresh pass against the landed code.
- **Residue decisions (C10, 2026-09-28):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c10-sessions-worktrees-trust.md` carries the interfaces, the state tables, the milestones and the gates.
  - *Lifecycle commands and the setup event*: C10 adds, owner main, `session.create { projectId, location }` (an app session id and a persisted setup intent; no process, no record), `session.intent.get/list/patch/discard/release` (`release` is the hand-back: `message` cleared, `draft` kept, the intent published), `session.start { appSessionId, message, options }` (an intent: the setup, worktree → trusting → launching → ready; a record: a resume, with the guards `archived`, `already_live`, `held`, `missing_directory`, `needs_trust` in that precedence, the exclusive ones from a fresh holder read), `session.fork { appSessionId, target, message, rewindTo }`, `session.archive { appSessionId, onlyIfIdle? }` (`onlyIfIdle` is the cleanup dialog's precondition: a session live at the recheck is answered `skipped_live`, never stopped), `session.delete { appSessionId, stopIfLive }`, `session.rename { appSessionId, title }`, `session.trust.check { directory }`, `session.revealPath`; owner engine-host, `session.forget { appSessionId }` and `engine.models { refresh }`; the events `session.setup { appSessionId, phase: worktree | trusting | launching | ready | failed, detail, outcome }` and `session.intent.changed`. `session.start` answers synchronously only its guards or `starting`; every later phase streams as `session.setup`. Every launch goes through C3's `session.launch`; C7's Send, the sidebar menus and the cards call these and never C3's launch directly. Binding (C7 and C11 code against the names).
  - *No sheet; the unstarted session* (owner's ruling 2026-09-28, D-20, §5.4 as amended): "+" and ⌘N open a session tab whose id has an intent and no record; one heading names the project and C7's composer frame sits centred beneath it; C7's chips bind to C10's `useSetupIntent` hook (`{ intent, options: { locations, branches, models, autoModeNote }, patch, start }`, C7's "Ask of C10" shape) and Send is the hook's `start`; the trust step, the relay-down fallback and a failed setup are cards docked through C7's `dockCard` inside `DockedCardFrame`; closing an unstarted tab discards the intent, asking only when the draft is non-empty; unstarted sessions have no sidebar row; intents and their drafts survive a restart. Binding on the hook shape, the slot signature and the docked seam (C5 and C7 code against them), advisory on copy.
  - *Trust*: the trust key is the git repository's main-worktree root (linked worktrees share it; SPEC §3 15.2, verified by the pass-3 probe; resolved with `--path-format=absolute`, so a subdirectory answers the root) or the directory's NFC realpath; MAWS reads `projects[<key>].hasTrustDialogAccepted` from the engine's own global-config path (`<configHome>/.config.json` when present, else `<configHome>/.claude<oauth suffix>.json`, SPEC §3 16.1) on demand and never writes it, and reads the key's own entry only: the headless engine gates project settings on that exact key (`Xf()`, SPEC §3 15.2) and ignores an older per-worktree `projects[<directory>]` entry, so a fallback to it would report trusted while the engine loaded nothing and the card stayed hidden (amended 2026-09-28, §19); the decision is a tri-state (`unanswered`, `accepted`, `declined`): only `accepted` attests (`initialize.workspaceTrust { accepted: true, directory: <trust key> }`; the same object under `claim_session.workspace_trust` for C12's spare, unverified there), `declined` launches unattested and the engine's notice is the session's warning, `unanswered` on an untrusted key docks the trust card; for a never-trusted key with `accepted` a trust bootstrap launch (attested, confirmed by `workspace_trust_recorded: true`, stopped) precedes the real launch because `.mcp.json`, plugins and frontmatter hooks read trust at launch (SPEC §45 line 4422) and `--await-initialize` changes only the `plugins` field; the bootstrap runs under a bootstrap id of its own, never the conversation's (a resume's bootstrap under the record's id would let C4 patch the conversation with the bootstrap's engine id and defaults), is forgotten at release, and is counted by occupancy through `EngineSessionStatus.cwd`; the decision is bound to the key it answered (`intent.trustKey`) and resets to `unanswered` when a location change moves the key, so accepting A never attests B; the home directory gets no bootstrap (the engine never persists its trust: `session_only`, `workspace_trust_recorded: false`), is attested per launch, and its card states that plugins, `.mcp.json` servers and agent hooks under `~` cannot be enabled; additional directories are never trust-gated (`--add-dir` roots are not settings sources). The engine's stderr notice is emitted by C3's normaliser as `notice.posted { key: 'trust:ignored' }` at the correlated `initialize` response when it did not record trust (`pass2/trust-no-attestation.jsonl` seq 5 and 6; none over `trust-attested`). `EngineSessionStatus.trust { attested, recorded }` is C10's additive X6 field. Binding as verified.
  - *Fork*: no transcript is copied. The engine resolves `--resume <id>` across the current project directory, every worktree's project directory and all of `projects/*` (SPEC §35 12.5, 17.1), declines an ambiguous duplicate, and writes `--fork-session`'s new session under the launch directory's slug with the history re-written under the new id (pass-3 `fork-worktree-fork.jsonl`); the new id is pinned with `--session-id` beside `--resume --fork-session` (SPEC §35 16.5–16.6; the Ask of C3; M1's `fork-pinned-id` fixture), so a fork's identity is known from spawn exactly as a new session's. Fork in place and fork to a worktree differ only in the `cwd` of the launch; rollback removes the worktree and record the fork created and never touches the source; a fork is refused while the source has a turn open in MAWS; the fork inherits the source's grants through `LaunchSpec.inheritGrantsFrom` and, when the source had a user title, is renamed `<title> (fork)` through the engine; C7's resubmit seam is a fork with `rewindTo`. The charter's "by copying the transcript under the new project slug" is withdrawn on this evidence (`[parent-impact]`, Decision Log). Binding.
  - *Retained intent*: `session.start` persists the message and options into the intent before any effect and keeps it, with per-attempt `progress` (the pinned engine id, a created worktree with its head, trust recorded for its key, the bootstrap id, the accepted launch, the handoff), until the session is identity-bound (`ready`, the first message handed to C3's queue and its `queueId` recorded as `progress.send` before the intent is removed); a retry after `needs_trust`, `relay_unreachable` or a failure re-runs the same intent with only what the human changed, so a refused fork stays a fork and a first message is never lost; a rejected guard (`invalid`, `missing_directory`, `refused`) and `session.intent.release` hand the message back by clearing `intent.message` in the service (the draft untouched), which is the fact C7's gate reads, while `needs_trust` and `relay_unreachable` retain it unchanged behind the docked card, whose continuation runs with it; a resume keeps the same shape for the message it carries. At app start an intent whose record exists is reconciled, never re-run: the pin's transcript, C3's persisted queue (`session.queue.list` for an unreserved id; its documented `unknown session` reads as empty, any other error propagates as that intent's reconciliation failure) and `progress.send` decide between finishing the handoff (bind the pin when its transcript exists, else unbind the record's engine id through C4's `sessions.unbindEngineId` so the next launch is a fresh pinned session; drop the intent; send nothing) and a failed attempt whose provisional record is removed as rollback removes it, the intent and pin retained and Retry recreating the record under the same app id; no reconciliation launches (D-16). Binding (D-16 and D-19 depend on it).
  - *Worktree convention and invocation*: `<repoRoot>/.claude/worktrees/<name with / as +>` on branch `worktree-<name+>` by default (the engine's `EnterWorktree` convention, SPEC §47 11.1); created from the main root whichever worktree the owner started in, so C4's membership rule keeps it under the same project; `git worktree add --no-track -b <branch> <path> <base>` for a new branch (`-b`, where the engine uses `-B`, because the chip promised not to reset an existing branch), `git worktree add <path> <branch>` for an existing one; base defaults to `HEAD` and is never fetched; `info/exclude` is left to the engine, which appends `**/.claude/worktrees/` under its own marker once per process per directory before its first scheduler-lock attempt there (SPEC §47 18.4), so its presence is recorded, not required. Binding on the convention (§4.5), advisory on mechanism.
  - *`git.worktree.*` beside C4's read-only service*: `git.worktree.list`, `status`, `branch`, `add`, `remove`, `deleteBranch` (owner main) in `src/main/git/worktrees.ts` over C4's runner, the only git mutations in P0; C4's service and its read-only guard are untouched; hand-made worktrees at any path with any branch are first-class; `status` and `branch` derive the cleanup state from git and a fresh holder read on every call and never cache it. Binding on the names and the read-only split, advisory on mechanism.
  - *Cleanup state machine*: states `busy` (a MAWS process, a foreign holder or an unbound registry holder in it, or locked), `dirty`, `clean`, `detached` (branch only), `gone`; the four operations are separate commands (archive through `session.archive` per id, `git.worktree.remove`, the same with `discard`, `git.worktree.deleteBranch`), each revalidating the identity the dialog captured (`expect { branch, head }`; refused `changed` when the owner's terminal moved it); archive sessions archives the worktree's idle sessions only (`occupancy.idleAppSessionIds`, each through `session.archive { onlyIfIdle: true }`, which rechecks holder authority inside the session's serialised operation and answers `skipped_live` rather than stopping a session that resumed since the snapshot), never stops a process, and names the live and skipped sessions (stopping is only ever the per-session archive dialog with its background-work disclosure); remove needs `clean`; discard-and-remove needs the acknowledgement naming the change count; delete branch runs only on a branch no checkout has and, unmerged into the main worktree's branch, needs the acknowledgement naming the commit count; a refusal stops at its step and undoes nothing. Binding on the four distinctions and the guards (§3, §4.5), advisory on copy.
  - *Holder authority*: "live in MAWS" is C3's `engine.sessions` (a reservation, until the OS reports the exit, each status carrying its spec's `cwd` (the Ask of C3) so a directory's reservations, a record-less bootstrap included, are counted without the index), "foreign" is a fresh registry read through C4's `fs.holders` (the Ask of C4), by session id for a session and by the registry record's `cwd` for a directory (so a hand-made worktree with a live terminal the index never saw is `busy`); a fail-closed `fs.holders` answer (`holder registry unreadable`) refuses the guarded operation with C4's message and is never read as "no holders"; `SessionRecord.holder` is the sidebar's projection and never a guard input; `self` is reset at app start (D-16); MAWS serialises its own lifecycle operations per session. Stated, not promised away: a client that writes no registry record (another SDK host's print-mode process) is invisible to MAWS and to the engine alike (pass-2 item 10), and a terminal that starts between the fresh read and the launch is caught only by the engine's own `--bg` refusal; both accepted under D-12. Binding as verified.
  - *Archive, unarchive, delete*: archive is refused for a foreign holder, `session.stop { reason: 'archive' }` when live (never under `onlyIfIdle`, the cleanup step's precondition, which answers `skipped_live` instead), `archived: true`, tabs closed, the sheet naming the session's running background tasks and workflows before the human confirms (§4.4: exit never silently discards background work), the cleanup dialog offered afterwards for the last session of a managed worktree, the store file kept so an unarchived session resumes with its queue and grants; unarchive is C4's `session.update { archived: false }` from a per-project "Archived sessions" dialog (C5's sidebar hides archived rows and gains no section); delete conversation is refused for a foreign holder and for a MAWS-live session unless "Stop and delete", moves nothing unless `session.stop` confirmed the exit (`{ exited: false }` answers `stop_unconfirmed`) and a fresh holder read is clean, enumerates the transcript, its sidecar directory, its `.precompact.json` and `.ccr-tip.json` siblings and its `<id>-<stamp>.cast` recordings into a persisted manifest, moves them to the Trash (`shell.trashItem`) with the transcript last so a failure half-way leaves the conversation discoverable and answers `partial` with the failed path, then forgets the store file and journal and removes the record; this Trash move is the only write C10 makes under `~/.claude`. Binding on the refusals, on "never silently" and on "whole or not at all" (D-12), advisory on the Trash and the sheets.
  - *Rollback*: per resource, never forced, and never a transcript: a launch that wrote one under the pin has a conversation, so its record is kept and bound and the intent dropped (only the human's Delete moves a transcript); otherwise a record this attempt created is removed (C5 keeps the tab through C10's retention predicate); a worktree created here is removed only when clean (`discard: false`), its attempt-created branch then deleted only when it has no commits (`branchState`), and `progress.worktree` cleared so the next attempt starts under the initial rules; else it is kept and reported (`remove_refused` with the count, `remove_failed` with git's message) with `progress.worktree` retained, so a retry passes retry validation (identity verified, no exists-checks) and reuses it, a `location` patch is refused `retained_worktree`, and the failure card offers discard with the acknowledgement or "Keep the worktree" (detached from the attempt: the location becomes that existing worktree); `session.forget` runs only when the intent is discarded, so the journal of a failed attempt survives as evidence; C4's watcher transitions during the operation are ignored and the record's final state is the service's. Binding (a rollback that forces would violate §4.5's "safe cleanup").
  - *Rename with provenance*: a live session is renamed through C3's `session.renameSession`, so the engine's store carries it and C4's index reads it back as the engine's fact; a session with no live process gets C4's override (`session.update { title }`), which a later terminal `/rename` supersedes under C4's title rule; no write-through on the next resume (it would append duplicate `custom-title` records to every resumed session). Binding (C4's rule), advisory on the omission.
  - *Foreign-live and attach*: `session.start` answers `held` for `terminal` and `background_job` holders from the fresh read (MAWS's own guard, since the engine has none for interactive or print-mode holders, pass-2 item 10) and the engine's refusal for a `--bg` holder (`is running as a background session (<short>)`, `pass2/resume-live-bg.jsonl` seq 5) is mapped to the same outcome when MAWS's read lost the race; "Attach in a terminal tab" opens C9's terminal with `command` = the pinned binary path, single-quoted, plus ` attach <short>`, in the job's cwd, from the session pane's holder notice and the row menu, never from the Overview card; "Fork a copy" is the escape hatch for any holder. Binding as verified.
  - *Grant snapshot hook*: resume keeps the snapshot by app id (C3 re-applies it), a fork inherits the source's through `inheritGrantsFrom`, archive keeps the store file, delete forgets it; nothing else copies or edits a snapshot. Binding (X6 additive).
  - *Setup fields*: location (project directory · new worktree with name, branch new or existing, and start-from · existing worktree from `git.worktree.list`) and add directory as the location chips above the box; permission mode and model with effort in the toolbar row below (model from `engine.models`, a host-side catalogue cached across launches, built-in before the first, with the "cannot hold auto mode" note where `supportsAutoMode` is false and the local-lane note on `claude/*` gateway models); inference (relay default, Direct per session) in the status line and the relay card, never a chip (§5.4); the first message is the composer's text, sent by main after `ready`; validation as the intent patch rules; the trust card lists the files trust would load, read from the directory itself; the relay card offers "Open ClaudeUsage, then start" (C5 action `gateway.openClaudeUsage`, registered by C11, disabled until then; the card polls the relay and starts by itself once it answers) and "Run direct"; the record exists from the real launch and reads `starting` through C3's first status (no digest of C10's own). Binding on the fields and the two auto-mode notes (§5.1, §5.4, §19), advisory on layout beyond D-20's rule, which decides it.
  - *Additional directories and resume*: `--add-dir` is not persisted by the engine (SPEC §24 9), so the record's `additionalDirectories` is the source of truth and is re-passed on every resume; discovered sessions have none. Binding as verified.
  - *Acceptance mechanics*: one scratch clone of the owner's MAWS repository seeded with real sentinels (an allow rule, a `SessionStart` hook, a project `.mcp.json` server, a directory-marketplace plugin, tracked in a commit so worktrees carry them) plus a plain directory under `/private/tmp`, a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for every launch and probe (amended 2026-09-28, §19, the live-acceptance rule after C3's case 3.17: the owner's `~/.claude` and `~/.claude.json` are read only at teardown, to confirm no trace; trust is compared with the pinned binary's own init frame under the same scratch home after an explicit first turn, the full extraction equal), a throwaway `MAWS_USER_DATA`, a ledger of every id and directory created; teardown runs the engine's `claude project purge` on the scratch paths first, while their transcripts still exist for it to discover, then removes the per-session artifacts of the one session MAWS deleted, then the clone, and distinguishes the engine's documented global leftovers from acceptance residue. Binding on never touching the owner's live checkouts and on the purge-before-delete order, advisory on mechanics.
  - *Testing*: vitest node for trust, launch mapping, the models cache, the worktree service, holder authority and the cleanup table over scratch repositories and a fake registry, and the session service over a scripted fake host, a temp index and a temp config home; vitest happy-dom for the hook, the cards and the sheets; Playwright over the built app with C3's replay transport (`MAWS_E2E_REPLAY_FIXTURES`, one fixture per launch in order, each launch pinned to its fixture's recorded id, the first `system/init` row's or, for a no-turn fixture, the `--session-id` pin its `spawn` row preserves: `trust-bootstrap-recorded` for the bootstrap, `auto-routed` for a real launch, `fork-pinned-id-{start,fork}` for a fork, so identities are distinct and the untrusted case reaches its recorded `true`; one case sends the fixture's own first prompt through the composer), a temp config home with a synthetic `.claude.json`, and scratch repositories; the live layer is the acceptance itself. Advisory.

### C11: Palette widgets, settings, gateway and usage — plan

- **Purpose:** the terminal's menu commands as widgets over the transcript, backed by the engine's reads and controls: model and effort (with gateway models and fast mode), permissions and rules, usage and context, config, plugins with reload, MCP status and reconnect, sandbox, hooks, memory, skills, agents, status, compact, rename, resume, export, diff; the settings screens (engine path and version, gateway default and per-session Direct option, keyboard map, theme, notification policy); the ClaudeUsage integration: usage windows, the accounts view with the routing account marked and sign-in handed off to ClaudeUsage, relay health (D-19); Session info shows the auto-mode lane from `get_status`, and the model picker notes on GPT-routed models that auto-mode reviews run locally on Sonnet 5 and count against usage.
- **Acceptance:** every widget listed opens from the palette and shows live engine data; changes made in a widget are reflected by the next init frame or read; switching a session to Direct restarts it with a visible notice; a launch with the relay stopped offers ClaudeUsage or Direct instead of failing; the accounts view matches ClaudeUsage's panel; usage windows match ClaudeUsage's panel.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C3, C7. blocks: C12.
- **Contracts:** consumes X2, X4, X6.
- **Design inheritance:** §3 palette list, §5.4 palette, §5.5, D-7, D-17 [binding for the list and sources, advisory for layout]; `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md` items 1, 2 and 13 (model catalogue flags, base URL from `get_status`, effective mode and refusal codes) [binding as verified]; the owner's screenshots of the terminal's menus in the vault note [advisory]; `mockups/06-accounts.html` (accounts view in ClaudeUsage's geometry, relay health states, Session info with the auto-mode lane, settings, the permissions widget as the pattern for engine-read widgets) and mockup 04's model widget and popovers [advisory, taste reference].
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** landed 2026-09-29, merged to master at b93ef742 from 8cee6352 (155 commits over f5555c4, master merged in four times and never rebased; `pnpm verify` exit 0 on the final code ba76a470: 4,269 unit tests, e2e 26, packaged 26, transcript 9, terminal 13, panel 23, c7 25, code 24, agents 18, widgets 15, settings 5; live 3/3 through the relay on C10's "+" scratch sessions, about US$0.67; eighteen shots `docs/doperpowers/plans/shots/c11-*.png`; every milestone, the C10 integration and the whole branch reviewed on opus, gateway rate limited, each clean after its fix waves). Owner's hands: ClaudeUsage activating on the hand-off; the Accounts tab beside ClaudeUsage's panel; ⌘W, ⌘K and ⌘1 recorded as chords read `Already bound to …` and nothing closes; the owner's own rules, MCP servers, plugins and hooks through `~/.claude` in a `pnpm dev` session; the Direct `Login` rows on a logged-in engine. Residue amended below at closure (2026-09-29): the reveal guard, `appearance.monoFont`, `layout.captureChords`, two widget-contract rules, the local-lane observation, the unstarted tab's route segment and the switch's relay precheck.
- **Residue decisions (C11, 2026-09-28; amended at closure 2026-09-29):** what the design left to the child, settled here; the execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c11-palette-settings-gateway-usage.md` carries the widget table, the interfaces, the milestones and the gates.
  - *Widget contract*: every one of the twenty `WIDGET_COMMANDS` names is registered through C7's `registerSlashWidget` from one `registerAllWidgets()` (closure amendments: a typed `/<widget> <args>` whose argument resolves applies through the widget's own control; a widget opened after the session's latest event reads again), rendered inside a `WidgetFrame` (head with the `/name`, body, foot `Read with <control> · <edited elsewhere>`), backed by exactly the read or control the execution document's table names (`list_models`, `set_model`, `apply_flag_settings { effortLevel | fastMode }`, `list_permission_rules`, `set_permission_mode`, `get_usage`, `get_context_usage`, `get_settings`, `system/init.plugins` with `reload_plugins`, `mcp_status` with `mcp_reconnect` and `mcp_toggle`, `get_sandbox_dialog`, `get_hooks_listing`, `get_memory_dialog`, `get_skills_dialog` then the catalogue, the catalogue's agents, `get_status`, `/compact` as a headless user message, C10's rename, a project session list for resume, `export_conversation`, `get_workspace_diff`), showing the engine's effective value after a change (the next init frame or read) from C3's result values (`setModel`'s `{ ok, error, code }`, `setPermissionMode`'s `{ effective, refusal, error }`, the void answers of `setEffort` and `applyFlagSettings`, `OperationReceipt` then `session.operation`), the engine's code in words on refusal with the prior value kept, and transport or timeout errors (`IpcError`) as a separate branch that infers nothing; MAWS itself writes nothing on disk under `~/.claude` or `~/.claude.json`: rules, settings, hooks, memory, plugin state, `.mcp.json` and sandbox are shown with the sentence naming where they are edited, and every control is audited as session-only (`set_model`, `apply_flag_settings`, `set_permission_mode`, `reload_plugins`, `mcp_reconnect`) except `mcp_toggle`, which the engine persists for the project in `~/.claude.json` (`enabledMcpServers` / `disabledMcpServers`, the terminal's own `/mcp` write) and which the widget therefore discloses as persistent beside the switch and in its foot, naming the file (architect's ruling 2026-09-28: a control the engine persists on MAWS's request is allowed, like an approval card's "always allow"); and because that write precedes the engine's connection attempt or cleanup (`cli.pretty.js` 2.1.283:583734, 583780), a failed toggle is never assumed to have left the state where it stood: after either outcome the widget refetches `mcp_status` and reads the next status, shows an enable that persisted but did not connect as on and not connected, and shows `Unconfirmed` when the read cannot establish the result. Reason: D-12, §4.2's "the palette reads init", and the terminal's `/permissions`, `/status`, `/plugins`, `/sandbox`, `/hooks`, `/memory`, `/skills`, `/export`, `/diff` and `/resume` having no headless twin (SPEC 28 §22) while their reads exist. Binding on the read-or-control per name, on "MAWS writes nothing itself" and on the persistence disclosure; advisory on layout and copy.

Reason: the wire carries no provider on a catalogue row and ClaudeUsage's hard-coded catalogue settles the gateway group; `supportsAutoMode` is absent rather than false on Haiku; effort has no subtype of its own; and the fixtures show the lane flip that the warning names. Binding as verified; advisory on copy.

vitest node for the migration, the patch merge, `engine.configure`, the launcher with `execFile` mocked, the reveal guard, `saveTextFile` and the gateway action through the real command validator; Playwright over the `#widgets` and `#panel` harnesses in two groups: the interactive widget cases on a fixture-backed command backend (`test/support/widget-backend.ts`: reads and controls answered from named fixture lines by subtype, in any order, installed in main through C5's `interceptCommand` under `MAWS_WIDGET_BACKEND=fixtures`; each case asserts the widget's intended call payload in the backend's log), and replay-conformance cases on C3's strict replay in which a scripted X2 driver, not the widgets, issues each fixture's recorded outbound frames in order with the recorded arguments (`reads.jsonl` seq 4–89 from its initial prompt; `test/fixtures/engine/auto-routed-controls.jsonl`, `pass2/auto-routed` with the unissuable `fork_conversation` pair omitted under a `meta provenance` row, seq 6–185) while the widgets, mounted passively, render the recorded answers; each spec its own `MAWS_USER_DATA` and each record a distinct engine session id; the redacted ClaudeUsage directory for the accounts cases; the live acceptance through the relay with ClaudeUsage's panel beside the app. Reason: C3's replay holds at the next recorded `out` row and ends on any unrecorded write, so a widget's own reads (`list_permission_rules` never recorded, `detail: 'full'` where `summary` was, a refetch on a status change) cannot be what drives it; the two harnesses therefore prove different things, behaviour and payloads on the backend, frame-for-frame fidelity on the replay. Advisory.

### C12: Finish — decomposing at dispatch

- **Purpose:** what makes P0 a daily driver (amended 2026-09-29 by the owner's decisions, §19): the memory budget policy under the amended §4.4 list with Send resuming a released session; MAWS-owned custody of the pinned engine and restart coordination; a Worktrees… dialog on the project menu; the finish sweep (the launch-environment probe and decision, split-pane items the owner names after the sitting; the title-rule defect went back to debt wave 4 with the review rulings, §19); the agents' pre-run of §10 under a scratch config home producing the owner's sitting script and evidence pack; and the recomposition run against §10. Dropped from P0: the prewarmed spare (P1, as §3 already had it), signing, notarization and the updater (distribution is owner-only for this release; keyboard map editing is C11's).
- **Cut (2026-09-29):** one plan-grain leaf, since owner-only distribution removes the release child's separate state owner; milestones after the document's review split the budget in three: M1 budget policy in the engine host (with the exited-history cap and the X1, X2, X6 shapes), M2 released sessions in the product (Send-to-resume first), M3 the measured run, M4 engine custody and restart coordination with the widened quit question, M5 finish sweep (the Worktrees… dialog, the launch-environment probe, the split-pane placeholder), M6 the §10 pre-run and the owner's script; the closing act (the owner's sitting, C8's and the charter's recomposition, §20) is the architect's. Grounding: `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-29-c12-grounding.md`.
- **Acceptance:** closes with §10 verified in one sitting; the agents' pre-run of every §10 item passed under a scratch home 2026-09-30 (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/c12/prerun.txt`, each item `both`), the owner's sitting pending.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: C8, C10, C11, debt wave 4 (on master before the executor is dispatched). blocks: none.
- **Contracts:** consumes all.
- **Design inheritance:** §4.4 policy [binding shape], §4.7 and §12 C1 *Packaging skeleton* and §3 P0 *Quit and reload safety* (the earlier "§3 P0 packaging line" pointed at nothing), `research/landscape.md` Part D [advisory].
- **Required:** required.
- **Status:** cut 2026-09-29; execution document `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-29-c12-finish.md` authored (e3720701), adversarially reviewed (five P1s, twelve P2s, all ruled and fixed, 4ba56f1a), re-reviewed once (two P1s, fixed 03dcd090), review closed; executor dispatched 2026-09-29 after C11's merge and debt wave 4; landed 2026-09-30 (merged to master at 12dc1d08 from d7fda71a, §19 "C12 landed"); the C3 handshake corrective landed a2b88bde the same day; the owner's sitting is next.
- **Residue decisions (C12, 2026-09-29; applied at landing 2026-09-30):** *Budget* (binding shape, §4.4): evaluation runs at C3's 10 s sample and at a policy change; the sum covers MAWS's live trees that are not already exiting, as `ps` measures them; hard reasons are §4.4's amended list, host work in flight (`busy`) and stateful servers by exact name, connected or pending; a candidate is also not engine-busy (`running` or `requires_action`; `unknown` never blocks) and quiet for one sample period, which a policy change cannot shorten; one release per evaluation and none while one is exiting, least recently used first by the host's `lastUsedAt`, evaluated and stopped in one synchronous step; an explicit stop, archive or quit overrides a release in flight; the default is 25% of `os.totalmem()`, with a setting in MiB and a stateful-servers list, both in settings version 4. *Released state* (binding for surfaces): the exit reason `budget` on X1 and X6, never on the wire (a release sends `end_session { reason: 'stop' }`); the mark lives on main's digest, not in the index (D-16), survives the index's `exited` seeds, drops when a terminal or background job takes the session, and clears on the next launch and on archive; it shows as one muted word on the row and card, a line in Session info and quiet transcript copy, with no attention item; Send resumes through C10's resume path via a C7 seam. *Over budget with no candidate* (binding): one banner per crossing, on the host's two-sample flag; the per-session stop is a sheet with C10's disclosure over `session.stop { reason: 'stop' }`. *Exited history* (advisory values): an exited stream keeps 500 events; the host keeps 24 exited rows, exempting an exited row that still holds a queue (what the list shows from disk stays editable; the whole-branch review's fix); older reads rebuild from the journal or the disk path. *Custody* (binding): a copy under `<userData>/engine/<version>/claude` with a manifest and SHA-256, made at pin time, verified at start, made again from a verifying source, and otherwise refused by main when a setup or resume begins, once custody and main's first configure push have answered (30 s bounds, failing closed), in words that name reinstalling the version or choosing a binary path; main is the only sender of `engine.configure`, and a pin change runs custody before its push; the installer's directory is read-only; an owner-chosen path is used as chosen; tests and replay bypass custody. *Quit* (binding): the busy report counts mid-turn sessions, host work and engine state, sessions with background work and busy terminals, forgets cached engine statuses on a host generation change, is single-flight and offers quit-when-idle; the guard always holds the quit while the report is computed; the `Restart` wording is ready for P1's updater. *Distribution* (binding for P0): `build:app`, development-signed when `MAWS_SIGN_IDENTITY` names an identity, else unsigned; P1-R1 to R4 written and deferred. *Worktrees…* (advisory layout, D-20): Codex's Settings › Worktrees rows with C10's states, occupancy and ladder, plus `New session here`; a claimed checkout is busy; creating a worktree writes the engine's `.claude/worktrees/` block into the common dir's `info/exclude` once, serialized per common dir. *Launch environment* (binding): X6's three lines.

## 13. Cross-child contracts

- **X1 Event graph and session record** (owner C1 for the types; C3 and C4 produce; everyone consumes). Identifiers kept distinct and never flattened: session id, message uuid, API message id, tool-use id, task id, agent id, parent tool-use id, request id. Normalised event kinds: session lifecycle, turn lifecycle (a turn carries its origin, so an engine-initiated turn after a workflow or background completion is distinguishable from one the human started), assistant block delta and complete, user message (with replay and bash tags), tool use, tool result (with spill path), tool progress, hook lifecycle and host hook callbacks, permission request and resolution, question request and answer, plan request and outcome, dialog and elicitation, subagent lifecycle and message, task lifecycle and notification, workflow progress, compaction, reset, retraction and rewind boundaries (rewind is app-synthesised from a successful `rewind_conversation`; the engine emits no frame for it), usage and context sample, error and reconnect, process exit. Transcript records, the engine's on-disk JSONL, are a separate loose type read as the parent chain from the latest leaf (§4.3). Session record: app id, engine session id, project id, directory (from the first record), worktree and branch, title with source (user, generated, tool), pin, archive state, holder (self, terminal pid, background job, none), last-seen cursor, unread flag, engine version, gateway state, model and effort, permission mode as requested and as effective with the refusal code when they differ. Attention item: id, session id, kind (permission, question, plan, dialog, elicitation, auto-mode unavailable, error, interrupted, held peer message), request reference, created at, state (pending, delivered, confirmed, retired). Denial kinds travel on tool results (`user-rejected`, `permission-rule`, `automode-blocked`, `automode-unavailable`, `automode-parsing-error`, `interrupted`, `cancelled`): `automode-blocked` is informational and counts in the turn divider and its cell as "auto-denied"; `automode-unavailable` stops the turn and is the Needs-you kind above, whose remedy is switching out of auto mode; server-lane allow verdicts are attributable after all (each `message_delta.safeguard_results` entry carries `evaluated/not_flagged` keyed by tool-use id), so tool events carry a safeguard field (`not_evaluated`, `not_flagged`, `flagged`); it is shown in cell detail and the session's turn summary, never as a per-cell badge, so an auto-mode session reads as quietly as D-18 intends. Binding: without one graph the transcript, the panels and the attention model would each invent their own keys. Canonical once landed: `src/shared/graph/*.ts` (ids, enums, events, session record, attention item) and `src/shared/wire/*.ts` (frames, control payloads, journal rows, transcript records); until then, the Interfaces section of C1's execution document. C12 additive (2026-09-29, landed 12dc1d08): `process.exited.reason` gains `budget` with a `budget` field naming the release, MAWS's own reason and never on the wire (a release sends `end_session { reason: 'stop' }`; an archive keeps its `archive`); `SessionDigest.released` (optional, `{ at, limitKiB }`; main's digest only, never the persisted record; applied once per release keyed on the launch, cleared by the next launch, a foreign holder or archive). Debt wave 4 (2026-09-29, landed a5ae05c2): `assistant.block.complete.supersedes` is `MessageUuid[]`, the message uuids the block replaces (refusal fallback, SPEC 45: every block frame of the refused attempt, on the replacement's first frame only), empty otherwise; C3 emits the wire's list, C4's backfill sends `[]`, C6 drops every named uuid's rows; pinned synthetically until a live refusal fallback is recorded. A disk-form `user.message` may carry `origin: 'coordinator'` with `isSynthetic: true`: a relayed message queued for a live child, which C4's backfill maps from the child transcript's `queued_command` attachment; it opens no turn and renders no row, and C8b's relay store keys `delivered` on it. W7 (2026-10-01, landed 8768e5bf): the record's holder `self` may carry `contestedBy`; the glyph vocabulary gains contested (⇄, warning), derived once for row, tab and card.
- **X2 IPC schema** (owner C1). Namespaced commands and events with runtime validation on both sides: `session.*`, `engine.*`, `fs.*`, `git.*`, `pty.*`, `layout.*`, `attention.*`, `settings.*`. Each registry entry names its owner (main, the engine host or the PTY host) so main routes a command without a second registry; the preload bridges only registered names; main validates command inputs and the events it emits; the renderer validates command outputs and the events it receives, and drops an invalid event rather than deliver it. The renderer never receives shell, git or engine handles. Binding: the security boundary and every child's integration surface. Canonical once landed: `src/shared/ipc/schema.ts` and `src/shared/ipc/host-protocol.ts`; until then, C1's execution document. The reconciled wave-2 command and event surface is recorded in `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-wave2-interface-ledger.md` until the code lands, and children extend X1, X3 and X7 additively as the ledger's section (b) lists. Additive 2026-09-28 from C8 (flagged, not departed): `files.*` (owner main: `list`, `read`, `watch`, `unwatch`, `openExternal`; event `files.changed`; `read` and `list` bound to the opened descriptor, `openExternal` path-checked only) from C8c; `git.status.remoteUrl` (C4); C5's three layout asks (panel follows `file`/`diff`/`agent` tabs; `openTab` refreshes an open file tab's line; `panelTabsOf`/`updatePanelTabs`); the `openFile` adapter carries `appSessionId`. C12 additive (2026-09-29, landed 12dc1d08): `engine.policy.set` and the `engine.budget` event (the host's sampled sum, the budget, the debounced over-with-no-candidate flag); `settings.engineCustody`, answering `Custody | null` (null when custody does not run, under tests and replay); `settings.engineApplied` (main answers once its `engine.configure` push for the current settings has landed, custody for a changed pin included; main is the only sender of that push, which the renderer no longer invokes); settings version 4 (`engine.memoryBudgetMiB`, `engine.statefulMcpServers`); the launch refusal answered by main when custody is not `ok`; `layout.quitWait` (command and event, `{ waiting: BusyReport | null }`, where `BusyReport` is `{ midTurn, backgroundWork, busyTerminals, terminalsUnknown }`) and `layout.quitWaitAnswer { action: 'quitNow' | 'cancel' }`, the channel from main's quit guard to the Quit-when-idle banner. Each C12 entry's name is in its `src/shared/ipc/names/<ns>.ts` list. Debt wave 5A (2026-09-30, landed 5d52598d), additive: `engine.models` takes an optional `cwd` beside `gateway`, the directory whose settings the launch will run with; the host answers the catalogue keyed by the model environment those settings produce (`<route>/env-<digest>` when the project, local or flag settings change it; the bare route key otherwise, so every existing entry still answers), else the route's, else the built-in list. Debt wave 5C (2026-09-30, landed e6814b0a), additive: `session.appEvents` (owner main) answers the app-synthesised events main keeps for a session (at most 20, picked from its own `session.eventBatch` by `isAppSynthesised`, dropped with the record), so a page that missed the batch shows C10's route notice at its time after a reload. Native sitting correction (2026-09-30, ffdfcdb9), additive: `layout.selectAll` is a main-to-renderer event with an empty strict payload. The native Edit menu runs the ordinary editor selection command and emits this event for the app page; only the terminal containing DOM focus selects its own buffer. The event grants no new filesystem or engine access. W1 additive (2026-10-01, landed c22771b7): `session.queue.hold { messageUuid }` ("Hold until the turn ends" on a waiting `next` steer: the engine's `cancel_async_message { message_uuid }` withdraws it and the app queue appends it; C1's `CancelAsyncMessageRequest` field is `message_uuid`, corrected from `uuid`); `session.interrupt` gains optional `holdEngineQueued` (the plain stop: ⌘., the Stop button and a route switch pause the app queue, withdraw every waiting `next` steer into it in write order under one 2 s deadline, then interrupt, because the engine starts a waiting steer the moment the turn is interrupted; Esc and a row's Send now interrupt without it; `cancelEngineQueued: true` stays the drop path); the queue state answered by `session.queue.list` and carried by the `session.queue` event gains optional `steers` (the `next` steers written and not yet started) and `withdrawn` (the uuids held back that way, whose cancelled bubble the transcript hides). W2 additive (2026-10-01, landed 15d50cb5): settings version 5 adds `appearance.transcriptWidth` (768, 960 or 1280; migrated from version 4 with 768), which the renderer applies as the root `--measure`; the next setting bumps to version 6. W3 additive (2026-10-01, landed e7edb59d): `settings.userEngineDefaults` (owner main) answers the user's `model`, `effortLevel` and `permissions.defaultMode` read from the config home's `settings.json`, never written; settings version 6 adds `permissions.autoFallback` (any engine mode but auto, acceptEdits by default; migrated from version 5); the next setting bumps to version 7. W5 (2026-10-01, landed d6ddf6d6): `layout.*` regions go to version 2 (`sidebar.openProject`, a project id, `'other'` or null, replacing `collapsedProjects` and `collapsedSections`; `overview`, the floating Overview's rect or null; version 1 migrated on read, pre-hydration region changes replayed); `TabRef` loses the `overview` kind, a pane may be empty, `LAYOUT_DEFAULTS.main` is one empty pane, and a stored `main.tabs` holding the Overview's tab is read without it; `session.create` takes an optional `location` (a worktree group's "+"); the `topBarEnd` slot is W6's mount point. W7 (2026-10-01, landed 8768e5bf), additive: `SessionRecord.holder.self` gains optional `contestedBy` (a foreign holder sharing a session MAWS runs); `fs.holders` and `fs.holderChanged` name foreign holders only (a registry record whose entrypoint is `sdk-ts` and whose pid is in the process table is MAWS's own; rows carry the record's `entrypoint`); the event `fs.foreignWrite { sessionId, appSessionId, holder }`, once per contest at the foreign process's first record, on which main yields (a notice, the queue paused while it holds messages, a stop once idle); `session.events` reads the transcript for an exited process whose session a foreign process held at or after the exit; `session.stop` gains `ifIdle` (decided in the host, else `skipped: true`); `session.takeOver { appSessionId, holder }` (owner main) answers `released` or `refused { reason, message }`, signalling only a terminal's interactive holder (another app's engine is refused `other_app`), after which the renderer resumes with `session.start`; settings version 7 adds `engine.memoryBudget.enabled` (on by default, migrated from 6) and `engine.policy.set` and the budget state gain `enabled`. W6 (2026-10-01, landed dc639473), additive except the retired tab: the `settings.usage` snapshot and `settings.usageChanged` gain `gateway` (`gateway.json`'s `routingEnabled` and port), `codex` (the Codex accounts, their saved sessions and the drain target the per-model `codex-pins.<model>.json` agree on) and `activity` (the last ten minutes of `gateway-requests.jsonl`, per account and as the health line), each null when its file is absent; MAWS still only reads ClaudeUsage's files; `TabRef` loses the `accounts` kind and a stored `main.tabs` holding it is read without it; the `accounts.open` action keeps its id (keymap overrides key on it), retitled Usage, and opens the pill's popover; the popover's account actions render disabled through `src/renderer/src/usage/controls.ts`, the one layer a ClaudeUsage control route replaces. W6 route half (2026-10-01, landed b4c7b47a), additive: `settings.usageControls` (owner main) answers `{ available, reason, state }` from ClaudeUsage's loopback control API (`GET /claudeusage/state` with the bearer token read from `<data dir>/control-token`; `no_route` for an absent token, 404 or a refused connection, `bad_token` for 401, `starting` for 503, `timeout` after 1.5 s), re-checked when the usage snapshot changes and when the popover opens; `settings.usageAction { action: 'route' | 'add' | 'reconnect' | 'remove' | 'panel', uuid?, email?, confirm? }` answers the API's result, a 409's `message` shown as the popover's notice; the URL override exists only under `MAWS_E2E` and only for plain loopback http, so the token never leaves the machine; per-account availability follows the API's `canRoute`, `canReconnect`, `canRemove`; a confirmed Route holds its answered drain target over the file snapshots until the reader names it or the account disappears.
- **X3 Presentation model** (owner C6). Row types: time separator, user, assistant text, work divider, activity group, cell, cell detail, change receipt, subagent row, agent message row, thinking, plan, question (historical), request card slot, error, boundary, replayed bash. Each tool event carries at derivation time: groupable or standalone, category in the fixed summary order (MCP sources, loaded skills, file changes, exploration, commands, web), denial marker with its denial kind, subagent lifecycle kind, group-start marker. Binding: C7's cards and C8's drill-in render the same rows. Additive 2026-09-28 from C8: `TranscriptSource.onReset`, and the child `ipcSource` owns disk-versus-wire provenance per child (C8's ask of C6). Debt wave 4 (2026-09-29, a37d35f1), additive: the transcript state carries the session's `cwd` (from `session.started` or `session.init`), against which a Grep scope is resolved before it counts as a read; the user row carries `placeholders`, the places where a pasted-text placeholder was actually inserted, so copy and `serializeRows` substitute only there. W1 (2026-10-01, landed c22771b7), additive except the retired cell: a user row may carry `pending: 'next' | 'held'` (the dimmed tail bubble under the turn, captioned "Sends at the next step" or "Sends when this turn ends"); an error row may carry `scope: 'hook'` and `action: 'edit_prompt'` (a hook that got in the way: "A hook blocked your prompt" with Edit prompt, "Hook re-prompted Claude", or a line naming the hook); the `hook` cell detail and the "ran N hooks" phrase are retired (successful and running hooks make no row, so they no longer count as work and the turn's final text is the answer); a turn's rows are one stream in event order in which steers, hook notices, boundaries, local-command output and the answer stay in view when the divider folds the rest (`isPersistentRow`); raw HTML renders as literal text (the parser's HTML constructs are off) and a slash-command record folds to one line; `TranscriptActions` gains `editPrompt`. W4 (2026-10-01, landed f80c8f66), additive: the `question` row is persistent (`isPersistentRow`, in view when the turn folds) while still counting as work for the answer's start, and renders as one line per question ("header or question → answer", Skipped and Not answered kept); C6's `EditDetail` exports `EditDiff` for the receipt's hover diff and `displayPath` renders paths relative to the session's directory.
- **X4 Request card** (owner C7). One component for permission, question, plan, dialog and elicitation, taking an X1 attention item and an answer callback, placed docked at the composer, in an Overview card, in a hover card (read-only tools, click only), and inline. Binding: the same decision must look and behave identically wherever it appears. W4 (2026-10-01, landed f80c8f66), additive: the question card steps one question at a time (a "{n} of {N}" pager, Next after every question (no auto-advance, owner's ruling 2026-10-01), Skip, Submit on the last step); its draft lives beside the request in the attention store's `drafts` map, keyed by the item and dropped when it closes, so every placement resumes it; `questionAnswers` returns the answered questions only and the one `{ kind: 'question', answers }` reply leaves a skipped question out, an empty map when every one is skipped, which the pinned engine accepts without error and reports to the model as unanswered (`pass3/w4-question-partial`, `w4-question-none`); Esc still sends the plain deny; the permission card's `local_settings` detail names `.claude/settings.local.json`.
- **X5 Panel host and tool registry** (owner C8; C8a implements the host; C8b, C8c, C9, C11 implement tools). Final 2026-09-28 in the C8 composite spec (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c8-right-panel.md`, "X5, final"), canonical at `src/renderer/src/panel/tool.ts` and `host.ts` once C8a lands (landed with C8a at b360711). A tool declares `id`, `title`, `icon`, `pinnable`, `multiInstance`, optional `menu` (false hides it from "+"; Log), `createState(init)` with a typed `params`, optional `open(state, params)` for single-instance re-targeting, `beforeClose` (a veto), `dispose`, `render`, and an optional `summary` section; the context carries a nullable `appSessionId` and `session`, C3's `status`, `pinned`, `visible`, `focusRequest`, `setTitle`, `close` and `openTool`. The host owns tab order, pin, maximize, the "+" menu and the resting summary; keeps instance state outside React across visibility, session switches and pins; writes persisted refs only through C5's layout store (`onChange` for the shown session, `updatePanelTabs(appSessionId, fn)` for a target session). Registry: `registerPanelTool(tool)` and `panelHost.addTool(toolId, { appSessionId, params?, pin?, activate? })`, where `appSessionId` is the target session and `activate: false` adds without switching. Persistence: `PanelTabRef { id, toolId, pinned }` is the whole persisted state; a tool whose params are its identity is transient, never pinned and dropped at restoration (Log in P0). Binding: tools land in four waves against one host. Two binding rules from C8a's closure, recorded at the C8 recomposition (2026-09-29): `dispose` releases everything an instance owns, a running shell included; unregistering a tool closes its tabs through the host's `closeTab`, and a `beforeClose` veto keeps the tab. W4 (2026-10-01, landed f80c8f66), additive: `addTool` with `activate`, and every activation it makes, un-collapses a hidden right panel.
- **X6 Launch contract** (owner C3; C10 extends). Absolute binary path, the flag set of §4.1, the constructed environment allowlist, initialize fields (hooks, `perTaskStopAffordance`, `supportedDialogKinds` listing at least `auto_mode_server_fallback` and `refusal_fallback_prompt`, `workspaceTrust`), the D-19 gateway variables by default, flag settings (dialog expiry, re-applied grants, and for direct launches the empty-string `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` env override that defeats the owner's `settings.json` routing), `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` per session, resume and fork flags, and for prewarmed spares `claim_session` with `workspace_trust`. The initialize response's `current_permission_mode` and each `system/init` frame's permission mode are the effective mode; `set_permission_mode` refusals carry a code (`auto_mode_settings`, `auto_mode_circuit_breaker`, `auto_mode_fast_mode`, `auto_mode_model`, `auto_mode_unavailable`) the UI must surface. Binding: verified behaviour depends on the exact set, and auto mode routed through the gateway is the owner's mainline path. Additive 2026-09-28 from C8: `EngineSessionStatus.taskListId: string | null` (C3). C12 additive (2026-09-29, landed 12dc1d08): `EngineSessionStatus.keepAlive` (the amended §4.4 hard reasons), `busy` (host work in flight: the queue's busy predicate, a pending `!` command, a held clear-and-implement), `exit.release`, `lastUsedAt`; the budget's candidate rule rules a row out only on `running` or `requires_action` (`unknown` never blocks; the quiet check, which a policy change cannot shorten, and the `turn` hard reason cover the follow-up window; c9ed8444, 4df238d9). The launch environment (C12 M5, ruled 2026-09-30, §19), three lines: (1) `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=true` stays in the constructed environment, as the mirror of the owner's terminal's behaviour (D9): the owner's terminal sets it in neither the login shell nor settings, but 2.1.283 turns the fork gate on by default in an interactive session and off in print mode; with the variable, MAWS's print-mode launch offered the same Agent input schema as the terminal (no `run_in_background`) and ran the dispatch in the background as the terminal did, and without it the schema gained `run_in_background` and the dispatch ran in the foreground; C8b's foreground-child state therefore stays unreachable and its item 4(vii) stays as restated. Evidence: `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/pass3/launch-env-terminal.jsonl`, `launch-env-fork.jsonl`, `launch-env-unset.jsonl`. (2) `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS` stays unset: the owner's terminal sets it in neither the login shell nor settings, and on the owner's default model (`opus`, `claude-opus-5-5`, outside 2.1.283's task-tool allowlist) the interactive terminal offers no TaskCreate or TodoWrite, as MAWS's print-mode launch without the variable does; with it, print mode adds TaskCreate, TaskGet, TaskList and TaskUpdate, so Background work's checklist reads `No tasks` on such a model, as the terminal has none. Evidence: `launch-env-terminal.jsonl`, `launch-env-unset.jsonl`, `launch-env-todo.jsonl`. (3) the constructed environment sets `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS=1`, a host-facing wire option rather than terminal behaviour (§4.1's mirror rule does not apply): 2.1.283 sends `system/session_state_changed` (`running`, `requires_action`, `idle`) only under it; with it set, one turn with a permission request emitted `running`, `requires_action`, `running`, `idle`, and nothing else in the launch changed (the same other frame kinds, init fields, tool definitions, system prompt size and permission request). Evidence: `launch-env-state-events.jsonl` against `launch-env-state-events-unset.jsonl`. C3 corrective (2026-09-30, landed a2b88bde), additive: `LaunchSpec.retry?: { attempt, maxAttempts, reason }`, present only on main's single automatic relaunch after `init_failed { code: 'no_output' }`, the 30 s first-output bound of §12 C3. Debt wave 5A (2026-09-30, landed 5d52598d), a recorded fact and an accepted exposure: 2.1.283 picks a `--bg` job's daemon binary without PATH: an executable whose real path is under `$XDG_DATA_HOME/claude/versions/` runs the installer's launcher `~/.local/bin/claude` (the owner's newest installed version), and any other executable runs itself (`spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/pass3/bg-daemon-binary.jsonl`, five arms). Custody's copy lives under `<userData>/engine/<version>/`, so the packaged and dev builds' bg daemons run the pin; only `MAWS_E2E=1` live runs, which launch the installer's file, run their bg daemons on the installed version, and that is accepted (no PATH or `XDG_DATA_HOME` change in the constructed environment, since every tool the engine runs would inherit it). W3 additive (2026-10-01, landed e7edb59d): `LaunchSpec.permissionFallback`, set with an auto launch; when initialize answers another mode the host sets the fallback with `set_permission_mode` before ready and keeps auto requested with the refusal code (`auto_mode_model` or `auto_mode_unavailable`), so the record keeps auto and the chip shows the fallback with a notice (`pass3/w3-auto-fallback`: on Haiku initialize answers `default` silently and `set_permission_mode acceptEdits` succeeds).
- **X7 Theme tokens** (owner C1). The token names in `mockups/tokens.css`, light-first values from `tokens-light.css`, dark alternate; components use tokens only, either as `var(--token)` or as the Tailwind utility of the same name that C1's `@theme inline` map generates. Binding names, advisory values. Canonical once landed: `src/renderer/src/styles/tokens.css` (values; light under `:root`, dark under `[data-theme="dark"]`) and the `@theme` map in `src/renderer/src/styles/app.css` (utilities). Namespaced token additions such as C9's `--term-*` are part of the binding name set once a child lands them. W2 (2026-10-01, landed 15d50cb5): `--measure` is 768px in px (no longer `ch`) with `--measure-pad` 16px and `--measure-wide` (`--measure` + 128px) for a table or code block that breaks out, read through the one `.thread-column` class the transcript's rows and the composer column share; `--text-prose` 14px at `--lh-prose` 1.625, `--text-code` 12px, `--text-ui` 13px; new tokens `--bg-user-message` (the text colour at 5 percent), `--radius-2xl` (22px) and `--shadow-composer`.
- **X8 Fixtures and replay** (C2 delivers; C4 owns the engine). Fixture format is the probe's journal: one JSON object per line with sequence, direction, timestamp and frame; a scenario is a file; sidecars sit beside it. Replay feeds either the raw journal (C3's normaliser) or a JSONL transcript (C4's backfill). Binding: tests across children depend on it.

## 14. Ordering and dependency map

- Wave 1, now: C1 and C2 in parallel.
- Wave 2, after C1 (and C2 for C3): C3, C4, C5, C6, C9 in parallel. C5 and C6 build on fixtures until C3 and C4 land.
- Wave 3: C7 (after C3, C6) and C8a (after C5 and C6) in parallel; C8b (after C8a, C3, C6, C7) and C8c (after C8a, C4, C6) with wave 4 (C8 cut approved 2026-09-28, `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-28-c8-right-panel.md`).
- Wave 4: C10 (after C3, C4, C5, C9) and C11 (after C3, C7) in parallel.
- Wave 5: C12, then recomposition against §10.

Forced sequence: the fixtures gate the engine host; the transcript gates the composer's cards and the panel's drill-in; the terminal gates the attach affordance. Everything else is parallel by construction because the contracts in §13 are fixed here.

## 15. Risks and mitigations

- The wire changes under us with a new Claude Code release: the engine is pinned per session and the conformance tests (C3) run against fixtures; upgrading is a deliberate re-probe (C2's harness stays).
- Real settings make processes far heavier than the bare probe: C2 measures the tree; the interim never-exit rule keeps behaviour predictable until C12's budget.
- Foreign-live detection races a terminal start: C4 marks by holder file and process liveness, and C10 refuses to resume a session whose holder is alive.
- ghostty-web fails the acceptance list: C9's spike gate switches to xterm.js without touching the tool.
- Scene A drowns Scene B or the reverse: §10 requires both in one sitting; C5 and C7 are separate children so neither is starved.
- The transcript renderer is rebuilt when grouping lands: X3 freezes the classification fields before any renderer exists.

## 16. Deferred and out of scope

Deferred (may return): everything in §3 P1 and P2; the extension model of D-9; the detachable supervisor (D-16); the all-accounts usage view; dictation; the bottom dock; the topology view for subagents; the replay scrubber; workflow pause, skip and retry if the engine ever exposes them; per-message approval of held peer messages (needs an engine change).

Explicitly out of scope for P0: bundle patches (D-1); Windows and Linux (D-11); a second engine (D-7); reproducing the 307-method Desktop contract (the design keeps only the essential workstation architecture); cloud sessions, sharing and remote control.

Deferred, from the peer study (maws-90, 2026-09-28): computer use for Claude Code through `plugins/cua` (the source of truth for the plugin that hosts OpenAI's computer-use tool under a non-Codex host; published as github.com/SSFSKIM/cua by git subtree). When it returns, MAWS bundles `cua-shim.mjs` as an app resource and names it in its own `--mcp-config`, so the app never depends on the user's `~/.claude` plugins; `research/codex-computer-use/README.md` §8 lists what the host must provide and §11 the routes.
