## 18. Surprises and discoveries

- 2026-09-28, probe: AskUserQuestion travels over the permission channel; `fork_conversation` is unsupported (routed); resume does not replay history; child agents stream per message only; the project slug is the realpath; every first-pass scenario ran routed through the gateway because the shell exports the gateway variables.
- 2026-09-28, research: Codex's center tabs are one chat plus its content tabs, not multiple conversations; MAWS's multi-session tabs are an intentional extension. `inline-composer` in Codex is an image-edit prompt. Claude Desktop's `LocalSessions` has 307 methods and no IPC stores; Desktop is an SDK client with a large app-side layer.
- 2026-09-28, probe pass 2: the owner's `settings.json` `env` block routes a session through the gateway even when the process environment has no gateway variables; a matching ask rule outranks an allow rule; server-lane allow verdicts are visible in `safeguard_results`, contradicting the wire-gap report; a finished workflow or background task starts an engine-initiated turn; rewind branches the JSONL instead of truncating it; `bash_command` output is invisible to the model without the echo frame; Haiku cannot hold auto mode; an interrupted call is reported as `user-rejected`; the owner's Claude login had expired, which blocked every unrouted model turn.
- 2026-09-28, peer probe (maws-14): the auto-mode classifier has a server lane (Claude routes, free, model invisible) and a local lane (GPT routes or after fallback, Sonnet 5, counted as usage); the earlier "always Sonnet, uncharged" fact held only for the Claude-routed mainline.
- 2026-09-28, critique: session-scoped grants are not persisted for local sessions; there is no acknowledgement frame for a control response; print mode leaves project-scoped grants gated without a trust record; `perTaskStopAffordance` absent makes Stop kill background agents.
- 2026-09-28, probe pass 3 (`get_workspace_diff` on a dirty tree, `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass3-workspace-diff.md`): the read takes no parameters and its base branch is fixed when the engine starts, so a `checkout -b` plus a commit mid-session still answers `source {kind:'working-tree'}` and omits the branch's commits; untracked files whose mtime and ctime predate the engine start are omitted from `perFileStats` and `stats` entirely, contrary to the SPEC and the schema text; hunks are computed in-process in jsdiff's `structuredPatch` style (a pure addition is `oldStart:1, oldLines:0`), not by git; staged and unstaged edits are merged with nothing marking staged; a modified binary has no hunks entry, so "no hunks" has three causes (binary, `skippedLarge`, `restricted`); `stats.filesCount` counts untracked rows while the line counts cover tracked text only.
- 2026-09-28, C3 live acceptance: case 3.17's `session.setCwd` observation sent `trust_accepted: true` against the owner's real config home, and the engine persisted `hasTrustDialogAccepted: true` for three `maws-live-scratch-*` projects in `~/.claude.json`, a breach of the read-only rule; the file is the owner's to clean. Every abort result on the wire is `is_error: true`, so an attention item derived from `aborted_*` results fires on every Stop.

## 19. Decision log

- 2026-10-02: F5 executor — Writer ownership comes from the holder predicate (`sdk-ts` plus a process-table PID), shared by holder classification, transcript branch pinning and foreign-write detection. The pinned engine's transcript records omit PID (`spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/pass3/w7-collision-disk/transcript.jsonl`); a PID-less SDK record is own only when live registry evidence for its session and entrypoint maps exclusively to MAWS PIDs. Another SDK holder makes such writes conservatively foreign; explicit PIDs use the exact predicate. Architect approved.
- 2026-10-02: F9 executor — Git read paths suppress fsmonitor, hooks, external diff/textconv, signature verification, pagers and transports through the runner policy; configured content filters remain enabled so normalized/LFS content compares correctly. Filter executables come from user or repository Git config, not the tracked tree; per architect ruling this boundary requires no renderer trust gate. Trusted worktree creation retains checkout hooks, with timeout recovery after process-group termination. Branch deletion retains Git's checked-out-worktree guard and config cleanup; when a raced deletion is recovered by recreating the branch, its prior branch config is lost and the refusal discloses that residual.

- 2026-09-28: Verification call for this composite: each plan child ends with an independent review at the astra rung (reviewer-medium by default, reviewer-high for C3 and C4); the parent closes by recomposition against §10 with a scale review of the integrated app. Alternative rejected: per-child review only, which would miss the seams §10 exists to test.
- 2026-09-28: Cut into twelve children rather than six or the original six milestones; reason: ownership and verification seams (live wire versus disk, shell versus transcript versus composer, terminal's own spike), with C8 and C12 left coarse for the frontier rule. Owner approved.
- 2026-09-28: Tracking by this map plus ledgers, no ticket board; execution of wave 1 by plan-executor subagents from the architect session; model routing for executors prefers opus and fable unless astra-high fits (narrowed by the owner later on 2026-09-28: plan-executors and task-executors run on opus; per-task reviews use the plugin's `task-reviewer` agent rather than astra-medium; whole-branch reviews stay on reviewer-medium or reviewer-high; document authoring and fix waves may use fable); reviewers always astra. Owner decided.
- 2026-09-28: Critique round 1 adopted in full except finding 14 (usage placement kept per D-17); round 2 wording holds adopted in full. Rejected alternative in round 2: a fixed idle timer for process exit, replaced by the interim never-exit rule.
- 2026-09-28: D-18 recorded: auto mode default, few prompts expected; consequence: C2 runs in auto mode, C7's card and the hover card remain but the Overview and Needs-you copy assume questions and escalations. Owner's note.
- 2026-09-28: D-18 follow-up from the critic adopted: X6 lists the dialog kinds and the auto-mode refusal codes; X1 carries requested versus effective permission mode and an auto-mode-unavailable attention kind; denial kinds are distinguished (auto-denied counts in the divider, auto-mode unavailable is Needs-you); C2 probes auto mode routed and unrouted. Flags C1 (X1 types) and C2 (scope), both in flight and notified.
- 2026-09-28: Auto-mode classifier, verified (binary 2.1.283, two live probes through the owner's gateway, public docs; peer session maws-14, with C2's `safeguard_results` fixture agreeing): auto mode has two lanes. Claude-routed sessions through the owner's gateway get server-side review at no charge; the main request carries a `safeguards` field with the `dangerous-tool-use-2026-09-03` beta, the verdict arrives as `safeguard_results` on the main response's `message_delta`, and the model that produced it is chosen server-side and not client-visible; the gateway relays both fields untouched on Claude routes. GPT-routed sessions, or any session after a server fallback, use the client's own classifier requests. The lane is decided by the main model's provider, not by which accounts the gateway holds: the server verdict rides on the main model's own request and judges the tool calls that response contains, so a request served by a non-Anthropic provider can carry no Anthropic verdict (the gateway answers `unavailable`, reason `provider_not_anthropic`) and the client classifies locally through a Claude account instead. Local lane: Sonnet 5 by default (`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL` honoured, a feature-flag override may replace it), demoted once per process to Opus 5.5 for Fable mains if the first call is unavailable; these requests count as usage. Neither lane emits a classifier frame on the wire; `get_status` shows the lane as "Auto mode server: Enabled/Disabled", `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_SERVER=0` forces the local lane, and stream-json emits a `system` warning for the billing notice. The `auto_mode_server_fallback` dialog stays declared; it appears only on non-subscription accounts. This replaces the earlier entry "always Sonnet, uncharged, even on gateway", which was right for the Claude-routed mainline and wrong for GPT-routed sessions. Consequences: C11 shows the lane in Session info and notes on GPT models that auto-mode reviews run locally on Sonnet 5 and count against usage; C3 renders the `system` warning as a session notice.
- 2026-09-28 (C2): X1 corrected on evidence: server-lane allow verdicts are attributable through `safeguard_results`, so tool events carry a safeguard field. Rendering decision: shown in cell detail and the turn summary only, never as a per-cell badge, to keep auto-mode sessions quiet (D-18). Alternative rejected: an "auto-approved" marker on every cell, which would label nearly every action in the owner's mainline mode.
- 2026-09-28 (C2): Unrouted launches pass the empty-string gateway env override through flag settings, because unsetting the variables does not defeat `settings.json` routing. The `CLAUDE_*` allowlist excludes the parent-session variables and the dialog-timeout override. `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` becomes a per-session launch choice.
- 2026-09-28 (C2): The JSONL is treated as append-only with branch anchors; the filesystem service follows the leaf's parent chain. Pass 1's truncation assumption is withdrawn.
- 2026-09-28 (C2): C2's unrouted model turns were blocked by the owner's expired login. Superseded the same day by D-19: the direct path is a fallback, not a P0 acceptance path, so the residual is closed as not required rather than waiting on a re-login.
- 2026-09-28: D-19 recorded (gateway default). Consequences: §3 P0 gateway bullet rewritten; §4.1 launch environment carries the gateway variables and `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=true` by default after a relay health check; X6 lists them; C11 gains the accounts view and relay-health behaviour and loses the symmetric toggle; the "unrouted" vocabulary becomes "direct". Alternative rejected: MAWS running its own account sign-in in P0, because ClaudeUsage owns the credential and the Chrome-profile sign-in flow; folding the relay or its accounts into MAWS is a next-phase candidate. Owner decided.
- 2026-09-28: Model override pass-through: the owner's `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_*_MODEL` variables are passed to the engine when present, shown in Session info; alternative rejected: stripping them, which would make "opus" mean something different from the terminal.
- 2026-09-28: Wave-2 plan. The execution documents for C3, C4, C5, C6 and C9 are authored in parallel with C1's execution, against the C1 document's Interfaces section as the interim canonical X1/X2; authors write residue bullets into their own documents and the architect pastes them into the charter, so five parallel writers never edit one file. Execution of wave 2 starts when C1's code is on master; each child runs as a plan-executor subagent in its own git worktree branched from that point, not as a sminos seat, because the review and fix loop already lives in the architect session and no child needs to outlive it; a seat is reserved for a child that must survive the session or needs live owner attention. Merges back to master go in dependency order (C3, C4, then C5, C6, C9) with the per-namespace IPC registry keeping them mechanical.
- 2026-09-28 (C4, parent-impact reconciled): §4.4 amended. The index keeps projects, sessions, attention items, drafts and replay fixtures; queued messages and grants move to the engine host's per-session state file; layout to `layout.json`. Reason: the queue's releaser is the engine host, which has no path to main's database. `node:sqlite` replaces better-sqlite3 on evidence (no prebuild for Electron 44's ABI 149; `node:sqlite` is built in with FTS5); library picks are advisory, so this is a recorded overturn, not a contract change.
- 2026-09-28 (architect, wave-2 reconciliation): attention items are owned by main in X2 (`attention.list`, `attention.changed`, `attention.request`): the engine host's broker sends broker-kind items to main over the host protocol, main's attention service (built by C3) merges them with the non-broker kinds, persists them through an `AttentionStore` port the index implements, and emits the change events. Reason: §4.4 puts attention items in the index and §4.6 makes broker items the source of Needs-you; only main sees both. The event stream is one snapshot command `session.events` and one streaming event `session.eventBatch`; the app-session key is `appSessionId` everywhere; children dedupe events by id, never by sequence, because backfilled disk events carry no sequence.
- 2026-09-28 (architect): children may extend X1, X3 and X7 additively in their own files (new event fields, a new X3 category, namespaced token names such as C9's `--term-*`) and never rename an existing name; the wave-2 interface ledger records each addition. Reason: C9's terminal tokens and C3's gateway fields on the session record are legitimate needs the cut could not foresee, and a rename would break siblings coding against C1's names.
- 2026-09-28 (C9, advisory overturn pending the spike): the terminal renderer is expected to be xterm.js 6 with the WebGL addon rather than ghostty-web, on research evidence: ghostty-web's last stable release is from 2025-12, Korean IME composition is broken (the owner types Korean), OSC 8 hyperlinks do not work, synchronized output is unsupported, and its WebAssembly needs a CSP the renderer does not grant. The spike still runs both and records the first failure; the verdict is final only after it.
- 2026-09-28 (C1 execution, surprise): node-pty 1.1.0's tarball ships `prebuilds/darwin-arm64/spawn-helper` without the execute bit (upstream issue 850), so every spawn failed with `posix_spawnp failed` while `pty.node` loaded fine; fixed by a root `postinstall` chmod. The *node-pty native module* residue (prebuild, no rebuild) stands; its consequence list now includes restoring the bit at install and proving in the packaged smoke that `asarUnpack` preserves it.
- 2026-09-28 (architect): a machine disruption stopped five agents mid-task (C3 fix wave, C9 final edits, C4/C5/C6 executors); all were resumed from their on-disk state (worktrees, uncommitted edits, SDE ledgers) rather than restarted, and nothing committed was lost.
- 2026-09-28 (architect): wave-3 documents (C7, C10) are authored while wave 2 executes, against the reviewed wave-2 documents and the ledger as contracts; each gets a refresh pass against the landed code before its executor starts. C11 and C8's decomposing run follow once the first wave-2 children land, to bound machine load.
- 2026-09-28 (C1, landed): merged at 640f5d3 after 30 commits; 239 tests, dev and packaged smokes, unsigned `MAWS.app`. Residue for later children: C3 probes the eleven unfixtured `system` subtypes to tighten their required fields; acceptance clean-checkout steps must exclude `.doperpowers/`; the execute-bit fix lives in a root `postinstall` (an `--ignore-scripts` install ships a broken spawn helper, caught by the packaged smoke). Review rungs: per-task reviews ran on the plugin's task-reviewer definition (sol, xhigh), the whole-branch review on astra (reviewer-medium); for wave 2 the executors are told to run per-task reviews on an astra rung, per the owner's rule.
- 2026-09-28 (C1): C1's residue decisions landed in §12 C1 (package manager, Electron and toolchain pins, module format, directory layout, validation library, state wiring, test runners, utility-process build and start, node-pty prebuild, theme switching with tokens-only enforcement, window security defaults, packaging skeleton, `pnpm verify`). X1 gains turn origin, hook lifecycle and callbacks, a rewind boundary and the transcript-record chain; X1, X2 and X7 name their canonical source files, with C1's execution document as the interim source of the concrete definitions. Alternative rejected: carrying the residue inside the execution document, which the living-spec reference reserves for constraints, milestones and the record.
- 2026-09-28 (C1): Adversarial review of the C1 execution document, ten findings fixed in it and reflected here: wire validators dispatch by discriminator instead of one zod union; the sandboxed preload bundles its dependencies (electron-vite externalizes them by default, and a sandboxed `require` cannot load them); the development CSP uses a per-launch nonce for the fast-refresh preamble; X1 gains a distinct `ParentToolUseId` brand and a `session.reconnect` event; the permission-mode refinement holds in both directions; host start is app-scoped and idempotent with a snapshot command and a reload-survival gate; rewind-chain evidence moves to a synthetic complete transcript fixture, the pass-2 snapshots being filtered summaries with absent parents, and `currentChain` reports a gap rather than fabricating; process checks use pids and `app.getAppMetrics()` instead of `pgrep` on `serviceName`, which is a display name; Electron 44.4.5's Node is 24.21.0, not 24.18; the X2 registry is composed per namespace (new residue *IPC registry composition*). Each mechanism was verified against the pinned package sources or the Electron 44 typings (document Decision Log).
- 2026-09-28 (C3): C3's residue decisions landed in §12 C3 (transport framing with a 64 Mi line cap, host-minted request ids and four timeout classes, the `session.*`, `engine.*` and `attention.*` X2 surface, the bash echo convention, spill path, reconnect and exit payloads, the per-launch journal, the `SessionStateStore` port, the process table, plain-Node conformance and gated live tests, routed verification by `get_status`, the relay health check, file checkpointing through `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_SDK_FILE_CHECKPOINTING`, auto-mode lanes on the session record, plan outcomes on the wire, steer, exit policy, session id pinning, entrypoint and host hooks, reload survival). X1's `SessionRecord.gateway` gains `baseUrl` and `autoModeServer` additively and X6 sets the checkpointing variable; alternative rejected: stamping `origin.kind: 'bash_echo'` on the wire, which fails closed at the engine's human-trust gates.
- 2026-09-28 (C3): Adversarial review of the C3 execution document, ten findings fixed in it and reflected in §12 C3, plus two coordinator rulings: every send is a persisted queue entry with a `submitting` state so two quick sends serialise and a crash around delivery leaves an `uncertain` entry for the human, never a silent drop or duplicate; every event derives from a journal row (`meta` rows for exit, reconnect and errors; the rewind boundary from the correlated `rewind_conversation` rows), so a journal rebuild reproduces the live graph after the ring rolls over; a failed `initialize` runs the termination ladder and keeps the process reservation until the real exit; id-less requests (MCP elicitation, `pass2/dialogs` seq 41) retire at their turn's end, §4.6's confirmation rule applying to items with an id; `rewind_files` returns paths and counts only for a dry run and `skippedLinks` for a restore (`cli.pretty.js` `vS`), so the outcome converts explicitly; the bounded control deadlines are 8 s under X2's 10 s router timeout and five `long` commands are operations with a receipt and a `session.operation` completion; the grant-persistence live case uses the pass-2 `grant-reapply` scenario, the ask-rule downgrade asserted separately; the replay transport answers the host's own handshake from a checked-in prelude and fails on any other unrecorded write; a permission denial carries no `scope`; `--settings` merges `extraSettings` under the protected X6 keys and refuses a conflict. Rulings folded in: `assistant.block.complete.blockIndex` is the stream's `content_block_start` index paired per message id (one `assistant` frame per block, verified over both passes), and the human prompt bubble derives from the host's own outgoing `user` frame with the engine's replay deduplicated by uuid, `system/init` marking acceptance rather than the turn's start. The ledger records the payload changes (`session.launch` refusal reasons, `session.send`, `QueueEntry`, `session.answer`, `session.rewind`, `session.rewindFiles`, the five operation commands, the new `session.operation` event, `session.stop` and `engine.shutdown` bounds).
- 2026-09-28 (C4): C4's residue decisions landed in §12 C4 (the filesystem service in the engine host and the index and git service in main, `node:sqlite`, one recursive `fs.watch` plus tailers, offset tailing with uuid re-anchoring and deterministic disk event ids, the sidecar retry schedule, holder detection, the index schema under §4.4 as amended, project membership, the `fs.*`, record-side `session.*` and `git.*` X2 surface, git invocation and watcher, the replay clock, read-only acceptance against the real store). The heavy-work worker of §4.1 is deferred to its first CPU-bound consumer; alternatives rejected: the index in the engine host, and chokidar in place of `fs.watch`.

- 2026-09-28 (architect, C9 `[parent-impact]` fork, item 1/9 for codex): xterm.js 6.0.0 discards the rows that `CSI S` scrolls out of a scroll region even when the region starts at row 1 (`InputHandler.ts` 1429–1438, unchanged in 6.1.0-beta.304), so codex 0.155.1's inline viewport (`CSI 1;39r CSI 10S CSI r`) loses shell rows above it permanently, where tmux, iTerm2 and kitty put them in scrollback (tmux 3.7b control: same bytes, zero rows lost; openai/codex #27644 seen from the terminal side). This overturns C9 M1's findings rows 1 and 13, which blamed codex's redraw. Ruling: the terminal adapter registers a `CSI S` handler through xterm's public parser API that, on the normal buffer with the region's top at row 1, scrolls through the core's line-feed path (one exact-pinned internal call, guarded at construction with a logged fallback to xterm's behaviour), pinned by headless tests on the recorded codex bytes and the lone sequence; the adapter rule gains a dated exception naming the internal and the version. Rejected: a `pnpm patch` of the minified bundle (opaque to review and to the next bump) and accepting the gap (daily row loss in the owner's most-used tool). (2) `session.steer` gains optional `priority: 'now' | 'next'`, default `'now'`; C7's Steer keeps `'now'` and its label and caret menu say it interrupts the running turn; C8b's subagent relay sends `'next'` so forwarding never aborts the parent's in-flight work, the copy saying the parent forwards when its turn ends; C3 records `'next'` mid-turn as the `steer-next` fixture. - 2026-09-28 (architect, debt wave 1A landed): the engine-host half merged at a164694 (6e2986a; 1,575 tests, typecheck and lint green; reviewed on opus in two rounds). Two rulings inside it: a `refused` drop before `started` pauses the queue rather than releasing the next message, and only when messages are waiting (a refusal may describe the engine's state; a paused queue is one click to resume, a drained one a cascade of notices); `fs.readSpill` refuses any path that is not already normal (the engine reports spill paths clean and absolute), because the kernel resolves `/X/..` only when `/X` exists, so a `..` segment was an existence oracle for outside names with no symlink needed, a variant older and stronger than the row the wave set out to close. The C3 document's Decision Log carries both. The `starting` status now precedes a launch's first row, so every event of a launch follows a live status under C5's liveness rule.

- 2026-09-29 (C8c, in flight, K1 corrected): the K1 file-service refinement's sentence "`read` has no race window" was wrong: `realpath(path)` and `stat(real)` are separate path steps, so an ancestor swapped symlink → directory → symlink across the open, the realpath and the stat makes the stat name the outside object the descriptor holds, and a swapping loop wins by chance. Ruled: `files.*` opens `real0` with macOS's `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` (0x20000000, absent from Node's `fs.constants`, passes through `openSync`; verified on the owner's Mac: a realpath opens, a path through `/tmp` or an in-root link answers `ELOOP`), so the open itself is the containment check; step 1 adds `lstat(path)` so a final-component symlink is refused; step 5 stays as the second check; `list` and `watch` open the same way; the constant is hard-coded with a macOS-only comment and a test that fails loudly where the flag is ignored. Also ruled: `files.openExternal` requires a regular file with no execute bit whose extension is on an allowlist (`opensExternally(name)`, shared with the viewer so the button shows only where the service would open: pdf, html, image, audio and video types, docx/xlsx/pptx, OpenDocument, rtf, pages/numbers/key, epub, txt/log/csv/tsv; legacy `.doc`/`.xls`/`.ppt` excluded for macros, agent-written files carrying no quarantine attribute), answering `not opened outside MAWS: <path>` otherwise, because "Open externally" on a Binary row would have launched an agent-written executable, `.command` or `.app`, and a denylist is incomplete by construction (Python Launcher runs `.py` on open). Recorded by C8c at 64c6eef; the C8 spec's K1 text is amended at C8c's closure.

- 2026-09-29 (C8b, G1 probes, relay regime amended): on the pinned 2.1.283 a relay's receipt never reaches the wire: the SendMessage lands in the child as a `queued_command` attachment (origin `coordinator`) in its disk transcript only, which C4's backfill drops, so `delivered` is unobservable live. Ruled: `forwarded` is the relay's resting state on this engine, its line reading "… gets it at its next tool round"; `matchesReceipt` stays coded and pinned on a synthetic frame, and accepts the disk-form attachment too, so that when C4 surfaces `queued_command` attachments in `fs.agentEvents` (an additive ask, tracker row for debt wave 3) `delivered` keys on that observation with no C8b change; the derived fixture drops the receipt batch and the e2e asserts `forwarded`; acceptance item 4(vi) records `inbox.txt` and the disk attachment as the evidence of delivery. Inferring `delivered` from the child's next tool round was rejected as the inference §5.6's failure semantics forbids. Refined by C8b's G1 review (7a07430): "no receipt on the wire" holds for a live child only; on the agent-stopped route (a background child that ended before the parent forwarded) the parent's SendMessage resumes the agent and the resume's `task_started` carries the relayed text as `prompt` live (`relay-to-child.jsonl` seq 548, C3's `subagent.started.prompt`), an observation, so `matchesReceipt` matches that start and such a relay goes `forwarded` → `delivered`; the terminal-node dismissal applies only when the node turns terminal after the relay reached `forwarded`. Further (C8b M2 review, 6b20ef6): with `forwarded` the resting state, only an `asked` relay blocks the relay composer (a second steer racing an unforwarded one); a send over a `forwarded` relay replaces it, so a child is not limited to one relay per run. Other G1 facts: the first wording routes SendMessage to the child id in all three parent states (idle starts a turn, two parent rounds because the parent first ToolSearches SendMessage; busy folds in at the tool boundary; foreground only after the child returns and resumes, a second `subagent.started` for the same id, so the disabled reason stands); R5 nests live through the dispatching Agent `tool.use`'s `agentId` (`subagent.started.parentToolUseId` is null); R6's frames all precede the `stop_task` response (3 to 9 ms), an unknown id succeeds with no frames; shells are listed only while `isBackground`; `tool.progress` is now produced, so C3's pass-3 list pin and three C6 presentation snapshots were amended. G1 spend US$1.80.

- 2026-09-29 (architect, C12 document review; reviewed on opus, gateway rate limited): the adversarial review of `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-09-29-c12-finish.md` (e3720701) found five P1s and twelve P2s, all accepted and fixed in the document by a fix wave; the rulings that go beyond the document. (1) Wave 4 keeps its rows: the title defect (with D10's triage first and the binding title rule, that a session's `none`-source title is the conversation's first human prompt, that host-originated user frames never title, and that `custom-title` and `ai-title` keep their precedence), the composer's two stale "Arrives with" reasons and the Workspace gear, and the `highlight.js` override check return to wave 4, which the C8-recomposition line already gave them; the C12 document's sweep keeps the Worktrees… dialog, the launch-environment probe with its X6 proposal and the split-pane placeholder (the exited-session ring cap stays with the host-policy milestone that applies it). Wave 4 lands on master before the C12 executor is dispatched, not only before the pre-run, which also removes the overlap in `schema.ts`, `bindings.ts` and the composer. (2) The process-budget milestone is a composite in disguise and is split in three (host policy with the exited cap and the X1, X2 and X6 shapes; Send-to-resume with the digest and the surfaces; the live run), so C12 has six milestones. (3) Release safety: a candidate is engine-idle, not host-busy (X6's status gains `busy`, from the queue's busy predicate, a pending `!` command and a held clear-and-implement, none of which the status carried), and quiet for one sample period since its last event; evaluate and stop run synchronously; an explicit stop, archive or quit overrides a budget ladder in flight; the sum excludes trees already exiting; the host computes the debounced no-candidate flag the banner reads. (4) The released mark survives both writers that would overwrite it (`applyStatusToDigest` maps an exit with reason `budget` to phase `idle` and keeps `released`; the read model's `setDigest` keeps the mark; C4's holder-change seed does not clear it); it clears on the next `starting` and on archive, so an unarchived session resumes as a fresh launch. (5) Custody: main refuses a launch itself when custody is not `ok`, with custody's message, before `session.launch` (C3's probe knows only `missing` and `version`, so a hash mismatch would otherwise launch); the fix a refusal names is reinstalling that version or choosing a binary path, since re-pinning is after P0 (D6); configure awaits custody before its first push, or the host falls back to the installer path; under `MAWS_E2E` and replay custody is bypassed and the host gets `ENGINE_BIN` as today, except one custody e2e that opts in against a scratch source directory. (6) Every live block runs under a scratch config home with the owner-file check before and after; the grant check reruns the exact granted command (C10 item 9: a grant is the exact command). (7) The quit question always holds the quit, computes the report with the pty probes in parallel, then disarms and quits; "with the report all zero the quit proceeds untouched" is not achievable, since `before-quit` must prevent synchronously. (8) The launch-environment probe runs the pinned binary by absolute path with the owner's default model on both halves and deletes the variable for the unset arm; the owner's script adds one observation in the real terminal (whether a plain Agent call runs in the foreground; whether TaskCreate is present). (9) The Worktrees… dialog binds sessions by C10's occupancy ids, not path equality; a checkout claimed by an in-flight setup, an open unstarted tab or a retained intent is busy. (10) A listed stateful server blocks from `pending`; ptc's own documentation says its namespace persists across `--resume`, so it is probably not stateful, and the owner decides the list in a script step before the sitting. (11) `build:owner` is dropped for `build:app` with the identity variable; the pre-run runs against the owner build through `e2e:packaged` where the harness supports it, and records the difference where it does not. (12) M2 needs C11 on master (it edits C11's `engine-configure.ts`, `EngineSection` and the re-pin confirm); the earlier "M2 may be taken first" is withdrawn.

The P1s: the rule keeping the released mark would also have kept it against C4's `foreign` seed, so a released session the owner resumed from a terminal would have read idle and offered Send-to-resume; the mark is now kept only against an incoming `exited` phase, and archive drops it and sets `exited`. Also ruled: the launch gate awaits custody and the first push, and runs when a setup or resume begins, before `fs.unfollow` or the record write; the real-store pre-run case keeps `MAWS_E2E=1`, since the packaged unsigned build otherwise reaches the login keychain; a budget release sends `end_session { reason: 'stop' }` on the wire, since the fixtures carry only `stop` and `quit` and D-1 admits no unprobed value, so `budget` is MAWS's exit reason on X1 and X6 only. Rulings at landing: (1) §10 item 9 restated, since on 2.1.283 both ⌘. and quit cancel a pending approval through `control_cancel_request` and only a crash loses one; (2) an archive keeps `end_session { reason: 'archive' }` on the wire (wave 4 recorded the frame) and only `budget` maps to `stop`; (3) the exited cap exempts a row holding a queue rather than adding disk-backed queue mutations, since what the list shows from disk must stay editable; (4) the intermittent resume stall at `initialize` (twice in four row Resumes of one session in the pre-run: 120 s with no frame back, not even the SessionStart hook frames, while the same spawn from a node script answered in 433 to 576 ms five of five; controls in `docs/doperpowers/evidence/c12/initialize-stall.txt`) is C3's handshake, and a corrective runs now, before the owner's sitting: a spike to reproduce and find the cause (the parent process, the captured login-shell values and the timing after the previous exit are the uncontrolled differences; the host's own reader on the reconnect path is a hypothesis, since an engine that answered and waits on stdin also sits idle with its connections open), then a fix with a bounded initialize timeout that surfaces the engine's stderr tail and leaves the row resumable; (5) `claude --bg` runs its job on the installed 2.1.285 daemon through `~/.local/bin/claude`, not the pin, even under a scratch home: a D-1 exposure for bg-held sessions, a tracker row for C3's launch contract; (6) the Monitor tool sits behind 2.1.283's `tengu_amber_sentinel` gate, off by default, so C8's monitor sitting item may be unobservable and records as such. Residue to the tracker, nine rows (three closed by the run: C3's exited ring 0bcd0b69, C7's exited Send e7a58772, C10's no-entry-point 17b539b9): the stall (C3, before the sitting), the cold first Overview paint of 57.9 s over 3,274 transcripts (C4), `claude --bg` off the pin (C3), a capped row's released id surviving archive (C12, a contract change), `git.worktree.status` null for a separate-git-dir main checkout (C10), C7's keyboard e2e cases failing in chained and group runs and passing directly (C7), the interrupt's failed glyph (C5·C6), `store.test.ts`'s expected warning noise (C12), the busy-quit e2e hang now bounded at 20 s (C12); the `highlight.js` row re-filed under C8c. The residue decisions are pasted into §12 C12; X1, X2 and X6 lose "pending its landing". The load rule was breached four times during the run, each recorded in the document (one e2e:c7 start at 47.7 not counted). §20 stays pending on the owner's sitting, which follows the C3 corrective; the owner's pre-steps B1 to B5 in `docs/doperpowers/evidence/c12/owner-script.txt` can run meanwhile.

- 2026-09-30 (C3 corrective landed; the sitting unblocked): the resume stall's corrective merged at a2b88bde (d8c1ef83 over b25a41f2; seven commits; ledger `docs/doperpowers/ledgers/2026-09-30-c3-handshake-stall.md`). The cause was not found but located: both stalled processes died by `SIGTERM`'s signal rather than exit 143, and 2.1.283 installs its own `SIGTERM` handler 75 to 100 ms after spawn (`spikes/protocol-probe/scenarios/pass3/sigterm-disposition.mjs`), so each stalled process was still in early boot two minutes in, before any transcript read or hook. Hypotheses: the host's reader and writer, negative (a new session object per launch, listeners attached inside the spawn call, the `out` row written after the OS write); stale state after `SIGTERM`, negative in 21 probe resumes; the environment, negative (MAWS's real engine environment differs from the probe's by two `node_modules/.bin` PATH entries); the product itself, 0 silent stalls in 74 row Resumes on the dev and the unsigned packaged builds at loads 6 to 12. Two candidates remain, both untested: the pre-run's stalls happened at a one-minute load above 60 under memory pressure, and the security context of the engine's Keychain lookup; `e2e/c3-stall.spec.ts` captures the next occurrence (`security` children, `sample`). Fixes: `LaunchWatch` counts only the live launch's events (15 of 29 resumes of a transcript whose last turn never ended had been killed 2 ms after spawn by C4's synthesised exit; 0 of 45 after); the 30 s first-output bound with `no_output` (the brief's flat 30 s class for `initialize` was rightly replaced, since SessionStart hooks run before the engine reads it); one visible automatic relaunch after `no_output`, ruled on the worker's recommendation and placed in main's resume path because the host's session is one process by construction; the retry's watch ignoring a late status from the launch it replaced. Gates at 483c3a18: typecheck, lint, 4,585 unit tests, e2e 28 with the two retry cases, e2e:c12 4; the merged tree re-gated (typecheck, lint, 4,585); `doperpowers:reviewer-high` two rounds on the first range (three P2s fixed) and one on the retry commit (correct). Spend about US$2.87 on haiku; the owner's files untouched. Contract: X6 gains `LaunchSpec.retry` (additive); §12 C3's timeout paragraph amended. Tracker: the stall row narrowed to its unexplained cause with the capture harness; two rows added (C12: closing the app leaves a ready engine alive as an orphan for 1 to 3 s; C3: SessionStart hooks count against `initialize`'s 120 s, so a slow hook can fail a legitimate resume). The owner's script notes at steps 4 and 9 updated. The sitting is unblocked.

- 2026-10-01 (W7 executor): interfaces that changed with W7's sessions and custody. X2: `SessionRecord.holder`'s `self` gains optional `contestedBy` (a foreign holder sharing a session MAWS runs; the shared `ForeignHolderSchema`); `fs.holders` and `fs.holderChanged` name foreign holders only (a live registry record whose entrypoint is `sdk-ts` and whose pid is in C3's process table is MAWS's own and never a holder, pass3/w7-collision); a new event `fs.foreignWrite { sessionId, appSessionId, holder }`, once per contest, at the foreign process's first record, on which main yields MAWS's process (a notice, the queue paused, a stop once idle); C3's `session.events` reads the transcript for an exited process whose session a foreign process held at or after its exit; `session.stop` gains `ifIdle` (stop only while the process could end without losing work, decided in the host; otherwise the answer carries `skipped: true`), which the yield uses; a new main command `session.takeOver { appSessionId, holder }` answering `released` or `refused { reason, message }`, after which the renderer resumes with `session.start` (pass3/w7-take-over), signalling only a terminal's interactive holder (`fs.holders` rows carry the record's optional `entrypoint`; another app's engine is refused `other_app`); settings version 7 adds `engine.memoryBudget.enabled` (on by default, migrated from 6), and `engine.policy.set` and the budget state gain `enabled`. C4: the index gives an exited record the remembered foreign holder instead of none; the chain follower takes `pinned` and `refold()` (notices keyed `transcript_branch_held` and `transcript_refold`). C5: the glyph table gains the contested row (⇄, warning). C6: the session source resets once when its record turns foreign-live after the live form. C10: the foreign-live notice offers Take over here. C11: Session info's Engine section gains Held by. Probe findings in `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass2.md` ("W7 probes").

Reproduction (about US$0.10 a run, Haiku through the relay, scratch config; fixtures `pass3/w7-collision`, `w7-collision-disk/`, `w7-take-over`): MAWS's `-p` process registers as `interactive` with entrypoint `sdk-ts`, a terminal as `cli`, and the engine refuses neither; the transcript forks once from the shared parent; before W7 MAWS's own record read as a terminal holder, the disk view flipped at every sibling write (the owner's vanishing text), nothing re-reported the terminal after MAWS's exit, the pane kept the dead stream and Send was refused `held`, and a later resume continued the terminal's branch, so MAWS's turn left the model's context; nothing crashed. Take-over, live: SIGTERM to a terminal-style holder the run started exits it with 143, the registry clears in about half a second, and the resume continues. Gates on the branch: typecheck, lint, 4,825 unit tests, 107 fixtures, `e2e:c12` 4, `e2e:settings` 6, `e2e:panel` 23, `e2e:transcript` 11; the architect re-ran typecheck, lint, 4,825 unit tests, the fixtures and `e2e:c12` and `e2e:panel` on master, all green. Review: a high-rung round (two P1, take-over could signal a pid after the holder was gone and the yield's stop could land after a new turn started; four P2, the contest baseline could swallow the first foreign write, the pane fell back to the old history once the terminal exited, the terminal-rewind check ran before the branch pin, a holder change during the pane's initial load was missed; all six fixed by a separate worker with regression tests) and a clean second round; the executor also found that a paused empty queue would silently hold the next Send and that take-over would have signalled another app's engine, both fixed. Not verified live: the yield's stop itself (its trigger is), `claude stop` for a `--bg` job (unit-tested; a live job starts a daemon), and the installed 2.1.287. One environmental note for the owner: during the first probe run the terminal-style engine's auto-updater re-created the shared `~/.local/bin/claude` link; its target stayed 2.1.287, and later runs set `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`. - 2026-10-01 (owner's ruling, W6's route half dispatched): the owner chose a worker on a branch of their ClaudeUsage repo for their review (`maws-control-route` off 1abd89a, in a separate worktree; nothing merges or restarts the running app). The contract both sides build to, binding: a loopback control API on the relay, answered before the verbatim forward and never forwarded, guarded by `Authorization: Bearer <token>` with the token in `<ClaudeUsage data dir>/control-token` (0600, written at launch); `GET /claudeusage/state` (version 1: `routing { enabled, ready, port, drainTarget, drainTargetIsManual }`, `accounts [{ uuid, email, status, isRoutingHere }]`, `codex [{ id, label, isDrainTarget }]`, additive fields allowed); `POST /claudeusage/route { uuid }`, `POST /claudeusage/accounts/add { email }` (202, opens the sheet), `POST /claudeusage/accounts/reconnect { uuid }` (202), `POST /claudeusage/accounts/remove { uuid, confirm: true }`, `POST /claudeusage/panel`; each action answers 200 `{ ok: true }` or 409 `{ ok: false, reason, message }` in the panel's own words; plus `applicationShouldHandleReopen` toggling the panel. Architect's simplification of E1's direction: MAWS keeps reading ClaudeUsage's files for limits and activity (already live) and uses the API only for readiness and the actions, so the state answer stays small and the two never disagree about what the panel shows. MAWS's side (X2 additive, owner main: `settings.usageControls`, `settings.usageAction`) lands behind `usage/controls.ts` and degrades to today's read-only popover whenever the route is absent, so it can merge before the owner merges their branch.
- 2026-10-01 (architect, ClaudeUsage branch ready for the owner's review): `maws-control-route` (five commits off 1abd89a, in the worktree `/Users/new/Developer/GitHub/claude-usage-menubar-maws-route`; the owner's checkout, the installed app, its data directory and the running relay untouched; not pushed, no pull request). As built: the token file is created once (64 hex, 0600, never replaced) and checked in constant time before the path; `/claudeusage/*` is answered before any forward (404 unknown, 405 wrong method, 400 bad body, 503 before the app model is wired); `GET /state` adds per-account `statusMessage`, `isActive`, `canRoute`, `needsReconnect`, `canReconnect`, `canRemove`; the actions answer the panel's own refusal words (`busy`, `unavailable`, `sign_in_failed`, `not_needed`, `removal_failed` beside the contract's); add and reconnect also bring the panel up, since the sheet lives there; the reopen handler toggles the panel after activating the app. Verified by 29 tests over real HTTP in a temporary data directory, `swift build` clean; two pre-existing `HistoryStoreTests` fail on master too because `HistoryStore.record` uses the real clock against a fixed 2026-08-29 date now outside the 14-day retention, the owner's to fix. Not checked without launching the app: the popover appearing and `open -b` reaching the reopen handler.

- 2026-10-01 (owner's question, Claude mods on MAWS): "explore whether we can use Claude mods for MAWS too, to let users' functional hook mods for desktop be applied on the MAWS GUI workspace". Study r6 (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/ux-2026-10-01/r6-claude-mods.txt`): a mod is a plugin with a function-hooks module run in the engine's sandboxed worker; "Claude Mods" names nothing in 2.1.283 or 2.1.287; Claude Desktop 2.16120.0 is a display client of the engine's `ui_surface_v1` remote-surface protocol (engine 2.1.285 onward, function hooks on by default from 2.1.286), attaching as surface "desktop" and rendering plain-data element trees. Engine-side hook points already run in MAWS sessions (the user's settings are passed through); the UI pushes (`ui_toast`, `ui_status`, `ui_log`, `ui_panes`, `ui_invalidate`) are dropped as unknown frames today. Plan: R0 probe (spike, dispatched), then R1 decode of toasts, status and log lines (small), R2 attach as a desktop surface with panes, the band, the footer slot, interaction and prompt access, R3 transcript sites; R2 and R3 are an extension surface and wait for the owner's ruling on D-9. Prerequisite for all: an engine ≥ 2.1.285 at runtime, which W13 brings. Neither patches the engine nor writes `~/.claude`.
- 2026-10-01 (R0 executor): the installed 2.1.287 probed with MAWS's launch under scratch config homes (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass3-agent-work.md`, R0 section; fixtures `pass3/mods-*`; US$0.10). (1) With the switch on, both the initialize answer and `system/init` carry `ui_surface_v1` in `capabilities`. (2) With no `ui_attach`, a mod's `$.ui.log`, `$.ui.status` and `$.ui.toast` reach stdout as `system` frames `ui_log`, `ui_status` and `ui_toast` (`{plugin, text, uuid, session_id}`, the toast with `timeout_ms`), `session.start`'s even before the initialize answer, and the engine itself drops a plugin's toast within 2 s of its last. (3) `ui_attach {surface:"desktop", client_id, viewport, answers}` answers `{surfaces:["desktop"]}` and `ui_render` of the `AbovePrompt` band (Desktop's props, `instance_id:"above-prompt"`) answers `{tree, props, rewritten, hooked:true}` with the mod's plain `Text` tree (an unhooked site answers `{tree:{type:"engine",ref:0}, hooked:false}`), and no `ui_invalidate` or `ui_panes` push comes without a plugin load or a pane. (4) 2.1.287 no longer reads `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_FUNCTION_HOOKS` (2.1.286 still does), so unset and `0` leave the switch on under a cold rollout cache while the owner's cached served value (`tengu_plugin_hooks_modules: false` in `~/.claude.json`) turns it off despite `"1"`, with no `ui_surface_v1`, no installed plugin's hooks module and `ui_*` requests refused: the owner's mods are off in every 2.1.287 session until the rollout serves the flag on, which r6 §4.1 did not foresee. (5) The dev-mods consent reaches a stdio host as an ordinary `can_use_tool` for `AskUserQuestion` (`tool_use_id: "confirm_<uuid>"`, header "Mods", `metadata.source: "mod_hot_reload"`, `requires_user_interaction: true`) between turns, after `session_state_changed requires_action`, and answering "Enable for this session" loads the mod at once. Merging these fixtures turns `src/engine-host/conformance/fixtures.test.ts` red (nine tests: the pass-3 list, `system/ui_*` as unknown frames, one refused `ui_render`, two censuses) until R1 maps the frames or the merge adds the entries.

Answers on the installed 2.1.287 under a scratch config dir (US$0.10): (1) with the switch on, `system/init` and the initialize answer carry `ui_surface_v1`; (2) a mod's toast, status and log arrive without any attach as `system` frames `ui_toast`/`ui_status`/`ui_log` shaped `{plugin, text, uuid, session_id}` (the toast adds `timeout_ms: 4000`), the `session.start` pushes before the initialize answer, a second toast from the same plugin within 2 s dropped by the engine; (3) `ui_attach {surface:"desktop", client_id, viewport, answers}` answers `{surfaces:["desktop"]}`, `ui_render` of the AbovePrompt band answers the mod's tree `{tree:{type:"Text",…}, props, rewritten:false, hooked:true}` and an unhooked site `{tree:{type:"engine",ref:0}, hooked:false}`; (4) 2.1.287 no longer reads `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_FUNCTION_HOOKS`: Anthropic's served rollout value decides, and on the owner's machine it is cached off (`~/.claude.json`), so the owner's mods load on 2.1.286 and not on 2.1.287 until the rollout serves on, in the terminal as in MAWS; MAWS cannot change this without patching the engine; (5) the dev-mods consent reaches a stdio host as an ordinary `can_use_tool` for `AskUserQuestion` (`tool_use_id: confirm_<uuid>`, header "Mods", `metadata.source: "mod_hot_reload"`, `requires_user_interaction: true`) between turns after a `requires_action` state, and "Enable for this session" loads the mod at once. - 2026-10-02 (F10 executor): one writer process per profile: acquire Electron's single-instance lock after setting `MAWS_USER_DATA` and before opening stores or hosts; a second instance of that profile quits without opening them, while different userData directories remain independent. Keep the lock for the app lifetime; settings patches synchronously re-read, migrate and merge the latest disk state before atomic replacement, refusing unreadable or invalid files rather than writing defaults. A failed migration rewrite retains the migrated settings and is retried by the next patch.
- F6 executor — 2026-10-02: A consumed `next` steer remains editable and rewindable; the transcript resolves its UUID through the message-owner index to the turn that absorbed it and hides that whole presentation turn (including its earlier work), retaining it under that rewind’s disclosure rather than silently ignoring the accepted rewind. This is a turn-granular presentation rule, not an engine truncation claim: §4.2 and `fixtures/pass3/steer-next.jsonl` seq 70–71 establish the fold, while `fixtures/pass2/rewind.jsonl` seq 41–43 establish host-owned truncation after a successful rewind; no new engine behaviour is assumed.

Rulings: MAWS gives a mod those row-level outcomes and keeps its own structure (turns, the work area's groups and fold, answer-first); the X3 sites are `user` → UserMessage, `assistant_text` → AssistantMessage, `cell` → ToolUse (and ToolResult, standalone and resolved), `group` → ToolGroup, `local_command` → CommandOutput, the question card and `question` row → AskUserQuestion; asks only for mounted rows; `onScreen` absent; Client surface modules stay deferred, the Client element drawing MAWS's own. D-9's R3 slice is dispatched (waves plan §2 K2, §3). The owner confirmed the fold ruling the same day: Desktop parity at row level, MAWS keeps its own groups and folds. Protocol limit recorded the same day: a render answer carries one `props` (the engine node it finds in the tree); MAWS, like Desktop, draws its own row into the first `engine` node in document order and nothing into later ones; no MAWS-side extension of the protocol.

- 2026-10-02 (R3 executor): the seven transcript sites use R2's attached desktop surface and validated renderer; MAWS's turns, answer-first placement, work fold and group count lines stay intact (commentary bodies change inside their fold). Residue: the terminal passes an assistant message UUID unchanged (2.1.287 chunk-5s6b4r7q, af → Qs); MAWS keeps it for the first text block and uses `<uuid>:text:<blockIndex>` for later blocks of that UUID to avoid concurrent-instance collisions (a streaming block without a UUID temporarily uses its stable X3 row id). UserMessage is asked only for a nonempty, nonsynthetic own prompt, never queued or dropped; X1 `human` maps to `sdk`, known `composer`/`bridge`/`sdk` survive, unstamped history is `unclassified`. Concealed native content stays mounted for state and measurement, inert and inaccessible; only a newly mounted already-known-hooked row withholds until its first answer, with an ellipsis after 5 s. ToolGroup uses the engine's flat calls, newest fitting calls retained in source order under Desktop's depth/node/character budget, with output omitted first; merged read members become individually drawable ToolUse sites without changing their group's count. AskUserQuestion's request is reconstructed from X1 Question with absent optional fields omitted (metadataSource is unavailable on a live request and omitted; receipt rows recover it from tool input when present); otherwise-valid text/number/helper-description rewrites that MAWS's choice card cannot faithfully represent are refused, never approximated; valid draft choices and free text survive presentation-only rewrites. The installed engine sends props for only the first engine node in a tree, so MAWS, like Desktop, renders native content there and leaves later engine slots empty: the owner's multi-span to-human drawing has the same protocol limit, not a MAWS-only workaround (fixtures and provenance in findings-pass3-agent-work.md). Pane closeOnEscape and holdToasts ride this wave; Client modules remain deferred, Client elements use native drawing; promptEdit remains deferred because X2 discards its rewritten box and the rich editor lacks the required caret/edit reconciliation. No engine or owner configuration was changed.
